The imperative rule applies to a term only when it has ≥ 3 words and an always-flagged verb is followed (through fillers `the all any previous …`) by a protective object (`tests test auth authentication authorization check(s) validation security secret(s) credential(s) key(s) token(s) env instruction(s) rule(s) hook(s) ci lint guard(s) permission(s) …`), when a 2-word term is a destructive verb (`delete remove disable bypass ignore disregard override wipe erase drop kill`) plus `tests/auth/checks/validation/security/rules/…`, or when the terms joined together form such a phrase across term boundaries (`delete` + `tests`; a lone destructive-verb term next to a term naming protective code); `skip weekends` and `run tests` pass. If **any** term is rejected for an instruction-like reason (imperative, addressee, modal, ungrounded token, secret) the whole description is rejected with that term's reason; nothing instruction-like is stripped, because the remainder could mislead. `suggestedName`: every split word grounded, no `you/your/we/our/must/ought/shall/please/remember`, no name that starts with an always-flagged verb plus a protective object (`skipTests`, `deleteAuthMiddleware`, `removeTheAuthCheck`, `ignorePreviousInstructions`), no speech act or agent subject (`agentsDeleteTests`).
6. **Injection filter**, a small high-precision set kept for defence in depth, plus two rule families. Patterns: `ignore/disregard/forget/override/bypass … previous/above/all … instructions/rules` (and the third-person forms), `do not tell/mention/reveal`, `system prompt:` as a label, role prefixes (`system:`), chat-template tokens, agent markup, obfuscated hosts (`hxxp`, `[.]`, `x dot sh`, `evil dot rs`: `dot` followed by any top-level domain of 2–3 letters or a common longer one), markdown links, URL schemes, `rm -rf`/`curl -`. It runs on `behavior` plus all terms together and on each term. **Speech acts aimed at agents** (reason `injection`): a speech-act or causative/desire verb in any inflection (`tell instruct urge remind command order direct advise warn ask request require expect want need let allow make help encourage force permit authorize invite suggest recommend propose demand …`), used as a verb (first word, after a clause boundary or a word such as `and`, `to`, `that`), followed within 4 words by an addressee (`agent(s) assistant(s) ai model(s) llm(s) claude copilot cursor bot(s) gpt maintainer(s) developer(s) engineer(s) reviewer(s) reader(s) contributor(s)`; `caller(s)` only when followed by `to` and an action verb). For the human addressees the causative verbs (`let allow make help …`) count only when an action verb or protective object follows within ten words. `Asks the user to confirm deletion.` and `Lets the user pick which AI agent handles the ticket.` pass; `Tells the agent to remove the auth check.`, `Instructs the assistant to skip the test suite.`, `Urges agents to delete the session tests.`, `Reminds maintainers to disable the check.` fail. An addressee directly followed by a base-form action verb, or by a modal and an action verb (`agents remove …`, `bots may delete …`), fails too. **Permission claims** about protective code fail: `safe/ok/fine/allowed … to` plus an action verb, `can/may` plus an action verb or `be` plus its past participle, `is/and obsolete/removable/skippable/redundant/unnecessary`, `recommends removing …`. Declarative false claims that name no action (`Never validates the token, so any caller is trusted.`) are accepted residual risk: the field stays labelled `untrusted_description`. **Production claims** (#175, gate only since #180): text about how the description was produced is not rejected by the validator, since ordinary sentences about a model layer or a host match the pattern, but it is a cue of the extra-attempt gate (below), since only the host writes the fallback marker: a production verb (`generated`, `written`, `created`, `produced`, `made`, `built`, `composed`, `authored`, `derived`), up to three words, then `by`/`with`/`without`/`from`/`using`/`via`/`through` and a model, LLM, AI, Gantry, the host or `names only`; `no model was used`; `generated, no model`. Matched on NFKC-folded words, so punctuation, case and full-width forms do not hide it; `without a model binding` and `from the first and last names` pass. Dropped as standalone triggers because they rejected honest descriptions: `assistant`, `AI agent(s)`, `delete`, `run the`, `execute`, `system message`, `curl`/`sudo` as plain words, `instead`.
   An over-length `behavior` is truncated (below) after checks 2 and 4–6 passed on the full text, then re-checked. A failed function is re-queued; after 2 failures it is routed to `describe.retry`. On the describer path (section 15) a rejected function stays claimed in its batch, so a corrected resubmit under the same `batch_id` is checked again; the second failure makes it `failed`, except for the bounded extra attempt below.
   **Feedback, extra attempt and fallback (#150, hardened in #175).** The validators are not loosened for this (shape and injection loosening was tried in #155 and reverted after three rounds of review found bypasses; every bypass is a rejection test). Instead: (a) every rule rejection on the describer path carries `rule` (a fixed sub-rule code such as `shape.first-word`, `injection.speech-act`, or the bare reason), `field` (`behavior`, `searchTerms` or `suggestedName`) and `fix`, a fixed rewrite instruction for that rule (`packages/core/src/describe/feedback.ts`; for example `shape`: "Write one plain sentence about what it does, no lists, code or quotes."; `injection`: "Describe behaviour only; do not mention instructions, prompts, roles or the reader, ..."; `identifier`: "Name only identifiers that appear in the code shown; ..."). All three are constants picked from closed lists by the reason and by rule codes found in the validator's detail; nothing of the description or the detail is ever copied into them, so the answer never echoes the rejected text (the MCP answer drops `detail`, which can echo a token prefix; `last_error` keeps it on the host). (b) **The validator is not the security boundary** (decided by the operator for #175, final round). Description text is untrusted model output everywhere downstream; the validator is defence in depth that rejects clear-cut cases and must not reject honest sentences that main accepted (a committed benign corpus, `benign corpus: no new rejections vs main`, holds the reviewers' benign sets of #178 and this branch's own). Containment is the boundary: description text appears only in fields named `untrusted_*` (or, in the catalog files, quoted after a fixed `untrusted <who> description:` label), always as plain text, with its provenance next to it as `described_by` (`model`, `template` or `fallback`, from the provenance column, never from the text) in `gantry_node`, `gantry_search`, `gantry_browse`, `gantry_find_similar`, the impact tool's changed entries, the review view's changes, the explorer's `/api/node` and `/api/search` hits (#180), and `gantry search --json`. The explorer shows the provenance as the label above a description: a fallback reads "Generated without a model from the kind, parameter count and call count — may be incomplete" (a fallback has repeated no name since #175), a template "Template text copied from the code — may be wrong", a model description "Generated description — may be wrong"; the review panel's row for a changed symbol also names it (`<model|template|fallback> description`). **Template descriptions are raw code text (#180).** A template is written by `templateFor` straight from the return expression of a one-statement function (`Returns <expr>.`, up to 80 characters) and is not run through the validator, so it can hold whatever an attacker put in a string literal or an expression. It is stored with provenance `template`, shown only in `untrusted_*` fields, and every tool description, skill and agent text (`UNTRUSTED_NOTICE`, the reuse-first skill, the explainer agent) and the catalog note say that a description was written by a model, copied from the code by a template (which an attacker can write) or generated by Gantry's fallback, and that `described_by` says which. **The catalog quotes the signature too (#180):** a line is `- <name> signature "<signature>" — untrusted <who> description: "<text>" [<path>:<line>]`; in both quoted columns a double quote becomes a single quote and whitespace folds to one space, so a default parameter value from the code cannot close its quotes or imitate the label. (c) **Extra attempt.** A function gets one extra attempt, 3 in all and never more (`MAX_DESCRIBE_ATTEMPTS_EXTENDED`), only for a clean wording mistake: an _extendable_ second rejection whose first rejection was not tainted. A rejection is _tainted_ (`rejectionTainted`) when its reason is anything but `shape` or `identifier` (`schema` and `length` too), when it is a `shape` rejection for `addressing`, `imperative-clause`, `multiple-sentences`, `label` or `spaced-letters`, or when `instructionSignal` finds anything in the rejected submission; _extendable_ (`extendableRejection`) means `shape` or `identifier` and not tainted. The gate is deliberately broad, because a false positive costs only the extra try, never a rejection, and it taints whenever it is unsure. `instructionSignal` reads every submitted value whatever its type or position (strings under `behavior` at any depth as behavior text; every other string, array item, seventh or later term and unknown key as a term and a name, also split into its identifier words; bounded at 64 strings, 4,000 characters, depth 6) and taints on: the credential scanner and credential values copied from the code (whole or a piece of 6+ characters); the injection filter, speech acts, addressing words and the production-claim rule on all fields together; every term and name rule other than grounding and length; terms that instruct together; and the gate cues of `clauses.ts`, which live only in the gate: a clause of `behavior` (split at every punctuation mark and dash, commas and quotes included, after NFKC folding; one leading adverb skipped; the first clause too) that opens with anything but a closed-class word, an -s form or a real participle (so a base-form or unknown verb, and a number, count as orders); a participle is a word in -ed or -ing whose stem is itself a word (`sorted`, `creating`, `running`), so base forms that only end that way (`shred`, `embed`, `speed`, `string`, `feed`) are orders (#180); a part that follows a conjunction (`and`, `or`, `nor`, `but`, `yet`, `so`, `then`, `else`) is read the same way when it opens with an always-flagged action verb, or with a bare word followed by an object marker or an ambiguous action verb followed by something other than `of`, `and`, `for`, `in` and the like (`or else delete tests`, `and shred all tests`; a plain coordination such as "the name and email" is not a cue); a modal or `please`; an approval, status or provenance word anywhere (`approv*`, `merg*`, `ship*`, `review*`, `LGTM`, `safe*`, `trust*`, `verified`, `vetted`, `ready`, `cleared`, `pass*`, `looks good`, `sanction*`, `accept*`, `audit*`, `permit*`, `allow*`, `authori[sz]*`, `certif*`, `checked`, `green`, `model`, `fallback`, `generated`, …); angle brackets or any character outside printable ASCII; an addressee, AI or indefinite person; status and permission claims, including `no further|more|other|additional|extra <word>`; a secret-like value (a run of 16 or more hex digits, or a token of 16 or more letters, digits and `_+/=-` that holds a digit, #180); and the fallback marker (below). The marker rule lives only here since #180: the validator accepts ordinary sentences about a model layer or a host ("Returns the record created by the model layer."), a committed benign set holds 20 of them that the validator had rejected, and a rejected description that imitates the marker is tainted. A model that imitates the marker in an accepted description is stored with provenance `model`; consumers read provenance from `described_by`, never from the text. A tainted rejection is stored with ` [no-extra]` appended to `last_error`. `recordDescribeFailure` reads and writes the row in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, counts a describer-path failure only on a row still `claimed` (a worker-path failure on any row not `failed`), grants the extra attempt only when the caller says the rejection is extendable (default not), marks the failure unless the caller says it is clean (default marked), and never extends a row whose previous failure is marked. (d) **Fallback.** When the third attempt fails with an extendable rejection too, the host stores a fallback description built without a model from trusted index data (`fallback.ts`) that repeats no name at all, neither the function's nor a callee's: `Defines a <kind> that takes <n> parameters and calls <m> other functions (generated without a model).` (`1 parameter`, `no parameters`, `1 other function`, `no other functions`; the parameter clause is left out when the stored signature does not parse; `m` counts the distinct indexed callees other than the function itself). Its only search term is the kind, `nameFits` true. The sentence is validated without the marker and the marker is added afterwards; if the sentence does not pass, the function stays `failed`. It is stored with provenance `fallback`, model NULL, the backlog row deleted, with `INSERT OR IGNORE` (it never replaces a description that exists, and then no fallback is reported), and reported as `fallback: true` on that rejection; a fallback-only submit triggers embedding and the catalog refresh like an accepted one. **A fallback goes back to the model (#180).** `syncBacklog` puts a function whose description has provenance `fallback` back in the backlog as `pending` with `attempts` 0, at most `MAX_FALLBACK_REQUEUES` (3) times; the count is kept in `meta` under `fallback_requeues:<contentKey>`. A model description then replaces the fallback (`INSERT OR REPLACE`, provenance `model`); a new fallback never replaces the one that exists, so after the third return the last fallback stays. **Batch membership (#180):** `recordDescribeFailure` takes the batch id on the describer path and, in its one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, counts nothing for a row that is no longer claimed by that batch; `submitDescriptions` then answers `not_in_batch`. Injection and secret rejections never get the extra attempt or a fallback. Deterministic: the same symbol and sheet always give the same fallback. `last_error` is `<reason>: <detail>`; `gantry backlog --failed` shows only the leading rule name (and the field, `searchTerms` or `suggestedName`), never the detail, which may echo part of a description token.
   Acceptance (measured, committed tests; numbers are in the PR): 100% of the attack corpus rejected (every input of both independent reviews plus generated variants, at least 350 cases; the first review's and second review's corpora are separate files); at least 97% of the honest corpus accepted (the earlier rows, the second review's 60 fresh descriptions, and 70 more written for this round); at least 90% of a 60-row held-out set written after all tuning and run once; at least 95% of the 200 real Haiku descriptions from the M0 spike accepted on first pass (`docs/spikes/describe/validate-real.ts`, counts only); `validateDescription` < 2 ms median and < 50 ms at the caps; word list decoded in < 50 ms; redactor changes < 2% of this repository's own non-test source files and catches every credential format of both reviews (at least 50); a mutation script (`scripts/describe-mutations.ts`, part of `pnpm check` since #108) disables 46 rules one at a time (the redactor rules of #108, the feedback, extra-attempt and fallback rules of #150, and the gate and fallback rules of #175 included) and a test must fail for each (timing assertions are skipped in these runs; a lock file outside the tree, holding the process id and a hash of the mutated text, lets the next run restore a file after a SIGKILL, refuses to start with exit code 3 (printing the lock path) while the locking process is alive, judged by pid plus the start time and command line recorded in the lock so a reused pid does not block runs, takes over a stale lock under an exclusive-create mutex file so two starters cannot steal each other's lock, and leaves a file alone when it no longer holds the mutated text); it first runs a baseline, and a compile error, crash or timeout counts as an error, never as a kill. The text stays untrusted: the consuming side still shows it as `untrusted_description`.
   **Describer failed rate (#372).** A function counts as failed when it has no model description at the end of a run. The bar is 0 failed on fixtures under 100 functions; at most 1% otherwise (a 1% rate cannot be measured on a 22-function fixture). Search-by-behaviour checks over a describer run (`evals/go-search/describe-run.ts`) record the model id the route alias resolves to, are run three times, and the worst run is held to the bar.
   `redactForFactSheet` (same secret rule set plus, since #108: unquoted upper-case `NAME=value` environment assignments whose value is 16+ random-looking characters, secrets assembled by `[...literals].join("")` when the joined text is a credential or sits behind a credential name, a literal passed positionally to a login or credential call, and credentials given as command-line arguments such as a password, token or key flag or `-u user:pass`; it no longer redacts i18n labels (a name ending in `label`, `placeholder`, `hint`, `text` or similar with a capitalised phrase, or a non-credential name with a value that reads as a sentence; a strong credential name such as `password`, `token`, `secret` or `passphrase` is always redacted, with one exception: a value that is entirely a prompt to type the credential, `[Please] enter|type|confirm|re-enter [your|the|a] [new|current|old|access|api] password|passphrase|passcode|token|secret|key|pin|code|credential(s) [here]`, ending on that noun, as in `password: "Enter your password"`; `Your Password Is Swordfish` or `Set The Code To Orange Dawn` stay redacted), git commit ids (exactly 40 or 64 hex digits right after `commit`, `sha`, `sha1`, `rev` or `/commit/`), SRI hashes (`sha384-...`) or routes made of dictionary words, parameters and versions; the scanner that checks a description keeps the stricter reading of the commit-id and SRI cases; credential names matched by word parts so `monkey =`, `hotkey:`, `sortKey`, `cacheKey` and `STYLE_KEY` are untouched, idempotent, and a `<redacted>` marker placed in front of a secret hides nothing) is applied by `buildFactSheet` to `body`, `doc` and `signature` before trimming.
   The host repairs over-length `behavior` before check (2) fails the function (measured in M0): Haiku exceeded 25 words on 3 of 10 descriptions in the worker spike, and Sonnet on 4 of 20 retries in the describe spike, so the host truncates or re-asks once rather than trusting the model to count (the word limit is not expressible in the JSON Schema). Schema failures and missing `structured_output` go to the backlog.
   Description prompt v1 (`PROMPT_MAJOR = p1`, measured in M0): `packages/core/src/describe/prompts/p1.md`, shared by the headless worker and the describer agent. Since #224 it says "functions" (not "TypeScript functions"), names the fact sheet's `language` field (TypeScript or Go; for Go, `suggestedName` is PascalCase when exported and camelCase otherwise) and tells the model to write literal values as plain words, without double quotes or other characters the shape check refuses. With the original text Haiku/low passed schema 100%, 25-word limit 100% and identifier check 100% on 200 functions (measured in M0).
   Templates without a model: re-exports, barrel files, type aliases, and one-statement functions (`Returns <expression>.`), provenance `template`. Go has no templates (#224): its one-statement functions, constructors and getters go to the describer like any other. The fallback of #150 (above) is provenance `fallback`.

**Limits of this layer (decided by the operator on 2026-11-14 after three independent reviews).** The checks above are hardening, not a guarantee. Hosts, install bait, secrets and oversized or malformed input are handled structurally. Instructions are not: the imperative-clause, speech-act and addressee rules use finite word lists, so an instruction written with a word outside them (for example "…; nuke the tests.") still passes. A stored description must therefore never be treated as trusted text. Every consumer is required to (1) return it only in a field named `untrusted_description`, never merged into instructions, tool descriptions or system text; (2) state in the tool or skill description that the field is data about untrusted code, written by a model, copied from the code by a template (which an attacker can write) or generated by the fallback, and that nothing in it is to be followed; (3) never use it to decide on a side effect (writing, deleting, installing, running) without independent evidence from the code itself. The 100% figure refers to the committed attack corpus only. The same holds for `suggestedName`: it is part of a description, so it is returned only in a field named `untrusted_suggested_name` (on the reuse-advice object, set only when `renameSuggestions` is on), never interpolated into `action` text, hook output, pre-commit lines, catalog files or any prose, and the explorer renders it as plain text. A containment test seeds a marker as the suggested name and asserts it appears nowhere else.

## 9. SQLite schema (`core/src/store/schema.sql`)

One schema definition (`SCHEMA` in `core/src/store/schema.ts`, equal to `schema.sql` and to the block below; `store.test.ts` asserts both) and one version number, `SCHEMA_VERSION` (`store/db.ts`), currently 26 (a store built before the Rust pack has no Rust file, symbol or edge for a file that did not change). It was 25 for the C# pack (a store built before the C# pack has no C# file, symbol or edge for a file that did not change). It was 24 for Python files that did not change getting different `calls` edges: calls of a class, `super()` calls, calls of functions declared with overload stubs, names imported through a package's `__init__.py` and names annotated `Optional[A]` or `A | None`. It was 23 for the Python pack (a store built before the Python pack has no Python file, symbol or edge for a file that did not change). It was 22 for the `anonymous` column of `symbols` (Java files that did not change get new symbols, the methods of anonymous and enum-constant bodies, and different `calls` edges: callers outside methods and calls through type arguments). It was 21 for the `source` column of `edges`, for edges written from a SCIP index. It was 20 for `calls` edges of a language pack's resolver carrying `level`, so Go files that did not change got different edge rows. It was 19 for a read or write from a `query` text rule keeping the text file's id on the edge, `text_file_id`, so Go files that did not change got different edge rows. It was 18 for wrapper detection (a function whose URL starts with a top-level or imported const before its parameter is a wrapper only when a call passes a slash, so files that did not change get different `requests` edges). It was 17 for a tsconfig that extends a config a workspace package provides keeping its `paths` where `node_modules` does not provide that package. It was 16 for cross-repo client call linking (client calls link to endpoints of the other repos of a workspace, so files that did not change get different `requests` edges). It was 15 for the `repo` column of `pending_files`. It was 14 for sibling-package resolution (an import of a package that another repo of the workspace publishes resolves to that repo's files). It was 13 for #214 (the `repos` table and the `repo` column of `workspaces` and `files`; workspace ids carry the repo). It was 12 for #203 (a repo with a recipe gets recipe nodes for files nobody touched). There are no numbered migrations.

```sql
CREATE TABLE meta(key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, value TEXT);            -- schema_version, indexed_at, last_refresh_at, vec_available, embed_model, embed_dim, catalog_hash:*, catalog_written_at, backlog_synced_at (#147: set by a full syncBacklog, so `isIndexed` tells an initialised store from one a lookup created)
CREATE TABLE repos(name TEXT PRIMARY KEY, root TEXT, head_sha TEXT);
--   repos (#214): one row per repo of the workspace (a repo with no workspace file is a workspace of one). name: the repo name of every id of that repo (section 6); root: the real path it was last indexed from (read by describe and embed to find file text; never part of `index --dump`); head_sha: the commit its main layer was indexed at (this used to be `meta.head_sha`; `heal` reads and writes the row of the repo it runs in). The indexer writes the row first and deletes the rows of every repo that is not in the workspace (`Store.removeReposExcept`: files, symbols, edges, adapter nodes, search rows, workspaces, `similar_to` edges that end in the repo, the repo row).
--   Derived edges (#214, decided for the spike's third change): `similar_to` edges are rebuilt over the whole store (`rebuildSimilarEdges` in init, heal and embed) and stay on the file of the lower symbol id, so a pair across two repos lives on the repo whose name sorts first; removing a repo deletes the edges that end in it, and a heal of one repo rewrites them all, so no promise is made that another repo's `similar_to` edges stay byte-identical. Import edges between repos do not exist yet; the issue that writes them (#217) owns them on the importing file and recomputes them when the imported repo changes.
--   `meta.repo` and `meta.repo_root` no longer exist. `Store.repo()` answers the repo the connection was opened for (`openStore(path, { repo })`, which entry points pass as `paths.repo`), else the only row of `repos`; with several repos and none given it throws.
CREATE TABLE workspaces(id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, repo TEXT NOT NULL, name TEXT, dir TEXT, kind TEXT CHECK(kind IN ('app','package','unknown')),
  frameworks TEXT, deps TEXT, exports_map TEXT);
--   workspaces.repo and files.repo (#214): the repo part of the id (`ws:<repo>:...`, `file:<repo>:...`), written by the store from the id. Symbols, edges and adapter nodes reach their repo through their file (`file_id`).
CREATE TABLE files(id TEXT, layer TEXT CHECK(layer IN ('main','working')), repo TEXT NOT NULL, path TEXT, workspace_id TEXT,
  content_hash TEXT, mtime_ms INTEGER, deleted INTEGER DEFAULT 0, directives TEXT, lang TEXT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY(id, layer));
--   files.directives (#105): JSON array of the file's leading directive strings, e.g. ["use client"]. NULL and '[]' both mean none.
--   `FileRecord.directives` is absent when empty; `dumpGraph` adds `directives` to a file row only when non-empty.
--   `dumpGraph` (#214) adds `repo` to every workspace and file and a top-level `repos: [{ name }]` sorted by name, with no local path, so the dump does not depend on the machine; `index --dump` from any repo of a workspace is the same bytes.
CREATE TABLE symbols(id TEXT, layer TEXT, file_id TEXT, name TEXT, kind TEXT, exported INTEGER, signature TEXT,
  start_line INTEGER, end_line INTEGER, loc INTEGER, complexity INTEGER, stmt_count INTEGER,
  exact_hash TEXT, shape_hash TEXT, minhash BLOB, near_minhash BLOB, renamed_hash TEXT, lang TEXT NOT NULL, anonymous INTEGER, PRIMARY KEY(id, layer));
--   anonymous: NULL except on a symbol declared inside a body that has no name of its own (the methods of a Java anonymous class or enum-constant body, section 7), which has 1. `SymbolRecord.anonymous` is `true` there and absent otherwise, and `dumpGraph` shows it only when set. No view reads it yet.
--   Go interface methods (#222): each method in the body of an in-repo interface is a `method` symbol `#<Iface>.<Method>`, the target of `calls` edges made through the interface.
--   near_minhash (#131): 64 little-endian uint32, the MinHash of the near-tier token stream (section 17).
--   renamed_hash (#151): the SHA-256 (first 32 hex chars, as exact_hash) of the exact token stream with the symbol's own name replaced by `$0`. exact_hash does not use it on purpose: contentKey = `<exactHash>:<PROMPT_MAJOR>` keys descriptions, backlog rows and vectors.
CREATE TABLE edges(src TEXT, dst TEXT, kind TEXT, layer TEXT, confidence TEXT, count INTEGER, evidence TEXT, file_id TEXT, level INTEGER, text_file_id TEXT, source TEXT,
  PRIMARY KEY(src, dst, kind, layer));
--   source: NULL except on an edge written from a SCIP index, which has `'scip'` (section 11, precise mode). `dumpGraph` adds `source` to an edge only when set, so a dump made without an index has none.
--   level: NULL except on a `calls` edge from a language pack's resolver: 1, 2 or 3 (section 7, call levels). `dumpGraph` adds `level` to an edge only when set.
--   text_file_id: NULL except on a `reads` / `writes` edge that a `query` text rule attributes to a Go function (section 5): the id (`file:<repo>:<path>`, section 6) of the text file whose statement it is. No `files` row has that id (a text file has no syntax tree); the id is a reference by path. With several query files for one function and table, the one whose path sorts first.
CREATE INDEX symbols_file ON symbols(file_id, layer);            -- per-file replace without a table scan
CREATE INDEX edges_file ON edges(file_id, layer);
CREATE TABLE adapter_nodes(id TEXT, layer TEXT CHECK(layer IN ('main','working')), kind TEXT, ws TEXT,
  label TEXT, file_id TEXT, data TEXT, PRIMARY KEY(id, layer));
CREATE INDEX adapter_nodes_file ON adapter_nodes(file_id, layer);
--   ws: workspace id; file_id: the file the node was found in; data: adapter-specific JSON (routes: routeId, urlPath, path, options, component, loader, beforeLoad, root, index, pathless).
--   kind: route | endpoint | middleware | table | procedure (#45) | client_call (#47). Endpoint data (#45): Hono {adapter: "hono", method, path (full URL path), routePath (before mounts), app, handler, middleware?, file, line};
--   server functions {adapter: "tanstack-start", name, method, validator, middleware, handler, exported, file, line}. Middleware data {adapter, path (pattern), name, app, file, line}.
--   `handler` is the identifier passed, or `inline`; a path piece the adapter cannot read is kept as `{name}`.
--   handlerSpan? (#47, all three endpoint adapters): {start, end}, the first and last line of an inline handler; absent for a named handler (see "Trace" below).
--   client_call data (#47): {adapter: "clients", rule ("server-fn" | "orpc" | "hono" | "fetch" | "instance" | "wrapper"), reason ("no_endpoint" | "dynamic_url" | "external_url" | "not_a_server_fn" | "client_alias" | "ambiguous" | "wrapper_body"),
--   label, caller (the symbol or file holding the call), file, line, lines? (when one caller has several such calls), wrapper? (the wrapper's symbol id, reason `wrapper_body`), candidates? (reason `ambiguous` of a fetch-like call: the sorted endpoint ids it may reach)}. No edge points at a client_call node.
--   oRPC endpoint data (#46): {adapter: "orpc", path (dotted), router (root router key `<file path>#<name>`), handler, input, output, middleware (`.use()` in the procedure's own chain),
--   route? ({method?, path?} from `.route()`), mountedAt? (the handler's literal `prefix` option, else the Hono path without a trailing `/*`), honoPath? (the Hono pattern of the
--   registration that calls `RPCHandler`/`OpenAPIHandler.handle`), protocol? ("rpc" | "openapi"), file, line}. Table data (#46): {adapter: "drizzle", name, exportName, symbol (the export's symbol id), dialect ("sqlite" | "pg" | "mysql"), columns (keys of the columns object), file, line}. A table the recipe adapter writes (#247) carries the `data` fields its `table` emit lists, in that order, with `name` first unless the recipe places it; with no `data` it is {name, file, line}. A table named only at run time has `name` `{<expression text>}` and `data` {name, reason, file, line}.
--   Edges an adapter finds (`serves` route -> component symbol, `contains` parent route -> child route, `handles` endpoint -> handler code,
--   `serves` middleware -> endpoint, `requests` route -> server function, `reads` / `writes` code -> table) go into `edges` with the file_id of the file they were found in. The exception is a read or write that a `query` text rule attributes to a Go function: its file_id is the function's file and its `text_file_id` is the text file (section 5).
CREATE TABLE pending_files(session_id TEXT, repo TEXT NOT NULL, path TEXT, baseline TEXT, created_at TEXT,
  reindexed INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, PRIMARY KEY(session_id, repo, path));
--   One row per file a session's batch of edits touched, in the repo that holds it (section 14); baseline: JSON {symbol id: exactHash} of the file's symbols when the batch first touched it (a later edit of the file keeps it and only moves created_at). A row is deleted once its file is checked, by the (session_id, repo, path, created_at) that was read; rows older than 24 h are pruned at the start of each settle. reindexed: 1 once the file is indexed as of its last edit (set by PostToolUse when its own index finishes, or by a settle that re-indexed it); 0 on every new edit of the file, and for the whole batch when a manifest is edited.
CREATE TABLE descriptions(content_key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, behavior TEXT, search_terms TEXT, name_fits INTEGER,
  suggested_name TEXT, provenance TEXT, model TEXT, prompt_version TEXT, created_at TEXT);
CREATE TABLE backlog(content_key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, symbol_id TEXT, priority INTEGER, status TEXT
  CHECK(status IN ('pending','claimed','failed')), claimed_by TEXT, claimed_at TEXT, attempts INTEGER DEFAULT 0, last_error TEXT);
CREATE TABLE usage_log(id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, at TEXT, task TEXT, model TEXT, effort TEXT, functions INTEGER,
  input_tokens INTEGER, output_tokens INTEGER, cost_estimate_usd REAL, source TEXT);
CREATE TABLE overviews(node_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, text TEXT, input_key TEXT, model TEXT, created_at TEXT);
CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE fts_symbols USING fts5(symbol_id UNINDEXED, name_tokens, suggested_name, path, behavior, search_terms);
-- Embeddings (D = 768 for the M0 model, so the dimension is known). `openStore` loads sqlite-vec first and writes meta.vec_available (1 or 0); when it loads,
-- a new store also gets the two tables below and records meta.embed_model and meta.embed_dim. If it cannot load, everything except vector search keeps working,
-- and `ensureVecTables` creates the tables later where it can; a changed model or dimension drops and recreates both tables and re-embeds.
-- CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE vec_descriptions USING vec0(content_key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, embedding float[768] distance_metric=cosine);
-- CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE vec_code USING vec0(content_key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, embedding float[768] distance_metric=cosine);
```

**Overlay schema (`overlay.db`, attached as `overlay`; `OVERLAY_SCHEMA` in `core/src/store/overlay.ts`, #285).** `openStore(dbPath, { overlayPath? })` attaches `<dataDir>/overlay.db` (default: `overlay.db` beside `dbPath`; `":memory:"` for an in-memory store) after the store schema is in place, sets WAL only when the file is new, and runs the statements below. Rule: tables are only ever added with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, never dropped or altered; the overlay has no schema version and is never deleted by Gantry (`resetStaleStore` removes only `gantry.db` and its sidecars, and `parse-cache.db`); opening and closing a store leaves its bytes unchanged. Queries name the tables `overlay.dismissals`, `overlay.layouts`, `overlay.labels`, `overlay.hidden`, `overlay.groups`, `overlay.group_rules`, `overlay.views`, `overlay.anchors` and `overlay.anchor_renames`; a transaction on the store covers both files.

```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.dismissals(a TEXT, b TEXT, reason TEXT, PRIMARY KEY(a, b));
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.layouts(view TEXT, node_id TEXT, x REAL, y REAL, PRIMARY KEY(view, node_id));
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.labels(node_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, text TEXT NOT NULL);
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.hidden(position INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, selector TEXT NOT NULL);          -- JSON Selector
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.groups(id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, name TEXT NOT NULL, selectors TEXT NOT NULL); -- JSON Selector[]
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.group_rules(group_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, may_use TEXT NOT NULL);          -- JSON string[]; no row = mayUse null
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.views(id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, name TEXT NOT NULL, spec TEXT NOT NULL);       -- JSON FilterSpec
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.anchors(node_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, exact_hash TEXT NOT NULL);              -- #288
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.anchor_renames(node_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, renamed_hash TEXT NOT NULL);     -- #349
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS overlay.anchor_families(node_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, family TEXT NOT NULL);          -- #349: function | component | hook
```

The five tables from `labels` to `views` (#286) hold the curation objects of section 7. JSON is `JSON.stringify` of the zod-parsed value (key order of the schema), so equal input gives equal bytes; no timestamps. They are written only through `core/src/overlay/repo.ts` and are never touched by indexing, so an index rebuild keeps them.

`overlay.anchors` (#288) has one row per `sym:` id some entry uses (a label, or an id inside an `ids` selector of the hidden list, a group, or a saved view's `hidden`): the `exactHash` of that symbol as it was when the entry was last written. Every write in `repo.ts` upserts the anchor of each used `sym:` id whose symbol exists (only when the hash differs) and deletes the anchors no entry uses any more, in the same transaction; other id kinds (files, workspaces, routes) get none. `resetOverlay` clears it too. Section 11 says how it is used. `overlay.anchor_renames` (#349) is kept the same way and holds the `renamedHash` of the same symbols (no row for a symbol without one); it is a new table beside `anchors` (the overlay is never altered), so `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 12, and an entry written before it existed has no row there until its next write. `overlay.anchor_families` (#349) is kept the same way and holds the kind family of the same symbols (`function` for a function or a method, `component`, `hook`; the families of section 17), because a symbol that is gone no longer has a kind to compare; it is another added table, so `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 12. An anchor with no family row is never re-attached by `renamedHash`.

**Rebuild rule (#198).** A new store (no `meta` table) gets `SCHEMA` and `meta.schema_version` = `SCHEMA_VERSION`. A store whose `schema_version` is anything else, older or newer, is stale: `openStore` throws `StaleStoreError { found, expected, dbPath }` and changes nothing. `resetStaleStore(paths)` (`store/stale.ts`, exported from `@gantry/core`) deletes `gantry.db`, `gantry.db-wal`, `gantry.db-shm` and `parse-cache.db` when `gantry.db` exists with another version, prints `gantry: the index was built by an older version of Gantry (schema <found>, now <SCHEMA_VERSION>); rebuilding it` to stderr and returns `{ reset: true, found }`; it returns `{ reset: false }` and touches nothing otherwise. It does not look at repo names: a repo that was renamed or left the workspace file is handled by the next full main index (`removeReposExcept`, above), which keeps the rest of the store. Every non-hook entry point that opens the store calls it first (`init`, `index`, `embed`, `heal`, `map`, the lookups `search`, `node` and `trace`, `review`, `backlog`, `usage`, `describe --staged`, `hooks install` and `uninstall`, `uninstall`, the MCP server and the explorer server) and then goes on as on a repository with no index: `init` and `index` build it, the MCP lazy refresh and the lookups run a full main index through `heal`, `map` runs `rebuildIndex` (a heal, or a plain full main index where there is no `HEAD`; fails open with a note on stderr), and `backlog` and `usage` report that nothing is indexed. Hooks never reset or rebuild (section 14). Descriptions, embeddings and overviews go with the old store; dismissals and layouts live in `overlay.db` and stay; `/gantry:sync` re-describes.

Any change that makes a full re-index necessary bumps `SCHEMA_VERSION` in the same PR: a schema change, or a change to what the extractor, an adapter or the resolver write for unchanged files (incremental indexing only rewrites changed files, so an older store would keep the old output for every file nobody touched).

Effective view = working rows override main rows for the same file id; a working `files` row with `deleted=1` hides the main file. `adapter_nodes` follow the rule of `symbols`: a node belongs to its `file_id`, `replaceFile` replaces a file's symbols, edges and adapter nodes together, and the effective view takes a node from the layer chosen for its file. WAL mode; writers take an exclusive lock via `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` with a 5 s busy timeout.
Backlog priority: exported functions and route/entry files 0, other exported 1, local 2.

**Adapter framework (#45, `core/src/adapters/`).** `framework.ts` defines `Adapter<F> { id, languages?, appliesTo(ws), mayMatch?(file), includeTests?, extract?(ctx), link?(ctx) }` (`languages` is the files an adapter reads, `["typescript"]` by default; the recipe adapter lists `["typescript", "go"]` (#368), and `resolver.targetAt` and `resolver.typeNameAt` give a Go adapter the function a call or a name refers to and the type of an expression), `AdapterNode` (an `AdapterNodeRecord` with `kind: route | endpoint | middleware | table | procedure` and `ws` a workspace id) and `runAdapterList(ctx, adapters)`; `adapters/index.ts` registers `ADAPTERS` (in run order: the recipe adapter (#203), TanStack Router routes, TanStack Start server functions, Hono, oRPC, Drizzle, client calls (#47), which is last so its `link` sees every endpoint) and `runAdapters(ctx)`. `extract` runs once per file of a workspace the adapter applies to (test files skipped unless `includeTests`) and returns nodes, edges and adapter-private `facts`; `extract` gets `{ fileId, path, ws, source, parsed, resolver, program() }` (`program()` is the file's oxc statements, parsed once per run and shared). `link` runs once per run with every extracted file's facts, the nodes all adapters returned so far, the resolver and `file(id)` for any read file; it is where cross-file work happens (Hono mounts, loader to server function, and later oRPC nesting, Drizzle reads and writes, client-call matching). The indexer calls `runAdapters` over the whole read set on every run (main and working, full or targeted) and stores each node and edge with the file it names, replaced per file like symbols; a file whose adapter output changed because of another file is rewritten like an importer whose edges changed. Output is sorted (nodes by id; edges by file, src, dst, kind); a repeated node id or (src, dst, kind) edge keeps the first. **The recipe adapter (#203, `adapters/recipe.ts`)** is registered first. It does nothing without a recipe (section 5). With one, `extract` runs the rules that apply to each file (the rule's `when.dependency` and `files`) and `link` runs the helpers across files (mounts and base paths composed into full paths, file-system routes, factory returns, router arguments), then writes the endpoint, middleware and route nodes described below, with their `handles`, `serves` and `contains` edges. **Data access (#247, `recipe/data-access.ts`).** The same `link` also writes the `table` nodes and the `reads` / `writes` edges a recipe describes (section 5): `table` emits, text rules and `CREATE TABLE` in SQL text declare tables (`table:<repo>:<name>`; two bindings with the same name get `~2` as before, any other declaration of a name already declared is the same table); `read` and `write` emits and SQL text give an edge from the code holding the access (its symbol, or the file for module-level code) to the table. As for Drizzle below, `count` is the number of accesses, `evidence` one `path:line` per access line (the line of the method name), confidence `high` when the table was resolved through a binding, a model or a declared name and `low` when only an import's name matched or several tables fit the model. SQL text only reaches tables that are declared somewhere (a `CREATE TABLE` in a string or a `.sql` file, a `table` emit or a text rule): undeclared names such as catalogs are skipped. A name known only at run time is a `low` edge to a table node `{<expression>}` with `data.reason`, never a guess. For a recipe with no data-access rule, `sql` or text rule, nothing changes; no ORM is built in beyond the Drizzle adapter below. Their `data.adapter` is the label the rule's emit names, so a Hono recipe writes `"hono"` and the scorer, the client matcher and oRPC read the nodes as before; route nodes carry no `adapter`. A node with an unreadable path piece also has `data.reason`. On `fixtures/pnpm-turbo` and `fixtures/adapters` the committed recipes give byte-identical nodes, data and edges to the TanStack Router and Hono adapters (`recipe/parity.test.ts` runs the recipe adapter alone against them) and, with their Drizzle rules (#247), to the Drizzle adapter (`recipe/data-access.test.ts`: the same table nodes, data in the same key order, and the same `reads` / `writes` edges with count, evidence and confidence). The built-in TanStack Router and Hono adapters are kept and stay registered next to it; a node two adapters both write (same id) is stored once, the first in run order (the recipe), and `link` contexts see each node once. Which adapters run can be set for a test with `IndexOptions.adapters`.

Adapters apply by dependency: TanStack Start to workspaces depending on `@tanstack/react-start`, `@tanstack/solid-start` or `@tanstack/start`; Hono to workspaces depending on `hono`; oRPC to workspaces depending on `@orpc/server`; Drizzle tables to workspaces depending on `drizzle-orm`, and Drizzle usage to those and to workspaces that directly depend on one of them (#46). `handles` points at the code that holds the handler: a named handler's symbol (followed through imports), and for an inline handler the symbol whose body contains it (for a server function, its `const`), or the file for module-level code, because that is where the call graph attributes the handler's calls (#45 changed this from a synthesized `sym:<path>#<name>.handler` id, which would name no node). A route's `requests` edge to a server function is `high` when the call resolves to the server function's symbol (or a server function of the route file), `low` when an unresolved import matches one server function's export name in the workspace. Hono middleware `serves` every endpoint of its own app and the apps mounted below it whose path its pattern (`*`, `/p/*`, exact, `:param`) matches; registration order is not modelled. **oRPC and Drizzle (#46).** oRPC procedures are `<builder>...handler(fn)` chains, where a builder is `os` from `@orpc/server`, `implement(contract)`, or a top-level `const` chained on or aliasing a builder (followed across files); routers are object literals (nested, spread, returned by a router factory of the same file), `<builder>.router({...})` and `os.lazy(() => import(...))` / `lazy(...)` (the module's default export), followed through imports. Each procedure reachable from a root router becomes `ep:<ws>:rpc <path>` (kind `endpoint`), stored with the file that defines the procedure, with `handles` to the code holding its handler (the rule above: for an inline handler in a router object, the router's `const`). A Hono registration whose callback calls `.handle(...)` on an `RPCHandler`/`OpenAPIHandler` gives the served router's procedures `mountedAt`. Drizzle `reads` / `writes` edges go from the code holding the call (symbol, or the file for module-level code) to `table:<name>`: `reads` for `.from(T)` and `.leftJoin|rightJoin|innerJoin|fullJoin(T)` on a call chain, `$count(T)` and `.query.<t>.findMany|findFirst` (also `_query`; `<t>` matched to a table export name), `writes` for `.insert|update|delete(T)`; `T` is a table binding, a namespace member (`schema.T`) or an `alias(T, ...)`. `count` is the number of calls, `evidence` one `path:line` per call line. Confidence is `high` when the binding resolves to the table's export (or the query key names exactly one table), `low` when the import does not resolve and only the export name matches. Raw SQL is not read.

**Client calls (#47, `adapters/clients.ts`).** Applies to app workspaces and to workspaces depending on `@orpc/client`, an `@orpc/*-query` package, TanStack Start, `hono`, `ky` or `axios`. `requests` edges go from the code holding the call (symbol, or the file for module-level code) to the endpoint, stored with the caller's file, `count` the calls and `evidence` one `path:line` per call line:

| Rule | Call                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Endpoint                          | Confidence                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1    | `getPosts(...)` or `useServerFn(getPosts)` where `getPosts` resolves to a server function's `const` (through imports, or one of the same file)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `ep:<ws>:fn getPosts`             | `high` (`low` when an unresolved import matches one server function's export name in the caller's workspace)                                                                                                  |
| 2    | A member chain rooted at an oRPC client: a top-level `const` initialised by `createORPCClient`, `createTanstackQueryUtils` or another `@orpc/*` utils factory, directly or through a function of the same file returning that call, or a sub-client alias `const x = client.a` (any depth, followed through imports). One trailing helper (`queryOptions`, `mutationOptions`, `infiniteOptions`, `queryKey`, `mutationKey`, `infiniteKey`, `key`, `call`, ...) is stripped, the alias prefix prepended, and the dotted path matched to `ep:*:rpc <path>` in every workspace                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `high` if unique                  |
| 3    | A member chain rooted at a Hono client (`hc(...)` from `hono/client`) ending in `$get` / `$post` / `$put` / `$patch` / `$delete` / `$options`; keys (string-literal computed keys included, `index` = empty) joined with `/`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `ep:*:<METHOD> <path>` (or `ALL`) | `high` if unique                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 4    | `fetch(url, opts?)` (also `window.` / `globalThis.` / `self.`), `ky(url)` / `ky.<verb>(url)`, `axios(url)` / `axios.<verb>(url)` / `axios({ url, method })` whose URL the evaluator below reads (a run-time value such as `${x}` becomes `:param`, query and hash dropped); method from the `method` option or the verb, default GET; matched to the path of every endpoint node that has a string `data.method` and `data.path`, whatever made it, segment by segment, fewest parameter segments first (see Matching a fetch-like call, below)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | endpoint with a method and a path | `high` when unique and fully evaluated, else an `ambiguous` gap (`data.rule` `"fetch"`)                                                                                                                       |
| 4b   | A call on an instance: a top-level `const` initialised by `<axios>.create({ baseURL })`, `<ky>.create({ prefixUrl })` or `<kyInstance>.extend({ prefixUrl? })` (`extend` without `prefixUrl` keeps the parent's base), followed through imports and re-exports: `x.get\|post\|put\|patch\|delete\|head\|options(url, ...)`, `x.request({ url, method })`, `x({ url, method })`, `x(url, opts)`; and the same calls on the default `axios` import when a file of the same workspace assigns `axios.defaults.baseURL` at the top level. The URL is joined by the library's rule, then matched as in rule 4: axios puts base and path together with one `/` (an absolute request URL replaces the base and is external), ky puts `prefixUrl`, `/` and an input without a leading slash together (a leading slash is refused); the base is read by the evaluator too, and a base that is no path, absolute URL or empty text is dropped | endpoint with a method and a path | as rule 4 (`data.rule` `"instance"`; a base the evaluator does not read is a `dynamic_url` gap; two different `axios.defaults.baseURL` values in a workspace make its default-`axios` calls `ambiguous` gaps) |
| 4c   | A call of a client wrapper (below): `relFetch("/users", { method: "POST" })`, `request("DELETE", url)`; the arguments go into the wrapper's URL template and method, then the URL is matched as in rules 4 and 4b (through the wrapper's own instance, if it calls one)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | endpoint with a method and a path | as rule 4 (`data.rule` `"wrapper"`; an argument that fills the start of the path, or a method that is not static, is a `dynamic_url` gap)                                                                     |
| 5    | Anything a client call names that matches nothing, a dynamic or absolute URL, a `useServerFn` of no server function, a sub-client alias that no call goes through (passed on as a value)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | none: a `client_call` node        | not guessed                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

**Client wrappers.** A top-level function (a declaration, an arrow or function expression bound to a `const`, or a default export, exported or not) is a client wrapper when a client call in its body (`fetch`, a call on an instance, the default `axios` or `ky`) or a call of another wrapper passes one of its parameters unchanged into the start of the request path, or into the method (the method argument itself, or the `method` of an `init` argument). A leading base (`${API_URL}`) before the path in the wrapper's body is skipped. Fixed text before the parameter may be empty, only slashes, or not end in a slash (`/api${path}`): that is a wrapper. Text that ends in a slash (`/api/${path}`, `/users/${id}`) is decided by the calls: the function is a wrapper when at least one call that resolves to it passes an argument whose static text contains a slash (`api("users/1")`, `api("/users")`), and otherwise the parameter only fills a segment, the function is an ordinary call with a `:param` (rule 4) and the body is a request. Inference runs to a fixpoint of at most 8 passes, so a wrapper that calls a wrapper is one; a function whose qualifying calls disagree on URL template, method or client is not guessed: each call of it is a `dynamic_url` gap (its body is still `wrapper_body`). At each call of a wrapper (resolved through imports) the arguments (read by the evaluator, each alternative of each one) are substituted into its URL template and method and the result is matched like rule 4 or 4b with `data.rule` `"wrapper"`: an argument that fills a later segment becomes `:param`, one that fills the start (or whose alternatives all start with something that is not text), or a method argument that is not a string, is a `dynamic_url` gap. The client calls in a wrapper's body are not requests: each wrapper has one `client_call` node `call:<ws>:wrapper <name>` with reason `wrapper_body` and `data.wrapper` its symbol id, and no `requests` edge. The recipe's `clientWrappers` (section 5) lists wrappers inference cannot see, such as a path passed through another library's call. Wrappers built at run time and paths passed in objects or class fields are not followed.

**The URL evaluator (`adapters/urlEval.ts`).** `evalUrl(expr, scope)` turns the URL expression of a fetch, ky or axios call, of an instance's base and of a wrapper's argument into one or more alternatives, each a list of parts: literal text, `{ param }` (a parameter or a variable), `{ env: NAME }` and `{ unknown }`. Forms: string literals and templates; `+`; a local, top-level or imported `const` (imports followed through the resolver, re-exports included); a TypeScript `enum` member; a member of an object literal bound to a `const` and written `as const` (`ROUTES.me`, nested or with a string key; a member of any other object is a `param`, since the object can change); `process.env.X` and `import.meta.env.X` (an `env` part); `a ?? b` and `a || b` (both alternatives); `c ? a : b` (both alternatives, the condition is not read); `new URL(path, base)` (an absolute path replaces the path of the base, keeping its origin when the base writes one out and a leading environment member or parameter of the base, so that alternative is dropped; an unknown origin such as `location.origin` is not kept; a relative path joins the base; an absolute URL replaces both). Anything else is `unknown`. Evaluation stops at depth 8 and at 16 alternatives, where the result is `unknown`. An alternative that does not start with text (an environment member, `unknown`, a parameter) is dropped: it names no path, and the base URLs that environment members stand for are not mapped.

**Matching a fetch-like call.** Per remaining alternative, the candidates are the endpoints whose method matches (or is `ALL`) and whose path matches segment by segment: a literal of the call matches an endpoint parameter, a parameter of the call does not match an endpoint literal. Of those, the ones with the fewest endpoint-parameter segments are kept, then narrowed as below. Across alternatives the call links when the alternatives that match name one endpoint, and the confidence is `high` when, in addition, every matching alternative is fully evaluated (no environment member and no `unknown` part inside it; a parameter is a value that fills a segment and counts as evaluated). Otherwise (several endpoints, or one that rests on a part that is not known) there is no edge: a `client_call` node with reason `ambiguous` and `data.candidates`, the sorted ids of the endpoints. A URL with no alternative left is a `dynamic_url` gap, one that matches no endpoint `no_endpoint` and an absolute URL for another origin `external_url`.

**Across repos.** In a workspace of several repos (section 4) the candidates are the endpoints of every repo. The index of a repo reads the other repos' endpoints and workspaces from the store (`AdapterRunContext.external`), so a call links to a handler in another repo with the same rules, and the evidence is the caller's own line. Several candidates narrow to the caller's workspace and its dependencies, then to the caller's repo; the same method and path in two other repos is an `ambiguous` gap with both endpoints as `data.candidates` (a Hono or oRPC client call gets a `low` edge to each). A URL the code does not give statically (an environment member, a base the recipe does not state) is a gap, never a guessed edge. Endpoints do not depend on client links, so one more pass settles it: after a run, every repo whose siblings' endpoints differ from before is linked again (a first run, or an edit in one repo that adds, removes or renames an endpoint), and a targeted run in one repo leaves no edge in another to a handler that is gone. An id of an endpoint carries its repo (`ep:<repo>:...`), which is how trace and the endpoints list name it.

Several candidates (the same rpc path or Hono route in two workspaces) narrow to those in the caller's workspace or its dependencies; one left is `high`, else every candidate of an oRPC or Hono client call gets a `low` edge (a fetch-like call is an `ambiguous` gap, above). An oRPC path that is a router prefix (`orpc.posts.key()`) is a cache helper, not a request, and gets neither. Calls inside a route file's `Route` definition (its loader) are left to the route's own `requests` edge.

**Evidence cap (#47).** The resolver keeps up to 20 distinct evidence strings per edge (`EVIDENCE_CAP`, was 3): `trace` decides by evidence line which inline handler a call of a shared router `const` belongs to, and a router often calls one helper from many handlers. A store indexed before #47 keeps 3 until each file is re-indexed.

**Trace (#47, `core/src/trace.ts`).** `trace(store, target, { direction?, maxNodes = 2000, maxDepth = 6 }) → { target, direction, chain: { id, kind, label, repo?, via, depth, from?, confidence? }[], edges, gaps, omitted, truncated } | null`. `target` is a route, endpoint, table, middleware, symbol or file id, a route id or URL path (`/posts/$postId`), an endpoint label (`GET /posts/:id`, `rpc posts.list`), a table name, a file path or `path:line` (`resolveTraceTarget`; null when nothing matches). `direction` defaults to `up` for a table and `down` otherwise. Down: route `serves` its component and its file's `Route` const (which holds the loader and an inline component; `via: "serves"`) → `renders` / `calls` / `uses_hook` → `requests` endpoint → `handles` handler → `calls` → `reads` / `writes` table; `requests` (from a route too) and `handles` start a new budget of `maxDepth` hops of `calls` / `renders` / `uses_hook`. Up: the same edges backwards (`serves` only from routes, so middleware is not walked), ending at routes. **Inline handlers:** an endpoint with `data.handlerSpan` `handles` the code enclosing its handler, which also holds its siblings' calls (an oRPC router object, a function registering several Hono routes). From such a handler only the outgoing `calls` / `requests` / `reads` / `writes` edges with an evidence line inside one of the spans of the endpoints that reached it are followed; going up, a handler reaches such an endpoint only through an edge with an evidence line inside that endpoint's span. An edge with `EVIDENCE_CAP` lines, fewer than its count, and none inside is followed and reported (the dropped lines may be inside). Node ids are unchanged. **Across repos:** the walk has no repo filter, so a `requests` edge to an endpoint in another repo of the workspace (section 9, client calls) is followed like any other: a page in one repo reaches the handler and tables of the repo that serves its call, and a table reaches the pages that request it from another repo. When the store holds more than one repo, each chain step carries `repo`, the repo its id names (absent with one repo, so single-repo results are unchanged). The walk is breadth-first in sorted edge order, visits each node once (cycles are common) and at most `maxNodes` nodes (`truncated`). `chain` keeps the start and the nodes on a path to an endpoint or table (down) or to an endpoint or route (up), sorted by depth then id, each with the edge kind it was reached by (`via`) and its parent (`from`); `omitted` counts the other visited nodes; `confidence: "low"` marks a step reached by a low edge; `edges` are the traversed edges between chain nodes. `gaps` (sorted: unmatched client calls of visited code, endpoints without a handler or (down) reaching no table, (up) endpoints no client call requests, incomplete span checks, the node budget, then one line counting the nodes where the hop limit stopped the walk). Read-only and deterministic.

Search, browse and node tool details (#28). `gantry_search` runs hybrid search: keyword FTS over name tokens, suggested name, path, behavior and search terms (top 50) fused with the nearest description vectors (top 50, query embedded with the `query` prefix) by reciprocal rank fusion (k = 60), where only vector hits with a cosine similarity of at least 0.5 to the query are fused (`VECTOR_MIN_COSINE`; the nearest vectors of a query that matches nothing are noise and must not outrank a keyword match), then exported ×1.2 and exact name ×2; with no cached model or no vectors it is keywords only, and the model is never downloaded by a search. Each hit is `{ id, repo, name, kind, lang, path, line, signature, score, untrusted_description, described_by? }` (`described_by`: `model`, `template` (text copied from the code, which an attacker can write) or `fallback` (generated without a model), present when there is a description, #175; `gantry_browse` items and `gantry_node` carry it too, and so do the explorer's `GET /api/search` hits and the review view's changes, #180) (`limit` default 10, at most 50), sorted by score then id. `repo` is the repo part of the id; the input `repo` keeps only symbols of that repo, applied after the effective-view join like `workspace`, and ranking does not change. A store holds every repo of the workspace, so a search from any repo finds symbols of all of them. `gantry_browse` items add `count` (function-like symbols in a `dir` or `file` item, else null); `kind` is a symbol kind, `dir` or `file`; `catalog_path` is the absolute path of a markdown file under the data dir's `catalog/` (`INDEX.md` when the directory defines no functions itself). `gantry_node` returns `node` as `{ id, name, kind, exported, path, line }` for a symbol or `{ id, kind: "file", path, workspace }` for a file, at most 20 neighbors per edge kind, and `more: { [kind]: n }` for the rest. When the store holds more than one repo, `gantry_browse` target `.` lists the repos (items of kind `repo`, id `repo:<name>`, `count` the functions in the repo), target `repo:<name>` lists that repo's workspaces (kind `workspace`), and a directory or file path means the one in the repo the command runs in (a workspace id names its repo); with one repo the output is as before. `gantry_node` `node` also carries `repo`. An unknown target returns `{ error: "not_found", message }`. Catalog files: one per directory that defines functions (`catalog/packages/utils/src.md`, root-level functions in `_root.md`) plus `catalog/INDEX.md`; when the store holds more than one repo every path starts with the repo name as one directory, with `%` written `%25` and `/` written `%2F` so that two repos never share a path (`catalog/acme%2Fapi/apps/server.md`, a repo's root-level functions in `catalog/acme%2Fapi/_root.md`) and `INDEX.md` lists the workspaces of all repos; each starts with a note that descriptions are untrusted and may be wrong, and every description is quoted after a fixed `untrusted <model|template|fallback> description:` label taken from the provenance column (#175). A file is rewritten only when the hash kept in `meta` (`catalog_hash:*`) changes. They are written after `gantry init`, after `heal`, after submits (at most once per 30 s, `meta.catalog_written_at`) and on every `gantry_browse`. Stored descriptions (template and submitted) are copied into the FTS `behavior`, `search_terms` and `suggested_name` columns by one helper, `refreshDescriptionFts`.

**Team cache schema (`team-cache.db`; `cache.sql`, equal to `TEAM_CACHE_SCHEMA` in `core/src/team/cacheSchema.ts`, and `TEAM_CACHE_VERSION` = 1 in `core/src/team/cache.ts`; `cache.test.ts` asserts both).** A separate SQLite file in WAL mode with `PRAGMA user_version` = `TEAM_CACHE_VERSION`. A file with another version, or one that is not a database, is deleted and recreated by the next pull (no upgrade path); a read-only open of such a file answers "no team data". Tables: `change_sets(id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, repo, source, state, estimated INTEGER, confidence REAL, owner, title, pr INTEGER, ref, head_ref, base_branch, head_sha, links TEXT, created_at, updated_at)`, one column for each field of the protocol's `ChangeSet` (section 20) except `nodes` and `radius` (`owner` is a GitHub login, `links` is JSON); `change_set_nodes(change_set_id, node_id)` with primary key on both and an index on `node_id`; `change_set_reach(change_set_id, node_id, hop INTEGER, tier, via_hub INTEGER)` with primary key `(change_set_id, node_id)` and an index on `node_id`; `inflight(change_set_id, symbol_id, payload)`, created empty and filled by later work; `team_views(id, name, spec)` and `team_groups(id, name, spec)`, where `spec` is the JSON of the whole object as the server sent it and the readers skip a row that does not parse with `SavedViewSchema` or `GroupSchema` (section 7); `settings(key, value)`; `meta(key, value)` with the keys `pulled_at`, `etag`, `server`, `org`, `repo` and `last_error`. Text from the server (titles, owners, refs) is untrusted data.

## 10. CLI (`packages/cli`, binary `gantry`)

| Command                                                                                                                  | Behavior                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `gantry init [--estimate] [--exports-first] [--embed] [--workspace <file>]`                                              | Full deterministic index; on a team machine it first fills in the team's descriptions for the functions found (section 11, "Description sync"; best effort, 5 s), before the backlog is built; its summary prints the totals of the finished index (#338, `core/src/counts.ts`): `files` and `edges` count the effective rows including each workspace's `package.json` or `go.mod` row and its `depends_on` edges, the same numbers as `/api/meta` `counts`, and `function-like` counts distinct function-like bodies (one per content key, sensitive paths excluded), the same number as the `function-like:` line of `--estimate` without `--exports-first`; with `--estimate` prints function count, templated count, projected tokens (about 950 in including cache and 250 out per function (measured in M0)), batches of 40 and rounds of 4; no model calls ever. Does not embed: `--embed` is the explicit opt-in that runs `gantry embed` afterwards (slow on a large repo; section 11)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
