| `gantry index [--files <p...>] [--layer main\|working] [--dump] [--scip <file>] [--workspace <file>]`                    | Deterministic index; `--dump` prints the sorted graph JSON (used by snapshot tests); `--scip <file>` reads a SCIP index for this run (precise mode, section 11), and the summary then has a `scip:` line with the documents, edges, unmapped and stale counts                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `gantry embed [--limit <n>] [--budget-ms <n>] [--json]`                                                                  | Embeds what has no vector yet (descriptions first, then normalized code of function-like symbols with `stmtCount ≥ 3`, exported and entry-file functions first, code cut to 1,500 characters and 512 tokens). Resumable: stored keys are skipped, every batch of 8 commits, so Ctrl-C or a crash loses at most one batch. Stops at `--limit` vectors or `--budget-ms`. Progress line on stderr every few seconds. Never run by `init`, hooks or heal                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `gantry heal [--from <sha>] [--workspace <file>]`                                                                        | Reconcile main graph to HEAD (section 11). With no store for the repo it prints `no index for this repository in <dataDir>` to stderr, exits 0 and creates nothing (#147)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `gantry search <q> [--kind] [--lang <lang>] [--repo <name>] [--json]`, `gantry node <id\|path:line> [--json]`            | Lookups; `search --lang typescript\|go` keeps only symbols of that language (an unknown value is an error; `go` finds nothing until a Go module exists, #220); `search --json` hits carry `lang` and `repo`; `search --repo <name>` keeps only symbols of that repo of the workspace (an unknown name finds nothing), and when the store has more than one repo the text table prefixes each name with `[<repo>]` (a store of one repo prints as before); `node` prints a `repo:` line when the store has more than one repo, and its `--json` carries `repo`; a bare path means the file of the repo the command runs in; `search --json` returns a stored description only as `untrusted_description` with `described_by` (#175)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `gantry usage [--json]`, `gantry backlog [--json] [--failed] [--retry-failed]`                                           | Usage summary; backlog counts. Both are read-only and create no data dir (#147). `backlog --json` is `{ total, byStatus, byPriority, fallback, data_dir, indexed }` (`fallback` = descriptions with provenance `fallback`, #150; the text form prints it when above 0) (`indexed` = `isIndexed`, false with zero counts when there is no store); the text form ends with `Data: <dataDir>`. `--failed` lists failed rows `{ failed: [{ content_key, symbol_id, attempts, reason, field? }], data_dir, indexed }`, `reason` being the rule name only (section 8); `--retry-failed` resets them to `pending` with `attempts` 0 and prints `{ retried, ... }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `gantry hooks install`, `gantry hooks uninstall`, `gantry uninstall [--keep-data]`                                       | Section 12                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `gantry hook <session-start\|post-tool-use\|stop\|pre-commit\|pre-push\|post-checkout\|post-merge\|post-rewrite> [args]` | Hook entry points                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `gantry describe --staged`                                                                                               | Pre-commit describer (timeboxed/strict modes only)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `gantry map [--port] [--no-open] [--workspace <file>]`                                                                   | Start explorer server                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `gantry review <base>...<head> \| --pr <n> [--json] [--open]`                                                            | Review data / open view                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `gantry duplicates [--json] [--since <ref>] [--workspace <w>]`                                                           | Duplicate groups (#280): `buildDuplicates` of the Duplicates view with the view's defaults, so the groups equal the view's. Opens the store like `search` (heal and refresh first). `--json` prints `{ summary, clusters }` (`summary`: `clusters`, `functions`, `duplicateLines`, `crossWorkspace`; `clusters` and their members exactly as the view returns them, without `nodes`, `edges`, `layoutHint`, `splits`). Text: the totals line `<clusters> groups · <functions> functions · <duplicateLines> duplicate lines · <crossWorkspace> across workspaces`, then per group `<n>. [<tier>] <size> functions, <loc> lines: keep <path>:<line> <name> (<keepReason>)` and one indented `<path>:<line> <name> (<tier> <score>)` per other member; no group adds `No duplicate groups.`. `--workspace` takes a workspace id or package name (repeatable; unknown is an error) and passes them as the view's `workspaces`. `--since <ref>` keeps the groups with a member among the symbols changed in `<ref>...HEAD` (the `reviewDiff` of `gantry review`, not the removed ones) or new or changed in the working layer, and the totals count what is kept; an unknown ref is exit 1. Exit 0 whenever the index exists.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `gantry trace <target> [--up\|--down] [--json]`                                                                          | Page → API → table chain (#47): heals and refreshes like `node`, then prints `trace` (section 9) as an indented chain with its gaps (each step led by `[<repo>]` when the workspace has several repos), or the whole result as JSON                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `gantry recipe check [<file>] [--nodes] [--limit <n>] [--json]`                                                          | Recipe check (#203): validates the recipe (default `.gantry/recipe.json`), runs every rule's examples (each `match` snippet gives at least one match, each `noMatch` none) and counts each rule's matches in this repo. A Go rule (#368) is checked on the Go grammar over the `.go` files of the repo, its snippets wrapped as a file or a function body (section 5, Go rules); `--nodes` lists its endpoints, middleware, tables, reads and writes like any other. Prints per rule `schema ok`, `examples ok` or the failed snippets, the match count, and a warning for 0 matches (a warning, never a failure). Exit 0 when the recipe is valid and every example passes, 1 otherwise. `--nodes` also lists the nodes the index makes from the recipe (#367): the recipe adapter runs through the indexer over the working tree, into a store in memory, with this recipe in place of the one on disk, so the list is what an index run with it stores. It prints a totals line (`N routes, N endpoints, N middleware, N tables, N reads, N writes`), then per kind, in that order and sorted by file, line and label, up to `--limit` entries (default 20 per kind) as `<kind> <label>  <file>:<line>` (a read or write adds `in <symbol>`; a route is a file and has no line), and `... N more <kind> (raise --limit to see them)` when there are more. Counts stay the default summary. `--json` prints `{ rules: [{ id, valid, examples: { passed, failed }, matches, warning? }], errors }`, and with `--nodes` also `nodes: { totals, entries: [{ kind, label, file, line, in? }], limit }`. A second path argument is not accepted: the command prints a usage line (it checks the repository of the current directory, reads one recipe file) and exits 1. Reads the file on disk; writes nothing. |
| `gantry config get\|set <key> [value]`                                                                                   | Project config                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `gantry team login\|link\|status\|logout [options]`                                                                      | Team connection (section 5, "Team config"). `login` signs in with a device code (prints the verification URL and the eight-character code, polls at the server's interval, stores the token and the GitHub login in the credentials file, section 4); `link` writes the `team` block to the project config, then says whether the repo is connected on the server; when that check fails it still exits 0 (warn, never block): it prints the `Linked ...` line, then a warning on stderr (the server cannot be reached, or `team_unauthorized` and to run `gantry team login`); `status` prints `server`, `org`, `user`, `repo` and `reachable` (`--json`: `{ server, org, user, repo, reachable, protocol }`) and exits 0 only when the checkout is linked, signed in and the server answers; `logout` removes the server's entry. A failure prints one line that names its `GantryError` code. Nothing is uploaded.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `gantry team sync-descriptions [--json]`                                                                                 | Download the team's descriptions for the functions this machine has none for (section 11, "Description sync"). Prints `filled <n> of <m> functions without a description` (`--json`: `{ "asked": <m>, "filled": <n> }`), exit 0. A local description is never overwritten, `team.upload.descriptions` does not apply to it, and it never uploads. Exit 1 with `not linked to a team` on stderr when there is no server, organisation or token, or the repo has no `owner/name` name; exit 1 with `Descriptions need a signed-in user. Run gantry team login.` for a CI token or a 403; other server errors print the `GantryError` line (exit 1). A server older than protocol version 2, or one that answers 404 or 405 for the route, gives `filled 0` and the line `server does not support description lookup` on stderr (exit 0).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `gantry team publish\|unpublish view\|group <id> [--json]`                                                               | Send a local saved view or group to the team (`PUT`), or remove it (`DELETE`); section 11, "Shared views and groups" and section 20. The id is looked up among the local views and groups (the overlay's, not the team cache's). Output: `Published <view\|group> <id> to <repo>.` or `Removed <view\|group> <id> from <repo>.`, or with `--json` `{ kind, id, published }` (`published` false after `unpublish`). After a success the team cache is pulled. Exit 0 on success; exit 1 with `Not published: <server message>` or `Not removed: <server message>` on stderr when the server refuses, an `unpublish` finds no such team item (`Not removed: the team has no <kind> <id>.`) or the server cannot be reached; exit 1 with `not linked to a team` when there is no server, organisation or sign-in; exit 2 with `gantry: no local <kind> with the id <id>` when the id is not a local view or group (also when there is no index; none is created). A failed cache refresh after an accepted change is a warning on stderr and exit 0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `gantry team boundaries --base <sha> --head <sha> [--json]`                                                              | List the edges of a pull request that break a team group's `mayUse` (section 11, "Team boundaries command"; section 14, "Team boundaries"). Reads the team's groups with one `GET /v1/pull` and the index as it is (the head checkout, indexed by the caller), and prints one readable line per edge, or with `--json` `{ "crossings": CrossingFinding[], "total": <n> }`. Exit 0 with or without findings; exit 1 with `not linked to a team` when there is no server, organisation or token (resolved by `resolveTeamTarget`, so `GANTRY_SERVER`, `GANTRY_ORG` and `GANTRY_TOKEN` work), and exit 1 with one stderr line when the server cannot be reached or refuses. The token is never printed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `gantry upload [--pr <n> ...] [--close [--merged]] [--descriptions] [--force-main] [--timeout <s>] [--json]`             | Send the graph of `main`, or a pull request's change set, to the team server, or close a change set, or with `--descriptions` send this machine's model-written descriptions, and print the result (section 11, "Upload command" and "Description upload"). With no server and organisation it prints `No team configured; nothing uploaded.` and exits 0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

**Upload payloads (`core/src/team/upload.ts`).** `gantry upload` is the command that sends them; any other command that uploads calls the same functions, so a dry run and a real upload carry the same bytes. See section 11, "Upload payloads" for what each builds and "Upload command" for the command.

**Release (`pnpm release <bump>`, #281).** Bumps the version files, writes `CHANGELOG.md`, commits and tags `v<version>`, then runs `pnpm build`, `packRelease(root, version)` and `packProtocol(root, version)`, which write `dist/gantry-<version>.tgz` and `dist/gantry-<version>.tgz.sha256` (`<hex>  gantry-<version>.tgz`), and `dist/gantry-protocol-<version>.tgz` with `dist/gantry-protocol-<version>.tgz.sha256` (`<hex>  gantry-protocol-<version>.tgz`). It prints the push commands and `gh release create v<version> <tarball> <sha256 file> <protocol tarball> <protocol sha256 file> --notes-from-tag`, and never pushes or uploads. `pnpm release:pack` runs only the pack step for the current version. The archive is a gzip'd ustar with one top directory `gantry/` holding the entries of `plugin/` (`bin/`, `dist/`, `agents/`, `skills/`, `hooks/`, `.claude-plugin/`, `.mcp.json`) directly, plus `LICENSE`, `package.json` (`scripts/release-deps/package.json` with `version` set) and `package-lock.json`; no `node_modules/`. **Protocol tarball:** what `npm pack` gives for `packages/protocol` compiled fresh from `packages/protocol/tsconfig.build.json` (no test files and no test-only support files, such as `src/testing/samples.ts`) into an empty temporary directory, so output a removed source left in `packages/protocol/dist` never ships, with the top directory `package/` and files only: `package.json` (the repo's, with `version` set to the root version, and without `devDependencies`), `LICENSE`, every compiled file under `dist/` except `*.test.*`, test-only support files (`dist/testing/samples.*`; `dist/testing/fakeServer.*` and `dist/testing/index.*`, the exported `./testing` entry point, do ship), source maps and `tsbuildinfo`, and every file under `examples/`. `exports` are `.` and `./testing`; its only dependency is `zod`, at the exact version `pnpm-lock.yaml` resolves, and `packages/protocol/package.json` carries the root version (`checkSync`). It uses the same writer, so it is byte-identical for the same commit too. `scripts/pack-protocol-install.test.ts` packs it with `packProtocol`, extracts it beside the installed `zod` and imports both entry points offline. The CLI tarball is byte-identical for the same commit: entries sorted by path, mode 0644 (0755 for directories and `bin/gantry`), uid and gid 0, empty owner names, mtime the tagged commit's committer time, gzip header mtime 0 and no file name. `scripts/release-deps/package.json` lists the `external` packages of `tsup.config.ts` at exact versions; `pnpm check` fails, naming the package, when one differs from the root `package.json`, and its lockfile is regenerated with `npm install --package-lock-only --ignore-scripts` whenever one changes.

**GitHub Action (`action/`, #282).** A composite Action that users add to a workflow of their own repo (`uses: jbknick/architecture-visualization-cc-plugin/action@v<version>`, documented in `action/README.md`) to get one comment per pull request. Inputs: `version` (required; the release tagged `v<version>`), `sha256` (required; the tarball's checksum), `github-token` (default `github.token`; the workflow needs `pull-requests: write`), `embed` (default `false`; `true` also runs `gantry embed`, which adds the code-vector tier), `base` and `head` (default: the pull request's base and head commits), and the optional `team-server`, `team-org` and `team-token` (default empty; with all three set the Action also uploads, see "Action upload step" in section 11; any later input is named `team-<name>`). Outputs `comment` (`created`, `updated` or `summary`) and `comment-url`. Steps: `actions/setup-node` (Node 26, pinned by commit), install (download `gantry-<version>.tgz` and its `.sha256` from the release by tag, stop unless the download, the input and the `.sha256` file agree, `tar`, `npm ci --omit=dev`; the env var `GANTRY_RELEASE_BASE` replaces the download directory, for trying a build from another place), `gantry init` on the checkout (a fresh data dir under the runner's temp dir), `gantry embed` when asked, then `node dist/action.js`, then the upload step when the team inputs are set. On the `closed` action of a pull request the index, embed and comment steps are skipped (their `if` lines test `github.event.action != 'closed'`), so a close costs the install and one request. The script is `action/src/main.ts`, bundled by tsup as `plugin/dist/action.js` and so carried by the release tarball (`gantry/dist/action.js`); it uses node built-ins and `fetch` only. It runs `gantry review <base>...<head> --json` and `gantry duplicates --since <base> --json`, builds the comment and posts it. No model calls. **Comment:** the body starts with the hidden marker `<!-- gantry:pr-comment -->` and is a summary, never the review JSON: risk and its reasons, the duplicates the pull request adds or changes (`duplicates --since`, section 14), a table of changed symbols with their direct and potential consumer counts, boundary crossings (`### Crossings`, the consumers that sit in another workspace, app or client and server), the team boundaries (`### Team boundaries`, see "Action team boundaries section" in section 11; only with the team inputs), the consumers per change (top 10 direct and top 10 potential, each with `path:line`, the rest counted; above them, when the reachable tier is not empty, the line `Reachable (4+ hops): <n>` (`impact.reachable.total`), and when there are hubs the line `Hubs: <name> (<consumers_total>), ...` with at most 3, then `and <n> more` (#295)), the notes of the review, and a footer naming the duplicate tiers (exact and near; also code-vector with `embed`). When the head commit has no `.gantry/recipe.json` the comment says the radius is structural only. Every list is capped, every line is at most 200 characters, text from the repo appears only inside code spans, and the whole body is at most 60,000 characters (GitHub's limit is 65,536); a body that is still too long loses, in order, consumers, notes, crossings and the changed-symbol table, and then the text is cut, always with a note saying so. **Posting:** the script lists the pull request's comments (following `Link` pages) and updates the first whose body starts with the marker, otherwise creates one, so a pull request never has more than one Gantry comment. A pull request from a fork (its head repo differs from its base repo, or is gone), a run that is not for a pull request, and any 401, 403 or 404 on the comment calls (or a server error that outlasts two retries) never fail the job: the body goes to the job summary and the run ends green. **Fail open (changed in the fix of #282):** the Action never fails the job for a Gantry error. A failed or invalid install input, download, checksum check, extraction, `npm ci`, `gantry init`, `gantry embed` or `gantry review` (non-zero exit, or output that is not JSON) is logged as a `::warning` line, the step exits 0, and the pull request still gets its one comment (found by the same marker and updated in place) headed "the analysis failed" with the reason as one line of at most 200 characters in a code span (text outside `A-Za-z0-9 ._:,;()/=@+-` is replaced by spaces in the shell steps; the script also strips backticks and line breaks). A checksum mismatch is logged as an `::error` line, the tarball is deleted and never extracted or run, and the step still exits 0. The earlier steps pass their reason to the last step through step outputs; the last step runs unless the job was cancelled. When the install failed there is no `dist/action.js`, so that step runs `action/post-failure.sh` from the Action's own checkout (bash, `curl` and `node` only) which builds the same comment text, lists the comments, updates or creates the marked one, and exits 0; when no comment can be posted (a fork, a refused token, no network) the same text goes to the job summary. A duplicate check that fails is reported inside the comment as before. The only failure left is a misconfigured workflow (no base and head to review, or no `GANTRY_BIN`); the comment and embed steps run only when the event carries a pull request (`github.event.pull_request` is set, so `pull_request_review` works) or when the `base` and `head` inputs are both set and never for its `closed` action, so a push to the default branch or a scheduled run, which has nothing to review, runs the install, the index and the upload step and cannot hit it. The Action is meant for the `pull_request` event and never runs the code it indexes. Tests (`pnpm vitest run action`) run the script against a fake GitHub API on `node:http` (`action/src/testkit.ts`) and make no real network call (the install step and `post-failure.sh` run too, against local files and the fake API); a snapshot test renders the comment from the committed review and duplicates pair in `action/testdata/` (made with `pnpm action:e2e --write-pair action/testdata`) and compares it byte for byte with `action/testdata/comment.md`; `pnpm action:e2e` runs the built script twice on a fixture branch and prints `created`, `updated`, `comments: 1`.

**Estimate formula (measured in M0, headless Haiku/low on 200 real functions):** `inputTokens = 950 × toDescribe`, `outputTokens = 250 × toDescribe`, `batches = ceil(toDescribe / 40)`, `rounds = ceil(batches / 4)`. Sequential headless time is about 0.96 s per function (about 62 functions per minute, derived from 60 / 0.96). The worker spike's 125–165 output tokens per function came from short synthetic functions and is not used; the 4-at-a-time wall times were not measured with parallel calls. Describer-subagent (Path B) throughput is not measured; do not print a subagent time estimate until it is (see `docs/spikes/GO-NO-GO.md`).

## 11. Index lifecycle

- **Action upload step (`action/src/upload.ts`, `action/src/upload-main.ts`; bundled as `plugin/dist/upload.js`, so `gantry/dist/upload.js` in the release tarball).** The step `Upload to the team server` (id `upload`) of `action/action.yml` runs after the comment step, only when `team-server`, `team-org` and `team-token` are all non-empty and the install did not fail (`if: ${{ !cancelled() && ... }}`). Event values (`GANTRY_EVENT`, `GANTRY_EVENT_ACTION`, `GANTRY_REF_NAME`, `GANTRY_DEFAULT_BRANCH`, `GANTRY_PR`, `GANTRY_PR_BASE_SHA`, `GANTRY_PR_HEAD_SHA`, `GANTRY_PR_OWNER`, `GANTRY_PR_TITLE`, `GANTRY_PR_HEAD_REF`, `GANTRY_PR_BASE_BRANCH`, `GANTRY_PR_CREATED_AT`, `GANTRY_PR_MERGED`, `GANTRY_HEAD_REPO`, `GANTRY_INDEX_FAILED`) reach the script through the step's environment only, never through shell interpolation; in this step `GANTRY_SERVER`, `GANTRY_ORG` and `GANTRY_TOKEN` are the three inputs (the comment step's `GANTRY_TOKEN` stays the GitHub token). `runUpload(env, { exec, log, cwd })` decides in this order and runs `$GANTRY_HOME/bin/gantry` with the team values and the pull request values (`GANTRY_PR_OWNER`, `GANTRY_PR_TITLE`, `GANTRY_PR_HEAD_REF`, `GANTRY_PR_BASE_BRANCH`, `GANTRY_PR_CREATED_AT`) in the child's environment: (1) a `pull_request` whose `GANTRY_HEAD_REPO` is not `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` (or is empty: a deleted fork) logs `fork: upload skipped` and runs nothing, for every action; (2) a `push` whose `GANTRY_REF_NAME` equals `GANTRY_DEFAULT_BRANCH` and whose `GITHUB_REF` starts with `refs/heads/` runs `gantry upload --force-main` (the Action checks the branch from the event because `origin/HEAD` is usually unset in CI), any other push logs `upload skipped: push to <branch> is not the default branch (<default>)`; (3) `pull_request` `opened`, `synchronize` or `reopened` runs `gantry upload --pr <number> --base <base sha> --head <head sha>`; (4) `closed` runs `gantry upload --pr <number> --head <head sha> --close`, plus `--merged` when `GANTRY_PR_MERGED` is `true`, even when the index failed; (5) anything else logs `upload skipped: pull request action <action> does not upload` (or `upload skipped: event <event> does not upload` for another event; `pull_request_target` never uploads). For (2) and (3) a non-empty `GANTRY_INDEX_FAILED` logs `upload skipped: the index failed` and runs nothing. A non-zero exit of the CLI, a CLI that cannot start or a thrown error logs exactly one `::warning title=Gantry::upload failed: <reason>` line (the CLI's last stderr line, on one line, with the token replaced by `***`), and `upload-main.ts` always exits 0. The token is never an argument and never logged. Tests: `action/src/upload.test.ts` (a fake `exec`), `action/src/action-yml.test.ts` and `action/src/upload.e2e.test.ts` (the built script and the real CLI against the fake protocol server, after `pnpm build`).
- **Team cache pull (`core/src/team/cache.ts`; MCP wiring in `mcp/src/team-pull.ts`).** `pullTeamCache(paths, config, { fetch?, timeoutMs? })` answers `updated`, `unchanged`, `skipped` (no `team` block, no server or no token) or `failed`, with the number of change sets in the cache. It calls `GET /v1/pull?repo=<paths.repo>` (section 20) through `teamClient`, with `If-None-Match` set to `meta.etag`, and follows `next_cursor` until a response has none. A 304 only sets `meta.pulled_at`; a 200 replaces every table except `inflight` (whose rows for change sets that are gone are deleted) in one transaction, so a reader never sees half a pull, and stores the response's `ETag`. The response is parsed with the tolerant schema, so a field this version does not list is ignored. Any failure (no answer within `timeoutMs`, a refused token, an answer that does not match the protocol) leaves the old rows and etag in place and writes the one-line error to `meta.last_error`; the next good pull clears it, and a failed pull never empties the cache. `timeoutMs` (default 5,000 ms) bounds the whole pull, every page included. If the file's `meta.server`, `meta.org` or `meta.repo` differs from the current settings, the file is deleted and recreated before the pull, so one checkout never shows another team's data. Only a file that is not a database or is corrupt (or of another version) is recreated; if another writer holds the lock, the pull waits at most 250 ms, answers `failed`, and leaves the file and its rows alone. A pull follows up to 600 pages. **When it runs.** The MCP server starts one pull when it starts (limit 3,000 ms; the server does not wait for it) and, before a tool call, another when the cache is older than 60 s and no pull was tried in the last 60 s (limit 1,500 ms: the call waits at most that long and goes on with the old cache on a timeout). Two calls 10 s apart therefore make one request. A pull error goes to the MCP log (`mcp.log`) and never into a tool result. `gantry team status --pull` pulls once, then prints the status; `gantry team status` without it never contacts the server for team state. Nothing else pulls, and no hook ever does (section 14).
- **Team cache readers.** `openTeamCache(paths, { readonly: true })` opens the file read-only and answers `undefined` when it is missing, unreadable or of another version; the readers `readChangeSets`, `readChangeSetsTouching(cache, nodeId)` (the change sets whose changed nodes or reach include the node, each with how it touches it), `readTeamGroups`, `readTeamViews`, `readTeamSetting(cache, key)` (one row of `settings`, such as `default_view`), `readTeamRepo(cache)` (`meta.repo`, the repo the cache was pulled for, or `undefined`), `cacheAgeSeconds` and `readCacheSummary` are synchronous, read-only and return empty results for `undefined`. `readChangeSetsTouching` on a cache of 200 change sets of 500 reach rows each, opening included, has a median under 20 ms over 20 runs (`cache.bench.test.ts`). `gantry team status` prints `cache: <n> open changes, pulled <age> ago` (`cache: empty` before the first pull; the age is in seconds, minutes, hours or days) and `--json` adds `cache: { change_sets, pulled_at, age_s, last_error }`; a failed pull shows the old numbers, and its error appears only in `last_error`.
- **Targeted re-index (#360).** A run that names its files (the PostToolUse hook, lazy refresh, heal) writes those files and then re-resolves only the files whose edges can have changed: the importers of what it wrote or deleted (walked on through every importer whose edges did change, so a barrel passes a change on), the files that carry adapter output, and the files whose text ends an import specifier with the name of a file it wrote (an import that was waiting for that file). Its cost follows the files named, not the repository (6,033-file repo: one edited file about 0.9 s, from 2.1 s). A file that is not edited gets a changed edge only through one of those routes or a full index.
- **Precise mode (`core/src/scip/`, `indexer.ts`).** Off unless `precise.scip` is set (section 5) or `gantry index --scip <file>` is given; the user makes the index with their own indexer, and Gantry never runs an indexer or a toolchain. The file is a SCIP index (`scip.proto`: `Index`, `Metadata`, `Document`, `Occurrence`, `SymbolInformation`), read by a protobuf reader written for it (`proto.ts`, `read.ts`; no dependency) as a stream, one document at a time. An occurrence range is `typed_range` (`single_line_range` or `multi_line_range`) when set, else the deprecated `range` list of 3 (`line, start, end`) or 4 (`start line, start, end line, end`) integers; lines and characters are 0-based; only lines place an occurrence, characters are counted by the document's `position_encoding` (UTF-8 bytes, UTF-16 code units or code points) where a document is checked for staleness. The document paths are relative to the root of the repo that is indexed. Each index run (every repo of a workspace reads its own file), including targeted runs and the importers they re-resolve, does this:
  - A document whose path is not an indexed file is skipped. **Stale:** a document is stale, and ignored and counted, when the file is not the one that was indexed. SCIP has no hash of the file; if the document carries its text (`Document.text`, which indexers are not expected to write) the text's sha256 is compared with the file's content hash, and otherwise the document is stale when the working file's modification time is later than the index file's, when a range lies outside the file, or when at the range of a definition of a symbol whose last descriptor is a type, a term or a method (not a local symbol, a parameter, a meta descriptor, `<constructor>` or `default`) the file does not spell the descriptor's name. A stale document keeps the file's inferred edges. The remaining limit is the modification time: a file whose content changed but whose modification time was kept at or before the index's (a copy that preserves times, a clock that went back) is read as unchanged, and a file only touched after the index is read as changed; make the index after the last edit.
  - **Definitions.** A definition occurrence (role `Definition`) of a non-local symbol maps to the innermost Gantry symbol of that file whose lines contain it, when the descriptor's name is the part of the symbol's name after the last dot (`<constructor>` for `constructor`). Several definitions of one SCIP symbol keep the first.
  - **References and edges.** SCIP has no role for a call, so the kind comes from the descriptor of the referenced symbol and the source text at the occurrence (read on the occurrence's line, with no language syntax beyond `(`, `<`, `?.` and `new `). A reference occurrence (any role but `Definition` and `Import`; not a local symbol) to a symbol whose definition maps to a Gantry symbol is an edge from the innermost function, method, component, hook or const that contains it, or from the file (its id as `src`) when none does, as for inferred edges: `confidence: "high"`, `source: "scip"`, no `level`, `count` the number of occurrences and `evidence` `<path>:<line>` of each (capped like other edges). Kind: `renders` when the range directly follows `<` or `</`; else `references_type` when the target is a class, interface, type or enum (not at file level; with `new ` before the range it is `calls`); else `calls`, or `uses_hook` when the target is a hook, when the target is a function (its last descriptor is a method, `name().`, or a term that maps to a function, method, component or hook) and the text after the range, past whitespace, `?.` and a `<...>` type-argument list, is `(`, or `new ` precedes the range. A symbol's reference to itself makes no edge. Any other reference (a read of a value, a function passed along, an import) makes no edge and is counted in `otherRefs`.
  - **Unmapped.** A definition that maps to no symbol, and a reference to a symbol with no definition mapped (a package that is not in the repo, a built-in), are counted in `unmapped`, not stored.
  - **Replacing.** For every file the index covers (a document that is not stale), the inferred edges of kinds `calls`, `renders`, `uses_hook` and `references_type` are dropped and the SCIP edges written in their place; the other kinds (`contains`, `imports`, `exports`, `extends`, `implements`, route and data edges) stay as they were. A file the index does not cover, or whose document is stale, keeps its inferred edges. A run that does not read an index (no flag, no config) writes only inferred edges and the dump has no `source` field.
  - `IndexResult.scip` is `{ documents, edges, unmapped, otherRefs, stale }`, summed over the repos that read an index and absent when none did: `documents` counts the documents that are indexed files (covered or stale), `edges` the SCIP edges written, `unmapped` and `otherRefs` as above, `stale` the ignored documents. The `gantry index` summary prints it as a `scip:` line. When the file is missing the index prints one warning (section 5) and nothing else changes; a file that is not a SCIP index fails the run.
- **Working layer:** updated (a) by the PostToolUse hook for the edited file, (b) by lazy refresh before every MCP call: `git status --porcelain=v1 -z` → re-index changed parseable files whose mtime differs from `files.mtime_ms`, (c) by the explorer's Refresh (M4, #40): there is no file watcher and no push. Every explorer API request first checks for changed files the same way as lazy refresh (`git status` plus mtime; cheap, no re-index), and view responses carry `stale: { files: n }` when files changed since the index was last brought up to date. The web shows an "Out of date (n files)" marker. `POST /api/refresh` (same token, Origin and Host checks as the rest of the API, `Content-Type: application/json`, body ignored) runs heal (only when `HEAD` moved) plus the working-layer re-index of those files and returns `{ changed: { files, functions }, ms, healed, files: [file ids], functions: { added, changed, removed } }` (#40 added the last three: `changed` holds counts, the lists say which ids, "changed" meaning the code's exact hash differs); one refresh runs at a time and a second request gets the same answer. `/api/meta` and `/api/view/:id` carry `stale`; the web fetches `/api/meta` again when its tab gets focus back, so the marker shows after a save in the editor without a watcher. The web's Refresh button calls `/api/refresh` and reloads the current view in place (and the inspector's node, source and overview). Existing nodes keep their positions: flat graphs through the `overlay.layouts` table; the project map (no saved positions, its treemap is derived from the counts) by keeping the previous layout of the same picture (view, focus, level, filters): the workspace boxes as they were, every node's previous treemap weight, and the scene's count-based structure decisions (root-file groups, lifted folders), until the picture changes or Re-layout is pressed. Added nodes are placed inside their parent; added and changed functions (and changed files) get a highlight until the next refresh; removed ones are dropped; the selection stays while its node exists. No model call. Done-when for M4: on a repo of about 1,500 files, Refresh after editing up to 20 files shows the new code in under 2 s, nothing that did not change moves, and the marker appears within one request of a save.
- **Recipe (#203):** each index reads the recipe of the tree it indexes: the main layer reads `.gantry/recipe.json` from `HEAD` (`git show`), so an uncommitted recipe changes nothing in main, and the working layer reads the file on disk. A changed recipe changes the output of every file, which the next full index rewrites; the PostToolUse hook does not watch the recipe file. Review reads each side's recipe from its own ref (`loadSideRecipes`, `core/src/review/recipe.ts`: the merge base for the base side, head for the head side), never from the working tree. Hooks never write `overlay.db` on this path, add no stderr line for a missing recipe, and spend only one file read or one `git show` and the ast-grep passes on files of workspaces a rule applies to.
- **Per-repo heal.** `heal(paths, { repo? })` works on one repo at a time. With `repo` it compares that repo's `repos.head_sha` with its git `HEAD`, re-indexes only that repo's changed files into main (or the whole repo when the diff is large, a manifest changed or nothing is indexed yet) and only its working-tree changes into the working layer; rows of the other repos are never deleted or rewritten. Without `repo` (`gantry heal`, the MCP lazy refresh, the explorer's Refresh, SessionStart) it heals each repo of the workspace in turn, in name order, each scoped the same way; `--from` needs a repo when the workspace has several. `refreshWorking` follows the same rule. The git hooks run in the repo where git fired and pass its name. PostToolUse and Stop find an edited file's repo from its path, refresh only that repo's working layer and settle each repo's files of the batch against that repo (`pending_files.repo`). After a per-repo heal the `similar_to` edges are rebuilt only for pairs with a symbol of that repo (`rebuildSimilarEdges` with `repo`), so pairs between other repos stay as they are. The explorer's staleness count and the "heal due" check cover every repo of the workspace. Hook budgets and fail-open behaviour are unchanged.
- **Main layer / heal:** `repos.head_sha` of the repo the command runs in vs `git rev-parse HEAD` (each repo of a workspace keeps its own head); changed files = `git diff --name-status <old> <new>`; re-index those into main, delete removed, clear their working rows if the working tree now matches. Run by post-checkout, post-merge, post-rewrite, SessionStart and `gantry heal`. A store with another `meta.schema_version` is deleted by `resetStaleStore` (section 9) before the next non-hook command opens it, and that command builds the index again from scratch; hooks never do (section 14).
- **Descriptions:** keyed by contentKey; any symbol whose contentKey has no description goes to the backlog. Never call a model during heal.
- **Embeddings:** filled lazily, never by `init`, heal or a hook, because embedding function bodies is slow compared with indexing (measured on the private test bed: about 140 ms per function after truncation, so about 7 minutes for 3,000 functions, against about 7 s for the whole deterministic index). `gantry embed` (or `gantry init --embed`) stores the missing `vec_descriptions` and `vec_code` rows, descriptions first, then code in backlog-priority order, resumably, in committed batches. Until a vector exists every consumer treats it as "no neighbours" (fail open); a changed contentKey has no vector until the next `gantry embed`.
- **Pre-commit:** read staged blobs only (`git diff --cached --name-only -z` + `git show :<path>`), parse them in memory, run local duplicate + import checks, print findings to stderr (visible to Claude in Bash output) and enqueue new functions. "New or changed" is measured against the main layer, so a copy that PostToolUse already put in the working layer is still reported. Modes `timeboxed`/`strict` additionally run `gantry describe --staged`. Always exit 0.

- **Upload payloads (`core/src/team/upload.ts`).** Two pure functions turn the index into what a team upload sends (section 20 holds the wire format); neither reads a file's text or sends a request. `buildMainSnapshot(store, paths): MainSnapshot` holds, for `paths.repo`: workspaces (`id`, `name`, `path`), files (`id`, `path`, `workspace`, `lang`), symbols (`id`, `name`, `kind`, `lang`, `path`, `workspace`, `line_start`, `line_end`, `exact_hash`, `renamed_hash`, `content_key`), edges (`from`, `to`, `kind`; `similar_to` left out; an edge belongs to the repo of its source, so its target may be an external package such as `ext:react`) and adapter nodes (`id`, `kind`, `workspace`, `label` on one line of at most 200 characters, trimmed and never rewritten: a label with a line break or another control character is sent empty and counted in a warning, `file_id`), plus `sha`, the `HEAD` of the checkout. `workspace` is a workspace id. `content_key` is `contentKey(exact_hash)` for a function-like symbol and null for any other; `renamed_hash` is null when the symbol has none. Every array is sorted by id (edges by `from`, `to`, `kind`), so one index gives one byte string. `buildChangeSet(store, paths, input): Promise<{ changeSet, warnings }>` takes `{ base, head, pr, owner, head_ref, base_branch, title?, created_at?, now? }` (a missing `head_ref` or `base_branch` throws) and builds a pull request's change set from `reviewDiff` and `computeImpact`: `nodes` are the changed symbol ids and changed file ids, sorted; `radius` merges the `direct`, `potential` and `reachable` lists and every hub's `members` of each change's consumers by id at the lowest hop (tier by hop: `direct` 1, `potential` 2 and 3, `reachable` 4 and over), with `via_hub` true for an id found only in hub members and the hub node added as an ordinary entry at its hop minus 1 (at least 1) when no list holds it. The wire cut is 500 entries for hops 1 to 3 and 2,500 for hop 4 and over, with `reachable_total` set when more were found, and `nodes` is cut to 5,000 ids with `nodes_total`. A cut that hides anything returns one line in `warnings` (`radius: sent <sent> of <known>`, `nodes: sent 5000 of <n>`; `buildMainSnapshotChecked` returns the adapter label warning), for the command to print once to stderr. `source` is `pr`, `state` `in_review`, `estimated` false, `links` empty, `ref` `#<n>`, `id` `cs:pr:<repo>#<n>`; `confidence` is never set; `updated_at` is `now` and `created_at` is the given time or `now`. Both results are parsed with the protocol schema before they are returned. `snapshotRecords` and `changeSetRecords` give the NDJSON records in the protocol's order for `splitIntoParts`. `fixtures/team-upload/` holds the committed payloads of `fixtures/pnpm-turbo` (origin `https://github.com/fixture/pnpm-turbo.git`, pull request 1 editing `slugify`) and the first part of the main snapshot. The guard test `upload.guard.test.ts` reads both payloads of `fixtures/pnpm-turbo` and `fixtures/go-basic` and fails on a key named `signature`, `body`, `text`, `code`, `snippet`, `doc`, `content`, `contents`, `fact_sheet` or `factSheet`, a `source` key holding anything but `pr` or `branch`, a string value with a newline or with a line of the fixture's source longer than 40 characters, and any symbol's signature (a short one as a whole field value, any one anywhere in the serialised payload), or a raw multi-line adapter label, occurring in the payload.
- **Upload command (`cli/src/commands/upload.ts`, `gantry upload`).** The one command that sends an index to a team server. It reads nothing from the repo's working tree for its settings: `resolveTeamTarget` (section 5) gives the server, organisation and token. The order is: usage errors (exit 2), then no server or organisation (`No team configured; nothing uploaded.`, exit 0, no request), then no token (`No token. Set GANTRY_TOKEN, or run gantry team login.`, exit 1), then the branch check, then the build. Without `--pr` it uploads the main snapshot and refuses (exit 1) unless the current branch is the one `git symbolic-ref --short refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` names (without `origin/`) or `--force-main` is given; when that command fails or the head is detached it refuses with `Cannot tell whether this is the default branch. Pass --force-main to upload this checkout as main.`, otherwise with `Not on the default branch. Pass --force-main to upload this checkout as main.` It first brings the index to `HEAD` (`heal`, a full index when none exists). `--pr <n>` needs `--base`, `--head`, an owner (`--owner` or `GANTRY_PR_OWNER`), the head branch (`--head-ref` or `GANTRY_PR_HEAD_REF`) and the target branch (`--base-branch` or `GANTRY_PR_BASE_BRANCH`), else it prints `--pr needs --base, --head, --owner, --head-ref and --base-branch` and exits 2; `--title` (`GANTRY_PR_TITLE`) and `--created-at` (`GANTRY_PR_CREATED_AT`, ISO) are left out when neither is given. `--close` needs `--pr` and `--head` (exit 2 otherwise), sends `POST /v1/repos/<repo>/change-sets/<id>/close` with `{ "head_sha": <the head's 40-character sha>, "merged": <--merged> }` and nothing else, and prints `Closed <repo>#<n> (merged).`, `Closed <repo>#<n> (not merged).` or, when the server had nothing to close, `Nothing to close for <repo>#<n>.` (all exit 0). `--merged` without `--close` is a usage error. An upload follows section 20, "Uploading in parts": `serialiseBody(kind, payload)` writes every JSON request body (checked with the route's request schema, compact, keys sorted alphabetically at every depth; kinds `main`, `change_set`, `close`, `descriptions`); the begin request is `uploadStart(records, sha?)`; `buildUploadParts(records, limits, sha?)` returns that request and the gzipped parts cut with `splitIntoParts` to the limits the begin response gave; at most 4 parts are in flight, and a part answered 5xx or by a network error is sent again up to 3 times. A 409 `conflict` stops the upload with the server's message; a 409 `budget` stops it with `Daily upload budget used; try again later.` (exit 1) and leaves the accepted parts to expire. The status is read every 2 s (one line is printed per state change, `queued` or `loading`) until `done` (`done in <s> s`, exit 0), `superseded` (exit 0), `failed` (the server's error, exit 1) or `--timeout` seconds (default 600; exit 1). `--json` prints one object, `{ kind: "main" | "change_set" | "close", status: "done" | "superseded" | "failed" | "closed" | "skipped", upload_id?, error?, seconds }`, and no progress lines. Every exit but a usage error prints exactly one object: `failed` (exit 1) carries `error` (the same text as stderr, token removed) for a refused branch, a missing token, a request the server refused or that did not arrive, an upload that failed or timed out, and an upload that needs more parts than the server takes; `skipped` (exit 0, no `error`) means no server and organisation are configured and nothing was sent (text mode prints `No team configured; nothing uploaded.`); a close that found nothing to close reports `done`. A usage error (exit 2) stays one text line on stderr and prints no object. The cut warnings of the payload builders (`radius: sent <sent> of <known>`, `nodes: sent 5000 of <n>`, the adapter label warning) go to stderr once each. The token is never printed, logged or put in an error: it is removed from any text the server sent back. `X-Gantry-Org` is sent only with a token that does not start with `gct_` (a user token). `gantry upload --descriptions` has its own `--json` object (see "Description upload"). Only the payload builders' records and the two request objects above are ever sent. Nothing a hook starts imports this file, `resolve.ts` or `resolveTeamTarget` (`team.guard.test.ts`, `cache.guard.test.ts`).
- **Team boundaries command (`cli/src/commands/team-boundaries.ts`, `gantry team boundaries`).** `teamBoundaries(ctx, { base, head, json? })` resolves its target with `resolveTeamTarget` (section 5); with no server, organisation or token it prints `not linked to a team` on stderr and exits 1, sending nothing. It makes one request, `GET /v1/pull?repo=<paths.repo>` through `teamClient`, and keeps the `groups` of the answer that parse with `GroupSchema` (a group that does not parse is skipped). It reads `reviewDiff(paths, base, head)` (section 14), takes as changed symbols the changes that have `after`, read with `store.getSymbol`, and as changed files the files with status `A`, `M` or `R`, opens the index as it is (no re-index; a missing index is exit 1 with `no index here: run gantry init first`), and calls `crossingFindings(store, changed, files, { groups, allEdges: true })` (section 14), setting `team: true` on each finding. `allEdges` is used because in a pull request check the index is the head checkout, so a changed function that already breaks the rule is listed, new edge or old. It prints `{ "crossings": [...], "total": <n> }` with `--json`; without it one line per finding, `<src group> -> <dst group>: <name> (<path>:<line>) uses <name> (<path>:<line>)` (an import: `<path> imports <path>`) and `<n> in total.`, or `No team boundary crossings.`. It warns and never blocks: exit 0 with or without findings. A `GantryError` or any other failure is one stderr line and exit 1, with the token removed. No hook imports it.
- **Action team boundaries section (`action/src/run.ts`, `action/src/comment.ts`).** The comment step of `action/action.yml` also receives `GANTRY_TEAM_SERVER`, `GANTRY_TEAM_ORG` and `GANTRY_TEAM_TOKEN` (the three team inputs; the step's `GANTRY_TOKEN` stays the GitHub token). When all three are non-empty and the pull request is not from a fork, `analyse()` runs `gantry team boundaries --base <base> --head <head> --json` after the duplicate check, with a child environment that holds the step's environment without the three `GANTRY_TEAM_*` values and with `GANTRY_SERVER`, `GANTRY_ORG` and `GANTRY_TOKEN` set from them (so the team token is the child's `GANTRY_TOKEN`, the GitHub token is not in that environment, and the other children and the GitHub client never get the team token). An entry of the answer that lacks a string group, name or path, or a numeric line, is dropped. A non-zero exit, an answer that is not JSON or that has no `crossings` logs exactly one `::warning title=Gantry::team boundaries unavailable: <reason>` line (one line, at most 300 characters, the token removed), leaves the section out and does not turn the comment into the failure comment. `buildComment` takes `teamBoundaries?: { crossings, total } | null`. Its section `### Team boundaries (<pairs>)` sits between `### Crossings` and `### Consumers`; it is separate from `Crossings`, which is unchanged. Findings are grouped by `(srcGroup, dstGroup)`, sorted by source group name then destination group name (code-point order); each pair is one list item of the form ``- `<src>` → `<dst>`: `<function>` uses `<function>`, ... (<n> edge[s] in total)`` with up to 3 `uses` items (an import, which has `srcLine` 0, shows its two file paths), up to 10 pairs, then `- and <n> more`. Every name is written through the comment's code-span escaping (backticks, line breaks and control characters removed, cut to 80 characters), because group names come from the team and symbol names from the repository. When the comment is too long the section is dropped after the consumers and notes and before `Crossings`. With no team values, or `teamBoundaries` null or empty, no section is written and the body is byte-identical to the one without this feature.
- **Description upload (`core/src/team/descriptionUpload.ts`, `gantry upload --descriptions`).** `uploadDescriptions(store, client, repo, { serialise? }): Promise<{ sent, stored, skipped }>` reads every row of the local `descriptions` table whose `provenance` is `model` and whose `model` is not null (`fallback` and `template` rows are rebuilt on every machine and never sent), ordered by `content_key`, and maps each to the wire `Description` field by field: `search_terms` is the parsed JSON array, `name_fits` is true when the column is 1, `suggested_name` is left out when null, and `content_key`, `behavior`, `provenance`, `model`, `prompt_version` and `created_at` (the local record's ISO 8601 UTC time) are copied, so a description stored by the server equals the local row in all nine fields. A row that the protocol schema would refuse is not sent and is counted in `skipped` (the command prints one stderr line with the count). Batches of 500 go to `POST /v1/descriptions`, each body written by `serialiseBody("descriptions", { repo, descriptions })` (the command passes it as `serialise`; the default checks the payload with the route's schema and writes compact JSON), and `stored` is the sum of the answers. The command resolves its target like `gantry upload`, with this order: `--descriptions` with `--pr` or `--close` is a usage error (exit 2, `--descriptions cannot be combined with --pr or --close`); no server and organisation prints `No team configured; nothing uploaded.` (exit 0); `team.upload.descriptions` false prints `Description upload is switched off (team.upload.descriptions).` (exit 0, no request); no token is exit 1; a token that starts with `gct_`, or a 403 from the route, prints `Descriptions need a signed-in user. Run gantry team login.` (exit 1; a CI token never sends description text); a 404 prints `The server does not accept descriptions.` (exit 1); other server errors print the `GantryError` line (exit 1). It prints `Uploading <n> descriptions for <repo>...` and `stored <k> new`, or under `--json` exactly one object on every exit but a usage error (no progress lines): `{ "status": "done" | "skipped" | "failed", "sent": <n>, "stored": <k>, "error"?: <text>, "reason"?: <text> }`. `skipped` (exit 0, zero counts) carries `reason` `no team`, `switched off` or `nothing to send`; `failed` (exit 1) carries `error`, the same text as stderr with the token removed, and `sent` and `stored` count the batches the server had already answered, so a later batch that fails keeps what was stored; `done` has the totals. It builds no graph, does not re-index, and reads no source: only the `descriptions` table. The repo name is `paths.repo`. The token is removed from any text printed. No hook imports `descriptionUpload.ts` (`team.guard.test.ts`).
- **Description sync (`core/src/team/descriptionSync.ts`, `gantry team sync-descriptions`, the MCP server's start-up).** The download side of the description upload: a function described on one machine of the team gets that description on another without running the describer again. `syncDescriptions(store, client, repo, { limit?, timeoutMs?, log? }): Promise<{ asked, filled, dropped }>` selects the effective symbols of `FUNCTION_LIKE_KINDS` that have no row in the local `descriptions` table (not those in a sensitive path, and not a content key the wire format refuses), in id order, one per `content_key`, up to `limit`, and asks `POST /v1/descriptions/lookup` (section 20) in batches of 500 keys; the request carries the repo name and content keys, never source or a signature. It awaits `client.ready()` first and sends nothing, logs `server does not support description lookup` and returns `filled: 0` when `supports(client.serverProtocol(), "POST /v1/descriptions/lookup")` is false, and does the same on a 404 or 405 from the route; any other server error rejects, leaving what earlier batches stored. The response is read tolerantly: its records are `unknown` to the schema and each is checked alone against the description shape (an unknown key is ignored), and a record that does not fit is dropped and counted in `dropped` (one line in the log), never failing the batch. A returned record is used only for a key that was asked, once, and only when its `provenance` is `model` and its `model` is not null (what the upload sends; a fallback or template description is rebuilt here and must not end a pending describe); it is dropped (and not counted as filled) when its `prompt_version` is not `PROMPT_MAJOR` or `validateDescription` refuses it against a fact sheet built from the local source the way `submitDescriptions` builds it (so the server is trusted no further than a model).
