An accepted record is stored with `storeDescriptions` without `replace`, with its `model`, `provenance` and `created_at` (`AcceptedDescription.createdAt`, default now), so the local row equals the original in all nine fields; a local description is never overwritten. `timeoutMs` bounds the whole run: on expiry it rejects and keeps what was stored. `syncTeamDescriptions(paths, config, { env, fetch?, limit?, timeoutMs?, log? })` resolves the team like `gantry upload` (`resolveTeamTarget`), answers `not_linked` (no server, organisation or token, or a repo with no `owner/name` name) or `ci_token` (a `gct_` token cannot read descriptions) without a request, and with no local index asks nothing; it is the one call the command and the server make. **When it runs.** `gantry team sync-descriptions` runs it with no limit. The MCP server runs it once after the start-up pull (section 11, "Team cache pull"), with `limit: 2000` and `timeoutMs: 5000`, apart from the pull and from every call: the server and the calls never wait for it, and a failure or a timeout is written to the MCP log and ignored, never into a tool result. `gantry init` runs it too, after the index is built and before the backlog is built (section 10), with the same limit and timeout, so a fresh clone is handed to the describer without the functions the team has already described; with no team, no sign-in or an unreachable server it does nothing, and a failure is one stderr line (`gantry: description sync skipped: <reason>`) and init carries on. Nothing else runs it and no hook does (section 14). **Trust.** A synced description is text from a server: it is stored as text, never executed and never rendered as HTML, and it is served only through the `untrusted_description` fields (section 15), like one written here. `team.upload.descriptions: false` does not stop the sync: that key governs upload only. Descriptions belong to one organisation (the `X-Gantry-Org` header) and are never shared across organisations.
- **Review diff (#42, `core/review/diff.ts`).** `reviewDiff(paths, base, head)` compares the merge base of the two refs with `head`; it reads the changed indexable files' blobs with `git show`, parses and hashes them in memory, and opens no store and writes nothing (not the main layer, not the working tree). The file list is `git diff --name-status -z -M`, filtered with `isIndexable` on either path; a file over `MAX_FILE_BYTES` on either side is skipped. Symbols match by id first (`modified` when `exactHash`, signature or export status differs; unchanged symbols are not listed). Leftover removed and added symbols are paired by `exactHash`, then by `renamedHash` (own name masked), preferring the same file: same file is `renamed`, another file is `moved`; inside a renamed file, leftovers with the same name are `moved`. `id` of a change is the after id (the before id for `removed`). Each side (`SymbolSide`) also carries `kind`, `exported` and `complexity` (added in #43; impact and risk read them from the diff because the store may sit on another commit). `signatureChanged` compares the whitespace-collapsed signature text and `exportedChanged` the export flag, both only when both sides exist; `locDelta` is lines after minus lines before. Import changes compare module specifier sets per file. `base` and `head` echo the arguments; `mergeBase` is the commit sha. Everything is sorted (changes by id then kind, files and import changes by path), so the JSON is identical across runs and data dirs (no timing field). A copied file (`C`) counts as added; git type changes count as modified. `gantry review <base>...<head> --json` (also `..`) and `--pr <n> --json` print the result on stdout and nothing else; since #44 the JSON also carries `impact` (the `Impact` of the section below, from `reviewImpact` on the index as it is: no heal, no re-index, nothing created; `null` when the repository has no index), because `/gantry:review` grounds a review in it. Without `--json` the command prints the text summary of the impact section below (#43). `--open` (#44) opens the review view (below) with the same start-or-reuse logic as `gantry_open_view` (`ensureExplorer` in `explorer/server/launch.ts`) and prints the tokenised URL as a last line `Review view: <url>` (on stderr with `--json`, so stdout stays JSON). `--pr` runs `gh pr view <n> --json baseRefName,headRefOid`, fetches `origin/<base>` and `pull/<n>/head` when missing (writes under `.git` only), and without a working `gh` fails with a message that names the range form. Done-when: a 20-file diff in under 3 s (measured about a second or less for 20 files and 200 functions).
- **Impact and risk (#43, consumers since #169; `core/review/impact.ts`, `consumers.ts`, `risk.ts`).** `computeImpact(store, changes, { maxHops: 3, files? })` is synchronous and reads only the store's effective graph (main plus working layer). Seeds are the ids (after and before) of every change that is not `added`. **Consumer edge kinds** (`CONSUMER_KINDS`): `calls`, `renders`, `uses_hook`, `extends`, `implements`, `references_type` and the route edges `serves`, `handles`, `requests` where an adapter indexed them. **Hop 1** is the union of every seed change's _direct consumers_ (`directConsumers`): the sources of incoming consumer edges to either id of the change, plus, when the change is exported (for a removed, renamed or moved symbol: exported before, and its base-side file; otherwise exported on either side, and its head-side file; never for `added`), the files that import its file, except an importer file one of whose symbols is already a direct consumer (then the index can tell which code uses it, and the file is not listed twice). Hops 2 and 3 follow the same consumer edge kinds, and a `file:` node (a module-level call site, an importer) continues through the files that import it. Decision (#169): the walk and the risk follow the new kinds from hop 1 on, as the issue recommended; before #169 only `calls`, `renders` and `uses_hook` were walked and only removed, renamed or moved exports counted their importer files. The risk rules keep their meaning: "direct callers" counts the distinct hop-1 nodes, which are now every direct consumer (so a barrel or a test file that imports a changed export counts). Each node is recorded at the first hop it appears at, ids sorted within a hop, seeds excluded; `callersByHop` stops at 3 hops or 500 callers (a note says so). **Reachable tier (#293).** Whether or not that walk stopped at 500 callers, `computeImpact` goes on (a walk of its own, from the hop-1 consumers, so a stop of the first tiers does not shorten it) over the same edge kinds (a `file:` node also through its importers) at hops 4, 5, and so on until nothing new is reached or the walk holds `REACHABLE_NODE_CAP` = 2500 nodes (seeds excluded, all hops counted, a hop's new nodes taken in sorted id order); on the cap it adds the note `The reachable walk stopped at 2500 nodes; deeper consumers are not listed.` The cap and the descending fan-in order are the values of the blast-depth spike (`docs/spikes/blast-depth.md`, "Decisions"). `Impact.reachable` counts the nodes first reached at hop 4 or later, per hop; `callersByHop` stays at hops 1 to 3. The walk reads the graph from memory: one `effectiveEdges` query loads every consumer edge (`ConsumerGraph.preload`), because one query per node rebuilds the effective-files view each time and takes minutes on a large repo; the answers are those of the per-node query, in the same order. `ImpactOptions.reachable` (default true) and `reachableCap` turn it off or lower the cap; with `reachable: false` nothing else in the output changes. Risk is unchanged: nothing reachable feeds `computeRisk`. `Impact = { changed, callersByHop: [{hop, ids}], crossings: [{from, to, kind}], risk: {score, level, reasons}, notes, consumers }`. **Consumers (#169)** frame the blast radius per change in two tiers. `consumers` has one entry per changed symbol (sorted by id; `added` ones too, by symbol edges only) and then one per changed file (`files`, sorted by path; `reviewImpact` passes the diff's files): `{ id (the change id or file:<path>), target: "symbol"|"file", direct, directTotal, potential, potentialTotal }`. **Directly affected** (`direct`, hop 1): everything that uses the changed item itself, as above; for a changed file, the files that import it. **Potentially affected** (`potential`): the consumers of those, hops 2 to 3, same edge kinds (a file through its importers), excluding the change itself and its direct consumers, each kept at its first hop with the direct consumer it reaches the change through (`via`) and the node it uses one step nearer (`next`; one path is enough, the first in sorted order); that walk stops after 500 nodes. Each entry is `{ id, name, kind (symbol kind, file, or the adapter node kind such as route), path, line (null for a file or adapter node), workspace (name or null), hop, uses (edge kinds to the change, or to next, sorted; imports for an importer file), crosses (the crossing kinds below, between the consumer's file and the changed item's, sorted), via?, next? }`: names and paths only, never descriptions. `direct` and `potential` are sorted by workspace (none last), then path, then name, then id, and capped at 200 entries (`MAX_CONSUMERS`) with the totals uncapped. **Reachable consumers** (`reachable`, `reachableTotal`) come from the same walk without a hop limit, per change, from that change's direct consumers (also when the potential tier hit its 500-node stop); entries are at hop 4 or later with the same `via`, `next` and `uses` rules and carry `pathConfidence` and `fanIn`. `pathConfidence` of a direct consumer is `high` when some edge from it to the change is `high` (an importer file counts as `high`); of a node at hop h it is `high` when a predecessor at hop h-1 with `high` path confidence reaches it through a `high` edge, else `low`. `fanIn` is the number of distinct sources of its incoming consumer edges (`CONSUMER_KINDS`, plus `imports` for a `file:` node, without `ext:` sources and self edges). The tier is ranked by `compareReachable`: hop, then `high` before `low`, then `fanIn` descending (`FAN_IN_ORDER`), then workspace, path, name, id. It is capped at 200 entries per hop (not per tier, so a wide hop cannot hide the hops after it), with `reachableTotal` uncapped. **Hubs collapse (#294).** A node is a hub when its fan-in (as defined above) is above `HUB_THRESHOLD` = 608, the `hub_threshold` of the blast-depth spike (`docs/spikes/blast-depth.md`, "Decisions"; fan-in as `fan_in_definition` there). The walk goes through a hub as through any node, but in the `potential` and `reachable` tiers (hop 2 and later) a consumer whose `next` is a hub is not listed: it is counted in that hub's `HubEntry` (`ChangeConsumers.hubs`) instead, and the walk goes on from it, so its own consumers are listed normally or collapsed under another hub. The `direct` tier never collapses (it lists the change's own consumers and the risk rule counts them), and a hub stays listed in its own tier. `potentialTotal` and `reachableTotal` keep counting every distinct consumer of their hops, collapsed ones included, so the totals do not change; the lists hold the rest (the reachable cap of 200 per hop applies to what is left). `HubEntry.consumersTotal` counts the consumers first reached through the hub (those within the potential walk's 500-node stop or the reachable walk's node cap), `members` lists up to 200 of them in `compareReachable` order (entries of the potential tier carry no `pathConfidence` or `fanIn`, so the order is that of `compareConsumers`). `hubs` is sorted by hop, then `consumersTotal` descending, then hub id. `Impact.hubs` has every hub any change's walk collapsed, `consumersTotal` the largest over the changes, sorted by `consumersTotal` descending, then hub id. With `reachable: false` the reachable tier and its hubs are empty. No risk input reads hubs. **Crossings** are judged between a changed symbol and its direct consumers, one entry per kind, sorted (`from` is the change id): `app` when the caller is in a workspace of kind `app` and the changed symbol in one of kind `package`, otherwise `workspace` when the workspaces differ, and `client_server` when exactly one of the two files starts with `"use client"`. **Risk** is the sum, capped at 100, of the rules in the exported `RISK_RULES` table: signature changed on an exported symbol +30; removed exported symbol that still has callers or importers +30; distinct hop-1 callers ×2 (max 20); crossings ×5 (max 20); a changed (not added) function with complexity above 15 +10; a changed function whose file no test file (`*.test.*`, `*.spec.*`) imports +10 (each flat rule counts once per review). Levels: below 30 low, below 60 medium, else high. `reasons` has one line per rule that fired (`+30 signature changed on an exported symbol`). **Notes:** a removed export still used (names up to three users); changed symbols the index does not hold (the store should be at the base for callers of removed code to exist; a store at the head lacks them, and the note says so); and, from `reviewImpact(paths, store, diff)` (async, reads head blobs with `git show`), new imports no workspace declares (`checkImports`, only for specifiers the diff added) and added functions with an exact or near duplicate (`findDuplicates`, tiers `exact` and `near`). The issue's contract has `computeImpact` return `Impact` synchronously, which stays true; the head-blob notes need the async `reviewImpact`. Output is deterministic. `gantry review <range>` without `--json` prints: the range and counts, `Risk: <level> (<score>/100)` with the reasons, callers per hop (hop 1 lists up to three ids), crossings (first five), then for each change with consumers (at most 20, the rest are in `--json`) `Consumers of <id>:` with `Directly affected (<n>):` (top five, `name (kind, path:line) uses; crosses ...`) and `Potentially affected (<n>):` (top three, `... hop <h> via <name>`), then (#295) `Reachable (<n>):` (top three, `... hop <h> via <name>`, `and <n> more`) when the change has reachable consumers and `Hubs (<k>):` with up to three lines `<name> (<kind>, <path>): <n> consumers at hop <h> in <w> workspace(s)` (`and <n> more`) when it has hubs, a line `  reachable (hop 4+): <total>` after the hop lines of the callers when the reachable tier is not empty (a change is listed when its direct, potential or reachable total is above 0), and notes; `--json` prints `impact.consumers` in full (both tiers, capped at 200 each); it runs `heal` and `refreshWorking` first, so the index is the checked-out tree. `gantry_impact` (section 15) shapes the same data for MCP.
- **Re-attaching hand-picked entries (#288; `core/src/overlay/reattach.ts`).** `reattachOverlay(store): { reattached: { from, to }[], unresolved: UnresolvedEntry[] }` looks at every anchored id (section 9) that is not in the effective graph and finds the effective function-like symbols (`function`, `method`, `component`, `hook`) with the same `exactHash`. Exactly one, and its id not already held by the same entry: the old id is rewritten to the new one everywhere it occurs (label row, selector JSON, anchor row) in one transaction. Zero or several: nothing changes. It matches by `exactHash` first; since `exactHash` includes the symbol's own name, a move or a file rename with an unchanged body re-attaches by it. When no symbol has the anchored `exactHash` (#349), it looks for effective function-like symbols in the same file and of the same kind family with the anchored `renamedHash` (`overlay.anchor_renames`), the same body under another name, and applies the same rule: exactly one and not already in the entry. The kind families are those of section 17 (`function` and `method` together, `component`, `hook`), compared with the family stored in `overlay.anchor_families`; a function renamed to `use[A-Z]…` (a hook) or to a PascalCase component with the same body is another family and stays unresolved (`gone`). Same file only, because a `renamedHash` also matches a copy written elsewhere under another name; a function renamed and moved at once stays unresolved. Several `exactHash` matches stay ambiguous without trying `renamedHash`. It writes only when an id changes, so an index with no moved symbol leaves `overlay.db` byte-identical. It runs only where the caller asks for it (`heal(paths, { reattach: true })`, `refreshWorking(paths, { reattach: true })`, both off by default): `gantry heal`, `gantry index`, `gantry init`, `rebuildIndex` (the `map` command), the MCP lazy refresh and `POST /api/refresh`. It runs on every such call, also when the heal or refresh itself changed nothing (a hook may have healed the commit first, without re-attaching); it writes only when an id changes. No hook entry point (SessionStart, the git hooks `post-checkout`, `post-merge`, `post-rewrite` and `pre-push`, PostToolUse, Stop) sets it, so a hook never writes `overlay.db`; the lookups (`search`, `node`, `trace`) do not set it either, and the next call above re-attaches. `UnresolvedEntry = { entry: "label" | "hidden" | "group" | "view", id, groupId?, viewId?, text?, reason: "gone" | "ambiguous" }`, sorted by entry, then id; `ambiguous` when the anchored hash (`exactHash`, else `renamedHash` in the same file) matches several symbols (or the only match is already in that entry), `gone` otherwise. `listUnresolved(store)` computes it without writing. Entries are never dropped for being unresolved.
- **Overlay at view time (#286; `explorer/server/overlay.ts`, `api.ts`).** Every `/api/view/:id` response is passed through `applyOverlay(ctx, result, state, spec?)` after the view is built, and carries `overlay: { hidden: number, groups: { id, name, members: string[] }[] }`. Labels: a node whose id has a label gets `label = text` and `labelOriginal = <previous label>` (`GraphNode.labelOriginal?`). Hidden: nodes matching the global hidden list or `spec.hidden` are removed with their descendants (by `parent`) and every edge with a removed end; `overlay.hidden` is the removed node count. Groups: for each group (all, or those in `spec.groups` when non-empty), `members` are the ids of response nodes matching its selectors, sorted; groups are sorted by id. Selectors see the store's path and workspace of a node, never its id's text. **Saved views:** `GET /api/view/:id?saved=<viewId>` applies a saved spec; an unknown id answers 404 `Unknown saved view: <id>`, a spec for another view 400. The spec's `level`, `focus` and `filters` are the defaults for the view's parameters and query parameters given explicitly win; the same saved view and index always give the same response bytes. **Routes** (same Host, Origin and token checks as the rest of the API; writes need `Content-Type: application/json` and a body of at most 64 KiB, else 413; a validation failure answers 400 `{ error: "<path>: <zod message>" }` from the first issue; `DELETE` is allowed only on the two `DELETE` routes, every other method and path keeps answering 405 with `allow: GET, HEAD`):

  | Method and path                  | Body                                                               | Response                      |
  | -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------- |
  | `GET /api/overlay`               | none                                                               | `OverlayState` + `unresolved` |
  | `PUT /api/overlay/labels`        | `Label` (the node must exist in the effective graph, else 404)     | `{ labels: Label[] }`         |
  | `PUT /api/overlay/hidden`        | `{ selectors: Selector[] }`                                        | `{ hidden: Selector[] }`      |
  | `PUT /api/overlay/groups/:id`    | `Group` without `id`                                               | `{ group: Group }`            |
  | `DELETE /api/overlay/groups/:id` | none                                                               | `{ deleted: id }` or 404      |
  | `PUT /api/overlay/views/:id`     | `{ name, spec }` (`spec.view` must be a registered view, else 400) | `{ view: SavedView }`         |
  | `DELETE /api/overlay/views/:id`  | none                                                               | `{ deleted: id }` or 404      |
  | `POST /api/overlay/reset`        | `{}`                                                               | `OverlayState` (empty)        |
- **Source slices (`GET /api/source`).** Query `path` (repo-relative), `start`, `end` (at most 400 lines), and optional `repo`, the name of a repo of the workspace: the file is then looked up among that repo's indexed files and read from the root the repo was indexed from (`repos.root`); without `repo` it is the repo the explorer runs in. An unknown `repo` is 404; the `..`, absolute-path, sensitive-file, not-indexed and symlink checks are the same for every repo.
- **Project map of several repos (`explorer/server/views/projectMap.ts`, `hierarchy.ts`, web `canvas/scene.ts`, `canvas/mapLayout.ts`, `canvas/mapDraw.ts`, `views/ProjectMapPanel.tsx`).** When the store holds more than one repo, the `project-map` view adds a top tier above the workspaces: a node `repo:<name>` (kind `repo`, label the repo name, `stats` summed from its workspaces) that is the `parent` of every workspace of that repo, and, in such a store, directory ids carry the repo (`dir:<repo>:<path>`; a store of one repo keeps `dir:<path>`), and a level `repos` before `workspaces` that returns the repo nodes only. The view option `repos=<name>|<name>` keeps the files of those repos, like `workspaces`. An edge whose two ends are in different repos carries `crossRepo: true`; edges between workspaces of different repos stay workspace to workspace at every level below `repos` and aggregate up to repo to repo edges at `repos`. Edges between repos come from resolved imports. `/api/meta` has `repos`, the sorted repo names (one name for a workspace of one). A store of one repo has no `repo` node, no `repos` level (`level=repos` is a 400), no `crossRepo` flag and answers exactly as before. The canvas draws each repo as a labelled container around the boxes of its workspaces (the layered layout places them inside a compound node), cross-repo links dashed, and the Repos level as one box per repo; the left panel's detail control gains "Repos" and a list of repos to filter by only when `/api/meta` has more than one repo.
- **Team layer (`explorer/server/team.ts`; web `views/TeamLayerToggle.tsx`, `TeamLayerPanel.tsx`, `TeamLayerRings.tsx`, `teamStore.ts`, `teamModel.ts`).** `GET /api/team/layer` returns `TeamLayerResponse` (section 7). It opens `team-cache.db` read-only through the cache readers (`readChangeSetDetails`, `readCacheRepo`, `cacheAgeSeconds`) and never calls a team server or `fetch`; change sets are sorted by `id` and `people` is sorted and unique. It answers `available: false` with empty lists and `age_s: null` when the file is absent, unreadable, of another version, or was pulled for another repo than the explorer's (`meta.repo` differs from `paths.repo`). The web app reads it each time the project map appears, after every Refresh of the top bar (`POST /api/refresh`, so a pull made since the page loaded shows the toggle) and on the panel's own `Refresh`, which only re-reads the cache; the newest read wins. If the cache has gone, or now belongs to another repo, the answer is `available: false` and the toggle disappears and the layer turns off. The `Team` toggle (a pressed-state button at the start of the map toolbar, `TreemapCanvas.tsx`) exists only when `available` is true; the layer is off by default and its state (on, picked change set, ticked people) lives in the session only. With the layer on, the canvas draws a marker (owners' initials and a count) on the top-right corner of each workspace box in `map_nodes` of a visible change set, and a docked panel lists the visible change sets, with the freshness line `Team data pulled <age> ago` (muted warning colour past 24 hours), the `People` checkbox group and `No open pull requests in the team data` when there are none. Picking a change set hides the markers and paints the change set on the map through the review layer's paint (changed nodes `modified`, hop 1 direct, hops 2 and 3 potential; a symbol also marks its file), with a solid line from each directly affected tile to the changed nodes it uses (`direct_uses`; between the tiles the map has, the symbols' or else their files'; an entry with no `direct_uses` row gets no line; at most 100 lines), and a dashed outline on each reachable tile (hop 4 and over) and on each tile reached through a hub (`via_hub`) in any tier, which keeps its tier's tint. A through-hub tile carries `through hub <name>` when exactly one non-hub entry sits one hop nearer (it is the hub, listed as an ordinary entry), else `through a hub`. The review legend (`Reachable, or through a hub` and `Solid: uses the change` rows) is docked in the picked row of the panel, so it never covers a label on the map. The change set's blast radius opens as rings (`TeamLayerRings`: one ring per tier, one dot per node up to 18, a `via_hub` entry of any tier a hollow dot) and its changed code as a list; a row selects the node in the inspector. One definition of changed code serves the header, the list and the hint: every changed symbol, plus each changed file with no listed symbol; when `nodes_total` exceeds the 200 ids sent the count is a floor (`N+`) and the hint says more are in the team data. When `radius_total` exceeds the 500 entries sent, the tier of the last entry sent and every later tier show a `+` (a floor). The freshness line adds the time since the data was read to `age_s` and is worked out again every minute, so it turns to the warning style past 24 hours without a Refresh. Closing the panel returns focus to the `Team` toggle. Each person has one colour from the workspace palette (their place in the sorted `people`), used for their initial in the filter, the rows and the markers, with the letter pulled toward the text colour until it reads at 4.5:1 on its chip in both themes. The largest-files markers are not drawn while the layer is on. Animations (panel 200 ms, markers 150 ms in and out, staggered 20 ms in, rings 240 ms) honour `prefers-reduced-motion`. Tests: `explorer/server/team.test.ts`, `web/src/views/team.test.tsx`, `web/e2e/team-layer.spec.ts`; the cache is seeded from `fixtures/team-changesets/change-sets.json` by `seedTeamCache(dbPath, file)` (`core/src/team/testing.ts`), and the mock's `scenario=team` serves three synthetic change sets by two people.
- **Curation controls (#287; web `components/CurationActions.tsx`, `CurationPanel.tsx`, `SavedViews.tsx`, `NameDialog.tsx`, `curation.ts`, `api.ts`, `store.tsx`).** The web holds the overlay (`GET /api/overlay`) in its store and loads it again after every write and after Refresh; a write is followed by a fetch of the open view. **Hash key `saved`** (the saved view id): `parseHash` and `formatHash` round-trip it, it is not a filter, and it goes to `/api/view/:id` as the query parameter `saved`. The inspector, on any node, has **Rename** (an inline `Label` field of at most 80 characters; Enter saves through `PUT /api/overlay/labels`, an empty value removes the label, and the inspector shows the original name under the new one), **Hide** (appends `{ type: "ids", ids: [<node id>] }` to the hidden list through `PUT /api/overlay/hidden`) and a `Group` combobox (the existing groups, then `New group…`). Choosing a group appends the node id to that group's first `ids` selector, or adds an `ids` selector when it has none. `New group…` asks for a name; the id is the slug of the name (`a-z0-9-`, at most 63 characters), a taken id gets `-2`, `-3`, and the new group is created with one `ids` selector holding the current node (a group needs at least one selector) and `mayUse: null`. The left panel's `Curation` section lists labels (node, text, remove), hidden entries (`Show again` removes that entry) and groups (name, `overlay.groups[].members` count of the open view, rename, delete). Hovering a group sets the "Largest files" hover (`rankView.setHoverFile`) to the group's first file member: the canvases draw one highlighted file, so only one member is marked. The top bar's `Saved view` combobox lists the saved views by name, then `Save view…` (dialog `Save view`, textbox `Name`; saves `{ view, level, focus, filters }` of the current route with `groups: []` and `hidden: []` under the slug of the name) and `Reset curation…` (confirm dialog, then `POST /api/overlay/reset`); choosing a saved view navigates to `#/<spec.view>?saved=<id>`. The two comboboxes are native `<select>` elements of the kit's `native-select`, and the dialogs are SmoothUI's `dialog`; every animation honours `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- **Review view (#44; on the map since #167; `explorer/server/views/review.ts`, `reviewView.ts`, web `views/Review*.tsx`).** View id `review`, hint `review`. Params (view options) `range` (`<base>...<head>`, at most 300 chars) or `pr` (a pull request number, resolved with `resolvePullRequest`); both is a 400, neither returns `{ nodes: [], edges: [], review: null }` and the web asks for a range. It runs `reviewDiff` and `reviewImpact` on the commits `reviewCommits(paths, base, head)` resolves (read-only: git blobs and the index as it is, no re-index) and caches the result in memory per (data dir, merge base, head sha), at most 8 entries, dropped when the index changes (`meta.head_sha` or `store.generation()`, which also moves on commits by other processes such as the hooks and the MCP server) and on every `POST /api/refresh`; a `pr` is resolved with `gh` at most once a minute per pull request (cleared by Refresh too). `gantry review --open` waits up to 10 s for a fresh explorer; `gantry_open_view` keeps 2.5 s and its timeout message says to retry in a few seconds. A bad or unknown ref, or a failing `gh`, is a 400 with the message. **Mode `map` (default): the review as a layer on the project map.** The response's nodes and edges are exactly the project map's for the same level and filters (`focus` is ignored: in the review it is the selected change), so every node sits where it sits on the plain map (tested: same nodes, edges and laid-out positions); `map=3d` draws on the 3D map's settlements instead and uses the Files level. `review.layer` = `{ marks, links, linksTotal, placed, unplaced }`, every id a node of the response: a mark `{ id, change?, changes?, hop? }` puts each changed symbol on the deepest node that shows it (either side's id, else either side's file, so a removed symbol shows at its base-side file while that file is in the index, otherwise it is in `unplaced` and only listed), and each changed file of the diff on its node; the kind is `added`, `modified`, `signature` (modified with a signature change), `moved` (renamed or moved) or `removed`, and on a shared node the first of removed, signature, moved, modified, added wins. Nodes without a change that hold a caller get the nearest `hop` (1 to 3). A link `{ id: src->dst, src, dst, change, weight, tier, hop }` is one of two tiers. `tier: "direct"` (`hop` 1) joins the node of each direct consumer (`directConsumers`, the impact walk's hop 1) to the node of the changed symbol. `tier: "potential"` (#173; `hop` 2 or 3, the nearest of the consumers it stands for) joins the node of each potentially affected consumer of a changed symbol (`consumers.potential`, so at most 200 per change) to the node of its `next`, one step nearer the change; a pair already joined by a direct link keeps only the direct one. Both are lifted to the level (workspace to workspace, folder to folder, file to file, function to function); pairs on one node or where one node contains the other are dropped; the colour is the change it points to (the strongest on a shared pair); `weight` counts the edges (direct) or consumers (potential) on the pair. `links` holds the direct links sorted by id, then the potential ones sorted by id, cut at 2,000 in all (`capLinks`), so a potential link never pushes out a direct one; `linksTotal` counts both tiers before the cut. `placed` maps every changed symbol and file id, and every listed consumer id (#169), to its node, so clicking a change or a consumer in the panel selects and zooms to that node. The same `range` / `pr` options on `project-map` add `review` (with the layer) to that view's answer and leave its nodes and edges unchanged; the 3D settlements read the layer from there. The web draws untouched cells faded (desaturated, low opacity) in place, changed leaves filled with their change's colour (containers and workspace boxes outlined), the workspace links receded, and, with nothing selected, the whole affected chain (#173): directly affected nodes (hop 1) with a clear tint and solid curved lines with arrowheads to the change, potentially affected nodes (hops 2 and 3) with a softer tint and dashed, fainter lines to the node one step nearer, in the change colours, the dashed lines drawn first so the solid ones sit on top. The filter `show` (the left panel's "Show": "Changed only" `show=changed`, "+ Directly affected" `show=direct`, "+ Potentially affected", the default, no key) sets how far the chain goes, also while a change is in focus; it never changes the server's answer. A changed or affected file gets its name whenever any label fits (it also tries the smaller fonts the largest file of a folder gets), and hovering an affected node adds its hop in words ("directly affected (hop 1)", "potentially affected (hop 2)") and its path to the change along the drawn lines, solid first ("Path to the change: a → b → c", at most four steps). The 3D settlements recolour the buildings the same way, recede the roads and draw arcs between building tops, solid for direct links and dashed (short tube pieces) and fainter for potential ones; at most 600 arcs, direct ones kept first; the building tooltip gets the same hop and path lines. The review layer is drawn in the settlements style only (city and islands on the `map-3d` view show a note). **Mode `rings` (`mode=rings`).** Ring 0 holds the changed symbols (`change`, `signatureChanged`), rings 1 to 3 the callers by hop (`hop`); every node carries `group` = its file path. On each ring the nodes are ordered by file, then id, with one empty slot between two files (and one closing the circle when there are two files or more), so a file's nodes sit together; radius `max(previous + 150, slots x 30 / 2pi)` (ring 0: centre for one slot, else at least 70) and angle clockwise from the top by slot, even rings starting half a step in and odd rings a quarter step. Edges point from the caller on ring h + 1 to the code it calls on ring h, as the impact walk found them (consumer edge kinds) (at most 2,000; `review.edgesTotal` counts all); `review.layer` is placed on the ring nodes. The web shows the caption "Centre: what changed. Each ring out: code that calls the ring inside it.", every ring titled in plain words with its count ("Changed in this range", "Call the changed code directly", "2 calls away", "3 calls away"), a faint arc and the file name over each file group, changed nodes in the layer's colours with their names always shown, callers in a neutral tone that fades by hop but stays readable on the outer rings (named when the ring has room, 40 nodes or fewer, the same room on every ring since #173; else on hover), arrowheads at the callee, the outer ring of the reachable tier (below), a tooltip with name, path and change kind or hop, and hovering or selecting a node keeps its path (inward to the centre and outward through its callers) and fades the rest. **The reachable tier on the rings.** When some change has reachable consumers or hubs, ring 4 follows ring 3: its nodes are the reachable consumers of every change as listed (hop 4 or more, already capped, each distinct id once) and one node per hub of hop 4 or more (a hub of hop 2 or 3 stands for potential consumers and has no node) with id `hub:<hub id>`, `kind: "hub"` and `count` = the hub's `consumersTotal` (the largest over the changes), all with `hop: 4` and grouped by file like the other rings. An edge goes from each reachable node to its `next` when the response draws it, and from a hub node to its hub; these edges have `confidence: "low"` and are drawn dashed and faint. The ring is a circle with `className="rv-ring rv-ring-reachable"` and `data-hop="4"`, titled "4 or more calls away" with `review.reachableTotal` as its count. A hub node is a `role="button"` with `aria-expanded` and the label `<hub name>, <n> consumers, collapsed` (`expanded` when open); a click, Enter or Space expands it: the hub's members join the outer ring beside it, grouped by file, with a dashed edge to the node one step nearer (the web lays the ring out again with the server's slots and spacing), and the same again collapses it. Reachable nodes and hub nodes take the faint tint of potential consumers; with a change in focus they stay in colour with the potential ones. The 2D map and the 3D settlements do not draw the reachable tier. `review` carries `range`, `pr?`, `base`, `head`, `mergeBase`, `headSha`, `mode`, `map`, `files` (with a per-file change count and the file's `consumers`, the files that import it, then theirs), `changes` (id, kind, name, symbol kind, path, line, both sides' id/path/line/signature/exported, `signatureChanged`, `exportedChanged`, `locDelta`, direct `callers` (= `consumers.directTotal`), `consumers` (#169: `{ direct, directTotal, potential, potentialTotal, reachable, reachableTotal, hubs }`, the core entries plus `node`, the node of this response that shows the consumer (itself, else its file), and for a potential or reachable one `nextNode`; `hubs` are the core `HubEntry` plus the `node` that shows the hub, with every member placed the same way), and `untrusted_description`, the stored behavior text cut at 300 chars: the only place a description appears, rendered as plain text), `callersByHop` counts (hops 1 to 3), `reachableTotal` (the distinct consumers at hop 4 or more, the collapsed ones included), `crossings`, `risk`, `rules` (`RISK_RULES`), `levels`, `notes`, `layer`, `edgesTotal?` (rings), `ms` and `cached`. Same 127.0.0.1, token, Origin and Host checks as every view; `gantry_open_view({ view: "review", focus?, filters: { range } })` deep-links it (map mode). The left panel shows the range form, the View toggle (On the map / Rings), the 2D / 3D toggle and the level (map mode), "Show" (map mode, see above), risk with its reasons and the totals "Directly affected n · Potentially affected m" (distinct consumers at hop 1 and at hops 2-3, each at its nearest hop, from `callersByHop`; plus "Lines: x of y drawn" when the cap cut some), changed symbols, changed files, crossings and notes (clicking one selects it and focuses it on the map); a legend of the colours and lines sits on the canvas; the inspector shows the change kind and the signature before and after. **Consumers in the web (#169).** The selected change or changed file expands in the left panel to its consumers under the headings "Directly affected (n)", "Potentially affected (n)" and "Reachable (n)" (`reachableTotal`; the listed reachable consumers, then one row per hub of hop 4 or more, "<hub name> · <n> consumers", that opens to its members grouped by workspace in the order of `byWorkspace`; a hub of hop 2 or 3 has its row under "Potentially affected" the same way), grouped by file, each with its name, kind, how it uses the change in words (calls, renders, uses the hook, extends, implements, uses the type, imports, serves (route), ...), its crossings and, for a potential one, `hop h via <direct consumer>` (a reachable one `hop h via <the node it uses>`); the inspector of a change or changed file gets "Used by (n)" with the two tiers "Directly affected" and "Potentially affected" (the hub rows of hop 2 and 3 included, no "Reachable" group). Clicking a consumer selects it (the map zooms to the node that shows it, the rings mark it) while its change stays in focus (`views/reviewFocus.ts`, kept only while the selection is the change or one of its consumers). While a change is in focus, the 2D map, the 3D settlements and the rings show only that change, its direct consumers strongly (the hop-1 tint, solid lines to the change) and its potential ones softly (the faint tint, dashed and faint lines to the node one step nearer), and fade the rest. Selecting a change (#171) fits the 2D map to the union of its node and its direct consumers' nodes, with padding and never closer than the plain map's zoom to one node, and the settlements camera frames the change and its direct consumers; clicking a consumer zooms to that consumer. Done-when for M5: a 20-file range gives data and view in under 10 s with the store unchanged (`evals/bench/m5.ts`, `docs/milestones/M5.md`).
- **Feature slices (view 5, #290; the `slices` view module in `explorer/server/`).** Slices come from `effectiveGroups(store)` (section 7), not from config. A file belongs to the first user group that matches it, else to its deepest default directory (`apps/*/src/features/*`, `apps/*/src/modules/*`, `packages/*`). A default slice has id `slice:<dir>` and label `<dir>`; a user group's slice has id `group:<groupId>` and the group's name as label. An app with no file in any group keeps the first-level route-segment fallback (`routeFallback`); `shared` is unchanged. With no user groups the response is byte-identical to the config-driven one.
- **Cross-stack views (#48; `explorer/server/views/{crossStack,endpoints,pageApi,apiTables,featurePath,tableUsage,adapterNode}.ts`, web `canvas/FlowCanvas.tsx`, `canvas/flowGeom.ts`, `views/CrossStackPanel.tsx`, `views/FlowFocusPrompt.tsx`).** Views 7 to 11 of the plan's view catalog, built on the adapter nodes and `trace` (section 9). They read the store through `snapshotOf(store)` (`core/src/store/snapshot.ts`): a read-only in-memory copy of the effective edges, adapter nodes and files, keyed on `store.generation()`, that answers `effectiveEdges` / `effectiveAdapterNodes` with the same filters and order as the store (tested: `trace` over it equals `trace` over the store for every route, endpoint and table of `fixtures/adapters`, both directions), so a view can trace every route or endpoint without a query per visited node; traces are memoised per generation. **Repo labels.** The endpoints and feature path views of a store with more than one repo set `repo` on every node whose id names one (endpoints, middleware, handlers, services; for the feature path also routes, client code and tables), the repo part of the id; with one repo no node has the field. A feature path from a page follows its requests into the repo that serves them (section 9, "Trace"). **Enabling.** `ViewModule.enabled` may be a function of the API context (a contract change in #48): `/api/meta` reports its value per view and adds `reason` (the module's `disabledReason`) to a disabled one; a disabled view answers 404. The counts come from one SQL `COUNT(*) ... GROUP BY kind` over the effective adapter nodes (`adapterCounts`, cached per generation), never from the snapshot, because `/api/meta` is fetched after every refresh and focus. Each cross-stack view is enabled when the index holds adapter nodes of the kinds it needs: `endpoints` and `feature-path` an endpoint, `page-api` a route and an endpoint, `api-tables` an endpoint and a table, `table-usage` a table; on a repo without adapters (`fixtures/single-pkg`) all five are disabled and the tab's tooltip says why. **Response fields.** Nodes carry `lane` (the column or swimlane, 0 leftmost), `rank` (order inside the lane) and, in the endpoints view, `band`; edges may carry `via` (how many places an aggregated edge stands for) and `viaSample` (up to 6 of them by name). Every response has `lanes: [{ lane, label }]`; `wsCounts` (endpoints, page-api, api-tables), `totals` (counts for the panel), and the view's own fields below. Node ids are the index's; names, paths, kinds and counts only, never descriptions (a containment test seeds a marker as every description and suggested name and checks all five views and the adapter-node inspector). Output is sorted and deterministic (snapshot tests on `fixtures/adapters`). **7 `endpoints` (hint `elk-layered`).** Every endpoint, `band` = its group (`rpc` for oRPC, `http` for Hono, `server fn` for TanStack Start), endpoints ranked by group then label; lanes Endpoint, Middleware, Handler, then one per call depth. Middleware: Hono `mw:` nodes that `serves` it, plus the names in the endpoint's `data.middleware` as `mwref:<package>:<name>` nodes (no adapter node stands behind one; `/api/node` answers it with `node.middlewareRef = { name, endpoints }`, the endpoints of that package naming it); edge `endpoint -middleware-> mw`. Handler: the `handles` target; then `calls` for `depth` hops (route param `hops`/`depth`, 1-4, default 2), an inline handler's first hop limited to calls with an evidence line inside its `handlerSpan` (as `trace`). At most 1,500 nodes (`totals.truncated`). Filters `ws` (workspace ids or names), `kind` (`rpc`/`http`/`fn`), `method` (GET, POST, ..., `RPC` for an oRPC procedure without an OpenAPI route), `prefix` (URL path, dotted rpc path or server function name). Extra fields `groups`, `methods`. **8 `page-api` (hint `columns`).** Lane 0 routes (by label), lane 1 endpoints (barycentre of their routes). A `requests` edge route -> endpoint aggregates the route's down trace: every `requests` edge into an endpoint from the route's front set (`frontSet`: the route and what it reaches through `serves` / `renders` / `calls` / `uses_hook` without passing an endpoint), so a handler calling another endpoint is not a page's request; `via` = the distinct requesting places (`viaSample` names them, `route loader` for the route itself), `weight` = `via`, `low` only when every request behind it is low (drawn dashed). A route whose front set holds unmatched client calls gets the badge `n unmatched`; `totals.unmatched` counts the distinct `client_call` nodes over the union of the shown routes' front sets (a shared component's call once). Filters `ws` (the routes' app), `prefix` (route URL prefix), `all` (also routes and endpoints without a link); `totals` counts routes and endpoints without a link and the unmatched calls. **9 `api-tables` (hint `columns`, 3 lanes).** From each endpoint's own part of its down trace (`endpointTables`: `requests` edges and other endpoints are not followed, and only code on a path to a `reads`/`writes` edge is kept). Collapsed (default): lane 1 holds the code that makes the database call, joined to the endpoint by a `uses` edge whose `via` is the number of functions between and which is `low` when no way from the endpoint to that code is made of high-confidence edges only; `services=all` draws every function on the way (`handles`, `calls`). Lane 2 tables; `reads` blue, `writes` orange, low dashed. `endpointTables: [{ endpoint, reads, writes }]` lists every shown endpoint (the bench reads it). Filters as the endpoints view plus `all` (also endpoints that reach no table, badge `no table`). **10 `feature-path` (hint `elk-layered`, swimlanes Page / Client / Endpoint / Service / Table).** Needs `focus`: a route or an endpoint (`resolveTraceTarget` forms); a table or symbol is a 400, an unknown target a 404. From a route its down trace; from an endpoint the down trace plus the up trace. Lanes by kind (route, endpoint, table) and, for code, by side: down, code whose `from` chain to the start passes an endpoint is Service, else Client; up, code on the `from` chain between another endpoint and the start is Service. Every traversed edge is drawn; at most 600 nodes (`truncated`); `gaps` are the traces' (at most 40); the focus carries the badge `focus`. Without a focus the answer has no nodes and lists `choices: [{ id, label, kind, group, note? }]` (routes, then endpoints by group), which the web's focus prompt shows. **11 `table-usage` (hint `tree-lr`, reversed).** Needs `focus` = a table (id or name; anything else is a 400). From the table's up trace: `lane = deepest - depth` (the table on the right), ranks by a pre-order walk of the `from` tree with writers before readers at depth 1; every direct reader and writer is drawn even when the trace dropped it for reaching no endpoint or page (badge `no endpoint`); depth-1 nodes carry `writes`, `reads` or `writes · reads`. Fields `table: { name, columns, dialect }`, `gaps`, `truncated`, `totals` (writers, readers, endpoints, routes); without a focus, `choices` (tables with their writer and reader counts). **Inspector.** `/api/node/:id` answers for adapter nodes (`ep:`, `table:`, `mw:`, `call:`, and `mwref:` as above) with `node.path`/`line` from the adapter data and no description; an endpoint adds `node.endpoint = { adapter, group, method, path, rpcPath, protocol, handler: { id, label, path, line } | null, handlerName, middleware, requestedBy, reads, writes }` (tables from `endpointTables`), a table `node.table = { name, exportName, dialect, columns, readers, writers, readerSample, writerSample }` (distinct code holding `reads` / `writes`, samples of 8). The web shows them as key/value rows (the code that reads or writes a table is listed once, under Connections), with "Show feature path" (routes, endpoints) and "Show table usage" (tables); a double-click on a route or endpoint in any cross-stack view opens its feature path, on a table its usage. **Web.** A response with `lanes` is drawn by `FlowCanvas` (plain 2D canvas, no WebGL; List / Graph toggle as usual): labelled boxes in lanes laid out by `canvas/layout.ts` from `lane` and `rank` (columns: one column per lane in rank order; tree: a tidy tree over lanes, root on the side with fewer nodes; elk-layered: ELK with one partition per lane and bands laid out apart, stacked and lane-aligned, or above 250 nodes a barycentre sweep), lane titles in a strip at the top, swimlane stripes in the feature path, band frames in the endpoints view, edges as curves with arrowheads (`reads` blue, `writes` orange, `low` dashed), and a tooltip on a box (kind, path, badges) or a line (what it is, the via count and sample, uncertain). Feature path and table usage register a focus prompt (picker grouped as `choices`, 200 per group then "and n more"); a focus the server refuses (unknown, wrong kind) shows the prompt again with the error as a notice. **Tabs.** With a dozen views the tab strip scrolls sideways: a "‹" / "›" button laid over each end that has hidden tabs, and the open view's tab is scrolled into sight on every view change (a deep link included) and on resize until the user scrolls the strip. Done-when for M6: the three hand-traced chains on the private test bed match (`evals/bench/m6.ts`, `docs/milestones/M6.md`).
- **Explorer UI kit (#252; web `components/smoothui/`, `components/ui/`, `components/Segment.tsx`, `lib/utils.ts`, `styles.css`).** The chrome (top bar, left panel, inspector, status bar, search, lists, badges and the filter panels of every view, the review and cross-stack panels included) is built from SmoothUI components (`tabs` for the view tabs, `breadcrumb`), shadcn primitives on the Base UI `base-nova` style (`button`, `badge`, `checkbox`, `input`, `native-select`) and hand-written controls only where neither fits (range sliders, kept native so `role="slider"` can be filled; `Segment`, a `role="radio"` button), with Hugeicons (`HugeiconsIcon`) for every icon. The canvases (sigma, flow, three, the review rings and the duplicates canvas) draw as before and their own overlays are not part of the kit. Tailwind loads the theme scale and the utilities only, in cascade layers; the explorer's own rules are unlayered and win, so the kit never fights a leftover rule. The shadcn colour names (`--background`, `--primary`, `--muted-foreground`, `--border`, `--ring` and the rest) point at the explorer's tokens (`--bg`, `--accent`, `--muted`, `--border`, `--accent`) in `styles.css`, so light and dark keep the existing contrast pairs (3:1 for marks, 4.5:1 for text). **Reduced motion is honoured:** every kit animation (the tab indicator, spinners, button press) is off under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (Motion's `useReducedMotion`, `motion-reduce:` utilities); Playwright e2e (`web/e2e/ui-kit.spec.ts`) and `pnpm readme:media` force it, so a second `pnpm build && pnpm readme:media` changes no PNG. Filter checkboxes are `role="checkbox"` elements with a hidden input (Base UI), so specs address them by role and name, not by label text.

## 12. Git hooks install

- `gantry hooks install` run in a workspace installs the hooks into every repo of it (each `.git/hooks/*` carries the same data dir, so every repo reads the one store), and `hooks uninstall` and `gantry uninstall` remove them from all. Each repo is handled as below on its own; in a workspace of several repos, the wrapper dir is `<dataDir>/hooks/<worktreeHash>` and the previous `core.hooksPath` is kept in `meta.prev_hooks_path:<repo>` (and `meta.prev_hooks_path_scope:<repo>`).
- Hooks: pre-commit, pre-push, post-checkout, post-merge, post-rewrite. Each is a 3-line sh script: `[ "$GANTRY_WORKER" = 1 ] || "$HOME/.gantry/bin/gantry" hook <name> "$@" </dev/null || true`, then chaining (git runs hooks without the plugin environment, so when the repo's resolved data dir is not under `~/.gantry/data` the install writes `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA='<base>'` in front of the shim call, #144, so the hook reads the same store as the plugin; when no plugin base applies the resolved base is `~/.gantry/data`, the install still proceeds and writes to stderr `gantry: warning: no plugin data directory found; the git hooks will read <dataDir>. Run this from /gantry:init or set CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA.`) (below), then `exit 0`. `GANTRY_WORKER=1` skips only the Gantry call, so a chained user hook still runs; Gantry's stdin is `/dev/null` so the chained hook still receives git's stdin.
- If `core.hooksPath` is unset: write into `.git/hooks/` (use `git rev-parse --git-path hooks`); if a hook file already exists, rename it `<name>.gantry-prev` and chain to it.
- If `core.hooksPath` is set (husky/lefthook): create `<dataDir>/hooks/` wrapper scripts that run Gantry then exec the original `<hooksPath>/<name>` with the same args, and set local `git config core.hooksPath <dataDir>/hooks`, saving the previous value in `meta.prev_hooks_path`.
- Shim `~/.gantry/bin/gantry`: sh script with absolute paths to `node` and `plugin/dist/cli.js` resolved at install; if the target is missing it exits 0.
- `gantry hooks uninstall` / `gantry uninstall` restore everything exactly.

## 13. Fixtures and evals

`fixtures/single-pkg` (one package.json, ~15 files), `fixtures/pnpm-turbo` (apps/web TanStack Router file routes, apps/api Hono + oRPC, packages/db Drizzle sqliteTable, packages/ui, packages/utils with a misleading-name duplicate pair), `fixtures/nx` (2 projects + project.json). `fixtures/bundler-aliases` (a pnpm workspace with no `node_modules` and no tsconfig `paths`: a Vite app with object-form aliases, a Vite app with the array form, a webpack app with a `$` exact alias, and an app whose alias value is computed; every aliased import resolves except the computed one's, which is unresolved with `alias_not_static`; numbers in `fixtures/README.md`). `fixtures/ts-references` (a repo with no workspace tool, tied together only by tsconfig project references: the root `tsconfig.json` has `"files": []` and references `packages/core` (`@refs/core`, `main` in `dist/`), `packages/util` (no `package.json`) and `apps/cli` (`@refs/cli`); it indexes to four workspaces, `apps/cli/src/main.ts` imports both packages' source, and no edge points into `dist/`; it has a dump snapshot). `fixtures/no-workspace-tool` (a repo with several `package.json` files and no workspace tool or tsconfig references: root `plain-root`, `shared` (`@plain/shared`, `main` `src/index.ts`), `server` and `web` depending on it, and `tools/scripts` as a nested folder; it indexes to five workspaces, `server` and `web` import `shared/src/index.ts`, and it has a dump snapshot). `fixtures/tsconfig-extends-workspace` (a pnpm workspace with no `node_modules`: `@acme/config-typescript/nextjs.json` extends `./base.json`, whose `paths` are `~shared/*`; `apps/web` extends it and sets its own `paths` `@/*`, `apps/admin` extends it with none; the import of `@/lib/format` in `apps/web` and of `~shared/date` in `apps/admin` both resolve, so `init` reports 0 unresolved; it has a dump snapshot). `fixtures/react-router-fastify` (#267): three React Router apps (data, declarative, framework mode with `flatRoutes()`), a Fastify API with prefixes across files, `route()`, an imported path constant and `@fastify/autoload`, and a Drizzle `pgTable` package; it has a dump snapshot like the other fixtures. `evals/seeded-duplicates/` holds 10 cases (3 with misleading names) each as `{ existing, newCode, expected }`, written by hand (no test-bed code). `evals/seeded-duplicates-go/` (#223) holds the Go set: a base module, 10 cases, 3 negatives and two runners (format in section 17). `evals/labelled/` holds the scripts that build the 200-function labelled set from the private test bed; the set itself (JSONL: factSheet, label behavior, nameFits) lives only in git-ignored `evals/private/`.

`fixtures/linking-repos` (hand-written; the tests copy each directory into its own git repo and write `gantry-workspace.json`): `web` calls `fetch` for `/api/users`, `/api/users/${id}`, `/invoices`, `/health`, a path nobody serves, an environment-based URL and an outside URL, and `client.api.users.$get()` on a Hono client, plus three TanStack Router file routes (`/users/$id`, `/orders/$id`, `/invoices/$id`) whose components call functions of `src/features/`, with a committed recipe; `users-api` and `billing` are two Hono apps that both serve `GET /health`, whose `GET /api/users/:id`, `GET /api/orders/:id`, `GET /invoices/:id` and `POST /invoices` handlers read or write the Drizzle tables `users`, `orders` and `invoices` through `src/db/queries.ts` (each repo has a committed recipe of Drizzle rules). `expected-traces.json` lists, for each route, the endpoint, handler and table its down trace must reach; `core/src/trace.test.ts` compares the traces to it. `fixtures/linking` (all hand-written, a pnpm workspace with `apps/*` and a `.gantry/recipe.json` of Hono rules): `@fx/users-api` (a Hono app mounting `users` at `/api`, with `/users`, `/users/:id`, `/users/me`, `/orders/:id`, `/posts/:postId/comments` and a root `/health`) and `@fx/billing` (`/health`, `/invoices`, `/invoices/:id`); `@fx/web` with `src/features/users.ts` (calls through `fetch`, `axios.create` and `ky.create` instances whose bases come from the environment or a constant, wrappers, an enum and object paths and an absolute URL: those whose URL starts with an environment member stay `dynamic_url` gaps), `src/lib/same-origin-http.ts` (`axios.create({ baseURL: "/api" })`, `ky.create({ prefixUrl: "/api" })`, a `kyRel.extend(...)` and four functions: `relFetch`, `relRequest` and `useRelFetch` are wrappers, `getUser` is not), `src/lib/index.ts` re-exporting the instances, `src/lib/opaque.ts` (`send`, a wrapper only through the recipe's `clientWrappers` entry), `src/features/same-origin.ts` (seven functions, one instance call each), `src/lib/same-origin-config.ts` (`API`, the enum `RelPaths` and the `as const` object `REL`), `src/features/same-origin-consts.ts` (eight functions, one call each, whose URL is built from those constants, `??`, a conditional or `new URL`; `cTwoBackends` calls a route both backends have and is an `ambiguous` gap), `src/features/same-origin-wrappers.ts` (six functions, one wrapper call each) and `src/features/reexport.ts` (a call on an instance imported through the re-export); `@fx/legacy-web` sets `axios.defaults.baseURL = "/api"` and calls the default `axios` three ways. `core/src/adapters/clients.test.ts` asserts the exact `requests` edges and the `wrapper_body` nodes.

`fixtures/pnpm-turbo` and `fixtures/adapters` each carry the reference recipe `.gantry/recipe.json` (#203: Hono and TanStack Router rules; #247: Drizzle `table`, `read` and `write` rules) that gives the nodes the built-in adapters give. `fixtures/adapters` (#45): a Hono app mounting a sub-app from another workspace with `.route()`, plus `basePath`, chained and `on` routes, imported path constants and middleware; TanStack Start server functions called from a route `loader` and `beforeLoad`; a test-file app and a package without the framework dependencies, which must be skipped. #46 adds `packages/db` (Drizzle `posts` and `users` tables on D1, object and callback column forms, select/join/alias, relational query, insert/update/delete, `$count`, raw SQL that is ignored) and `packages/api` (oRPC builders across files, plain-object, `.router()`, lazy, factory and contract-first routers, an unresolved schema import read at low confidence), served by `apps/server` through an `RPCHandler` in a Hono `use('/rpc/*')`. #47 adds client calls in `apps/web` (an oRPC client built by a local factory and its TanStack Query utils, a sub-client alias used directly and one passed on, a Hono `hc` client with a computed key, `useServerFn`, `fetch` with a template URL and an external one, a missing procedure and a router-prefix `key()`), `posts.get` / `posts.authors` inline siblings of `postsRouter` that touch different tables, and a `/users` route; the trace from `/posts/$postId` reaches `rpc posts.get` and `table:posts`, and up from `table:posts` lists that page. `evals/bench/m6.ts` (#48) builds a fresh index of a repo into a data dir outside it, checks hand-traced chains from a git-ignored `evals/private/m6-expected.json` (`{ features: [{ name, route, endpoints, tables, raw_sql_tables?, pages }] }`: endpoints in trace(route) down, tables equal to the endpoints' trace tables, pages in trace(table) up for every table) through `trace` and through the views' API, and times views 7 to 11 (API cold and warm, page load to the drawn canvas through `gantry_open_view`); it prints aggregate numbers only.

`fixtures/deep-radius` (#293, all hand-written; a feature overlay `fixtures/deep-radius.feature/`): workspaces `packages/core` (`@deep/core`), `apps/web` and `apps/api`, with a chain of calls six hops long from `base` (hops 1 to 3: `step1`, `step2`, `step3` and `hub`; hop 4: `step4`, `Runner.helper` and 610 one-line users of `hub`, split between the two apps; hop 5: `zetaHigh` (a `high` edge) and `Runner.alphaLow` (a `low` edge, through `this.helper()`); hop 6: `Page`). The overlay changes the body of `base` only. `scripts/fixture-repo.ts <name> <dest>` builds a fixture into a directory as a git repo (branch `main`, plus `feature` for a fixture with an overlay) with a fixed author and date, so the commit SHAs are the same everywhere. `fixtures/deep-radius.review.json` is the stdout of `gantry review main...feature --json` on that repo (directory named `repo`) indexed at `main`; a CLI test compares it byte for byte. `scripts/reachable-bench.ts` times the walk on an indexed repo and checks it against a per-node reference walk (not part of `pnpm check`).

`fixtures/go-basic` (#221, all hand-written): a root module `example.com/gobasic` (`go.work` using `.` and `./tools`) with `cmd/server` (`package main`), `internal/store` (struct `Store` with `Get` and `Put`, constructor `New`, interface `Reader`, a `_test.go` file; since #222 also `Mirror` (a second `Reader`), `Cache` (embeds `Store`), `Warm`, `Lookup` (a call through `Reader`) and `Dump` (a call in a `range` over `[]*Store`)), `internal/app` (#222: `App.Run` calls `a.deps.Store.Put` through a field chain and `text.Slugify` plain, while `cmd/server` reaches it as `str.Slugify` through an aliased import), `internal/text` (`Slugify`, `trimDashes`, a `const` block with two names on one line, a `var`, a type alias, a `String` method, two `init` functions), `internal/net` (`listen.go` and `listen_unix.go` with `//go:build unix`, both declaring `listen`), `internal/gen/zz_generated.go` (generated header), a `vendor/` folder that must be skipped, and a second module `tools` (`example.com/gobasic/tools`, requiring the first); dump snapshot `dump-go-basic.json`. `evals/go/score-symbols.ts <dump.json> <truth.json>` (#221) scores the Go symbols of an `index --dump` against `docs/spikes/v2-go/truth` (`go run . <repo> > truth.json`): truth `struct` is `class` and `var` is `const`, symbols match on `(file, kind, receiver, name)` and `_test.go` files are outside the truth; it prints `{ repo, perKind: { <kind>: { truth, found, matched, precision, recall } }, all }` as JSON and exits 1 when any kind is below 99% precision or recall (2 on unreadable input). `evals/go/score-calls.ts <dump.json> <truth.json>` (#222) scores the Go `calls` and `implements` edges the same way: edges are `(caller, callee)` pairs as the graph stores them, truth `pkgpath.Name` / `pkgpath.Recv.Name` is mapped to symbols through the module path of the workspace holding the file and the package directory, only truth files in `callFiles` (the host build) are scored, each truth edge takes the kind of its first site, a function calling itself is left out, and a call through an interface is matched on (caller, method name). It prints `{ module, perKind: { samePackage, qualified, concreteMethod, promoted, interfaceMethod }: { truth, matched, recall }, all: { truth, matched, recall, found }, precision, implements: { truth, found, matched, recall, precision } }` and exits 1 when same-package or qualified recall is below 0.99, concrete-method recall below 0.90 or precision below 0.999 (2 on unreadable input); promoted-method, interface-method and `implements` figures have no bar. `score-symbols.ts` leaves interface-body methods out of the truth comparison. `caddy` and `hugo` are the held-out Go repos in `evals/oss/repos.json` (stack `go`).

`fixtures/java-basic` (all hand-written): a two-module Maven project, no TypeScript: the parent `pom.xml` (`java-basic`, listing `core` and `app`), `core` (`com.acme.core`: interface `Greeter`, `PoliteGreeter` implementing it, `LoudGreeter` extending `PoliteGreeter` and calling `super.greet` and `Strings.shout`, `Strings` with a static `shout`, `Formatter` with two overloads of `format` and two of `pad`, a test `StringsTest`, and `target/classes/.../Stale.java`, which must be skipped) and `app` (`com.acme.app`, depending on `core`: `Main` with `main`, an on-demand import and a constructor call; `App` with a field typed by `Greeter`, a static import of `Strings.shout`, an inner class `Inner` calling a private method of `App`, and a static nested class; a `Person` record; `generated/Version.java`, generated by its path); dump snapshot `dump-java-basic.json`, byte-equal from two fresh data dirs. The snapshot shows the four call cases, all level 2: the interface call (`App.run` to `Greeter.greet`, `high`), the overload (`Formatter.format` to both `pad` overloads, `low` each; `format(text, 1)` and `formatter.format(hello)` pick one overload by their argument count, `high`), the static import (`App.run` to `Strings.shout`, `high`) and the inner class (`App.Inner.ping` to `App.log`, `high`).

`fixtures/csharp-basic` (all hand-written): two projects, no TypeScript. `src/Core/Acme.Core.csproj` (named by its file, `Acme.Core`) holds `IGreeter.cs` (interface `IGreeter.Greet`), `PoliteGreeter.cs` (implementing it), `Strings.cs` (`Strings.Shout`), `StringExtensions.cs` (`Twice`, an extension method of `string`), `Report.cs` and `Report.Totals.cs` (the two parts of `partial class Report`: `Render` and the constructor in one, `Header` in the other), `Schema.Designer.cs` (generated, by its path) and `obj/Debug/Stale.cs` (skipped). `src/App/App.csproj` (`<AssemblyName>Acme.App</AssemblyName>`, a `<ProjectReference>` to `Acme.Core.csproj`, so a `depends_on` edge) holds `Program.cs` (`Main` makes the workspace an app; `using Acme.Core;` and `using static Acme.Core.Strings;`) and `ProgramTests.cs` (a test file). `fixtures/rust-basic` (all hand-written): a two-crate workspace, no TypeScript, no Cargo needed. `fixtures/scip/pnpm-turbo.scip` is a SCIP index of `fixtures/pnpm-turbo`, made once with scip-typescript 0.6.2 (the command is in `fixtures/scip/README.md`) and committed as data; the precise-mode tests (`core/src/scip/`, `gantry index --scip` in the CLI tests) read it. On that fixture it has 23 documents, of which 22 are indexed files, and gives 12 edges (7 `calls`, 4 `renders`, 1 `uses_hook`; two of the calls come from the file `apps/api/src/app.ts`), 262 unmapped occurrences, 29 other references and no stale document. The tests date a copy of the index an hour ahead, because the copied fixture's files are newer than the committed index.

`fixtures/multirepo` (#214, all hand-written; reused by the multirepo issues that follow): a workspace of two separate repos under one directory. `gantry-workspace.json` is `{ "repos": [{ "path": "./web" }, { "path": "./api", "repo": "acme/api" }] }`. `web/` (repo name `web`, a single package `mr-web` depending on `@mr/contracts` ^1.0.0 and `react`): `src/pages/posts.tsx` (a component that calls `fetch("/api/posts")` and renders with `formatDate` from `@mr/contracts`), `src/lib/slug.ts` (`makeSlug`, six statements, a near-copy of the api repo's `toSlug`) and `src/lib/only-in-web.ts` (`debounceInput`). `api/` (repo name `acme/api`, a pnpm workspace with `apps/*` and `packages/*` and a private root package): `apps/server` (`mr-server`, depending on `hono` and `@mr/contracts`) with `src/index.ts` (a Hono app, `app.get("/api/posts", listPosts)`), `src/posts.ts` (`listPosts`), `src/slug.ts` (`toSlug`, six statements) and `src/retry.ts` (`computeRetryDelay`, which exists only here); `packages/contracts` (`@mr/contracts`, `"exports": { ".": "./src/index.ts" }`) with `formatDate` and `parseCursor`. `materializeMultirepo()` in `packages/core/test/helpers/fixture.ts` copies the fixture to a temp directory, runs `git init -b main`, sets a local user and commits (`add -A -f`, `commit -m fixture`, fixed author and dates) inside `web/` and inside `api/` separately (the temp root is not a repo) and returns `{ root, repos: { web, api }, git(repo, args), cleanup }`. The dump snapshot is `packages/core/src/__snapshots__/dump-multirepo.json`; the tests are `core/src/workspace/file.test.ts` and `core/src/multirepo.test.ts`.

`fixtures/go-recipes` (#368, all hand-written): four small Go modules, each a repo of its own with a committed `.gantry/recipe.json` of Go rules, for the Go rule shape of section 5. `nethttp`: a `ServeMux` app (patterns with a method and a path, a bare `/`, a host pattern that the rule's constraint leaves out, a router passed to `registerUsers`, a method value as handler, an inline handler, and `registerLegacy`, which no call names and whose rules set `uncalled`). `chi`: a router with `Use`, `Mount` of a function that returns a router and of a method that returns one, `Route` with a nested `Route`, `Group` and `Handle`. `gin`: `Default()`, `Group` chains and a group passed to a function in another package. `sql`: raw SQL through `database/sql` (a string, a raw string over several lines, a `+` chain with a constant, a name from a parameter), migrations in `sqlFiles`, a typed value saved through a mapper-like type (`model` and `methods`), and a query file whose blocks name generated Go functions (a `query` text rule). `core/src/recipe/go-recipes.test.ts` asserts the exact endpoints (method, full path, handler), tables, reads and writes of each, and that `recipe check --nodes` lists the nodes the index stores.

`fixtures/overlay-groups` (#289, all hand-written): `@demo/shop` with `src/orders/invoice.ts` (`totalDue`), `src/catalog/price.ts` (`formatPrice`) and `src/catalog/cart.ts` (imports and calls `totalDue`), plus `groups.json` (test data, not read by Gantry: groups `orders` with `mayUse: []` and `catalog` with `mayUse: ["orders"]`) and `invoice.edited.txt`, the edit that makes `totalDue` import and call `formatPrice`, which crosses the boundary of `orders`.

**OSS corpus (#260).** `evals/oss/repos.json` is `{ repos: [{ name, url, sha, stack, lang, role }] }`: `name` unique and matching `[a-z0-9._-]+`, `url` https (or `file://`, for tests), `sha` 40 lowercase hex, `stack` non-empty lowercase strings, `lang` a subset of `["ts","go","java","python"]`, `role` `"tuning"` or `"held-out"`, entries sorted by `name`, and the optional `pip` (the Python packages installed into the environment the Python SCIP indexer runs in) and `pythonPath` (folders of the repo, relative to its root, put on the Python path of that indexer where the code imports from a source root such as `src`). Nothing is measured on a repo that is not in it. `pnpm eval:clone [--only <name>...] [--repos <file>]` clones each repo at its SHA into `$GANTRY_OSS_DIR` (default `~/Dev/gantry-oss`)`/<name>@<sha7>`, never overwriting a clone at another commit or with a dirty tree, and refusing a directory inside this repository (exit 0 all present, 1 a clone failed, 2 invalid `repos.json`, wrong commit, dirty tree or bad directory). The script's tests run in `pnpm check` on `scripts/eval-fixtures/tiny` with no network.

**Packs proof (`scripts/eval-packs.ts`, `scripts/eval-packs-score.ts`, `evals/packs/repos.json`, `docs/packs-proof.md`).** The Java and Python packs measured against the SCIP truth of two pinned public repos per language (the file has the OSS corpus schema, `role` `held-out`; clone with `pnpm eval:clone --repos evals/packs/repos.json`). `pnpm eval:packs [--only <name>...] [--repos <file>] [--runs <n>] [--truth] [--report <file>] [--json <file>]` takes each clone, indexes it without `--scip` and then with it, each time with `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA` set to a new temporary folder (no real index is touched), three times in a row by default, and reads the peak memory from `/usr/bin/time`. The SCIP files `<name>.scip` are made once with `scip-java` and `scip-python` (the toolchain is used for the truth and nothing else; `--truth` makes a missing one; Gantry never runs an indexer), live outside this repository and are not committed; `GANTRY_OSS_DIR` is the clones, `GANTRY_PACKS_TRUTH_DIR` the SCIP files (default `<GANTRY_OSS_DIR>/packs-truth`), `GANTRY_CLI` the built CLI, and `GANTRY_SCIP_JAVA`, `GANTRY_SCIP_PYTHON`, `GANTRY_PACKS_PYTHON` and `JAVA_HOME` the tools of `--truth`. It reads the SCIP file with `core/scip` (`readScip`, `scipDescriptor`), not a second reader. Scored: the files that the graph holds and the index has a document for. Symbols: precision and recall per kind (`function`, `method`, `class`, and `interface` and `enum` as well for Java) of the graph's symbols against the SCIP definitions, each definition mapped to the innermost symbol of its file that holds its line and is spelled like it; a definition that no source declares (an implicit constructor) is counted apart and is no definition of the truth. Calls: every SCIP reference to a function, method or constructor (for Python only where `(` follows the name, and a class called as `Foo(...)` is a call of `Foo.__init__` when `Foo` declares it, else in no truth and an edge at that site is not scored; for Java a name followed by `=` is an annotation element, no call) from the innermost symbol that holds the line, to a target of the repo, is a truth call; recall is over the unique (caller, target) pairs against the graph's `calls` edges of any confidence, precision over the graph's edges (a `high` edge is right when its pair is in the truth; low-confidence precision is per call site, a site being right when its true target is among its low edges), each split by level and confidence. A SCIP `local` symbol that is a function or method stays in the truth and is mapped to the graph's symbol for that body where there is one (the anonymous and enum-constant body methods), else it is a miss; a self-recursive call stays in the truth and is a miss; both are counted apart. Java lambdas and method references are measured as they are. The bars are the maintainer's, not changed after a result: symbols at least 97% precision and recall, for each of `function`, `method` and `class` (`interface` and `enum` are reported, not held to it); Java calls at least 99% precision on `high` edges and 85% recall; Python calls at least 99% on `high` edges, 90% on low-confidence edges and 70% recall; the worst of the runs of each repo decides, no ratio is rounded up, and `docs/packs-proof.md` has one `Bar met: yes` or `Bar met: no` line per language. A missed bar is a result (the script exits 0); nothing measured is changed to meet a bar. `scripts/eval-packs.test.ts` runs the scoring on a synthetic index with no toolchain and no network.

## 14. Hook I/O contracts

- **Hooks and the team cache.** No hook starts a request to a team server or to anything else on a network, and no hook waits on one: hooks never import `pullTeamCache`, `core/src/team/client.ts`, `descriptionSync.ts` (`syncDescriptions`, `syncTeamDescriptions`: the description sync, section 11) or `publishView`, `publishGroup`, `unpublishView` and `unpublishGroup`, and may use only the team cache readers (section 11) and the merge readers of section 7, which open `team-cache.db` read-only and return empty results when it is missing. Team data is as of the last pull. `cache.guard.test.ts` and `team.guard.test.ts` check the hook sources, and every file they import, for those imports and check that the readers never call `fetch`.
- **PostToolUse** (matcher `Write|Edit|MultiEdit`), the trigger: stdin JSON with `tool_name`, `tool_input.file_path`, `cwd`, `session_id`. It records the edited file in the session's batch and re-indexes it if it is a source file; with a `session_id` it prints nothing. Budget 1,500 ms warm on 2,000 files.
- **Stop**, the end of the batch (#131): stdin JSON with `session_id`, `cwd` (and `stop_hook_active`, not read). It settles the session's batch (below). On findings, stdout exactly `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"Stop","additionalContext":"<≤600 chars>"}}`; on none, no stdout. Same budget.
- Without a `session_id` a PostToolUse edit is a batch of one, under a key of its own (never shared with another call; its row is removed when the call ends): it is settled at once and the findings are printed as `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PostToolUse","additionalContext":"<≤600 chars>"}}`.
- **SessionStart:** heal, then print one plain-text line only if the index is missing (`Gantry: run /gantry:init to index this repo`; a store without a built backlog, `isIndexed` false, counts as missing) or backlog ≥ `backlogReminderThreshold` (`Gantry: N functions await descriptions — run /gantry:sync when convenient`). With no store it creates nothing (#147). Claude Code adds plain stdout of SessionStart to the model's context.

- **Stale store (#198).** A store whose `schema_version` is not `SCHEMA_VERSION` (section 9) makes `openStore` throw `StaleStoreError`. Hooks (the git hooks, SessionStart, PostToolUse, Stop and pre-commit) never reset or rebuild it: they catch the error, log one line to `<dataDir>/logs/hooks.log`, exit 0 with their normal empty output and leave the store as it is. SessionStart then prints its usual `Gantry: run /gantry:init to index this repo` line. The next non-hook command rebuilds the store.
