Implementation notes (#31; checked against the Claude Code hooks docs on 2026-11-14; Stop added in #131 and checked against https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks on 2026-11-15). `hooks/hooks.json` is `{ "description"?, "hooks": { "<Event>": [ { "matcher"?, "hooks": [ { "type": "command", "command": "...", "timeout": <seconds> } ] } ] } }`: the event names sit under a `hooks` key and each matcher group holds a nested `hooks` array (Stop takes no matcher). Claude Code sends more fields than the contract needs (`transcript_path`, `hook_event_name`, `tool_use_id`, `permission_mode`, `last_assistant_message`, ...); the hooks read only the fields above. Exit 0 with the JSON above on stdout adds `additionalContext` to Claude's context (the docs cap it at 10,000 characters; ours is 600); for Stop the docs say it is non-error feedback and the conversation continues so Claude can act on it, so a turn that left a duplicate gets one more step to reuse it. Stop fires for the main agent only (SubagentStop is separate and not used): a subagent's edits join the session's batch and are settled when the main agent stops. Exit 2 would show stderr to Claude and any other non-zero code shows a "hook error" notice, so a Gantry hook never exits non-zero.

The pipeline is trigger, then batch re-index, then checks, then a double-check, then output (#131; the M2 replay showed that checking each edit on its own alerts on the first half of a move and on an import whose dependency the same change adds to package.json):

- **Trigger (PostToolUse).** Any edited file that can change a finding is recorded in `pending_files` (section 9) for the session, under the repo that holds the file (found from its path): a source file (`isIndexable`, which also excludes sensitive files), a `package.json`, a `tsconfig*.json` or `pnpm-workspace.yaml`. Other files are ignored. The first time a batch touches a file, the `exactHash` of each of its symbols is stored as the file's baseline. A source file is re-indexed into the working layer at once (a store write, never a write into the repo), so the MCP tools see it; nothing is checked yet.
- **Batch re-index (Stop).** Every source file of the batch with `reindexed` 0 (a trigger cut off before its index finished, or a manifest edited after the file) or gone from disk is re-indexed into the working layer, 8 files per committed step, each marked `reindexed` (so a batch too large for one Stop continues at the next); the manifest rows are removed once that is done. Each index run re-reads the workspaces from disk (`package.json` name, dependencies and exports, the workspace list, `tsconfig` paths) and stores them, so the checks see the post-batch state; a deleted file is marked deleted. Files whose trigger finished its index are not indexed again. A source file changed afterwards by a shell command is not re-read here; the double-check covers the matched side only.
- **Progress and concurrency.** A settle first prunes rows older than 24 h, then works file by file. Each step takes the store's write lock with a short wait (200 ms); when another settle or index holds it, the settle stops there and the rest stays in the batch (fail open). A file's row is deleted in the same transaction as its check, by the `created_at` that was read, so a file edited again during the settle, and a file added to the batch meanwhile, stay for the next Stop. No file is started after the budget minus a margin (300 ms, or a quarter of the budget if smaller), so the findings of the files already checked are printed in time; the files it had no time for stay in the batch, and every Stop makes progress on it. Opening the store itself can still wait on a lock held by a long index run; the hook budget then cuts the Stop off and nothing is lost but time, since no row was deleted.
- **Known limit: baselines are per session.** Two sessions editing the same file each keep their own baseline. When session B first touched a file before session A changed other functions in it, B's Stop also reports A's changes (they differ from B's baseline); when A changed it first, B's baseline already includes A's changes and B reports only its own.
- **Checks.** For each source file of the batch, the function-like symbols that are new or whose `exactHash` differs from the baseline are checked with `findDuplicates` (tiers exact, near, code_vector). The `semantic` tier is not used. `code_vector` reads stored vectors only and never loads the model, so a new symbol without a vector is checked by the exact and near tiers alone. An import is reported only if `checkImports` flags it for the file and does not flag it for the `HEAD` version of the file (only imports added since the last commit).
- **Double-check against disk**, just before output. A duplicate is kept only while the matched function is still in its file on disk: unchanged since it was indexed, or changed but still exact or near to the new function at the configured thresholds (a changed code_vector match is dropped, as its vector cannot be recomputed in the hook). This drops the first half of a move once the old copy has been edited away or deleted, also by a shell command that ran no hook. An undeclared-import finding is dropped when the workspace's or the root's `package.json` on disk now declares the package (dependencies, devDependencies, peerDependencies or optionalDependencies). Dropped findings are silent. The double-check runs before the matches of a symbol are cut to 2, so a dropped match makes room for the next one; for one symbol, when any kept match has `inline: true` advice only the inline matches are listed.
- **Group boundaries (#289).** A group whose `mayUse` is not null is a boundary (section 7); `null` means no rule. A _crossing_ is an edge of kind `imports` (file to file), `calls`, `renders` or `uses_hook` whose source is a member of a boundary group G and whose target is a member of a group H, with H not G, H not in G's `mayUse`, and the source not also a member of H. Membership uses `matchSelector` on the node; a symbol is a member when it or its file matches. Only crossings the batch adds are reported: edges from the batch's new or changed symbols (and, for `imports`, from the batch's files) that the main layer does not have. The check reads the groups once per file with `readOverlay` and reads nothing else but the store; it never writes `overlay.db`. It runs per file after that file's duplicate and import checks, inside the same step, so it shares the budget margin, and a crossing goes through the double-check too: it is dropped when the source file on disk no longer holds an import of the target (a relative specifier is resolved against the source file; a package or alias specifier cannot be resolved here and keeps the crossing). pre-commit adds the crossings of the staged symbols and files, against the main layer. Boundaries only warn: nothing is ever blocked (section 5). **Team boundaries.** The groups of the team cache are boundaries on every machine, by the same rule. `crossingFindings(store, changed, files, opts?)` takes `opts.groups` (replaces the groups read from the overlay) and `opts.allEdges: true` (keeps the edges the main layer already has, for a run where the index is the head checkout); with no `opts` it behaves as above. In `settle`, once per call, the team cache is opened read-only (`openTeamCache`) and read with `readTeamRepo` (section 11) and `mergedGroups` (section 7); when it is missing, its `meta.repo` is not this repo, or it holds no group, nothing is added and the output is the one without a cache. Otherwise the local call uses the local groups that no team group shadows, and a second call passes all the team groups, so every one counts for membership, but only those with `mayUse` not null act as boundaries (a team group with `mayUse: null` is never a source, though a call into it is still named); each team finding has `team: true` and goes through the same disk double-check. The team call reads only that cache file, never a server, and runs inside the hook's budget (100 team groups and 20 changed symbols: median under 100 ms, `teamBoundaries.test.ts`). `teamBoundaries.e2e.test.ts` runs the built hooks with `fetch` and `net` connect replaced by functions that throw. **Pull request comment.** The Action's comment has a separate `### Team boundaries` section made from `gantry team boundaries` (section 11), which calls `crossingFindings` with the groups of the team server's pull and `allEdges: true`; it warns and never fails the check, and it reads the groups from the server, never from the repository.
- **Output.** Names, paths, tiers, scores and actions only; no body and no stored description. A line is `Gantry: <new> (<path>:<line>) looks like <existing> in <path>:<line> [<tier> <score>]. <action>`, or `Gantry: <action> (line N)` for imports, whole lines only up to 600 chars; files in path order, in each file symbols lowest in the file first, then imports, then crossings. A crossing between two symbols is `Gantry: <srcName> (<srcPath>:<srcLine>) in group <srcGroupName> uses <dstName> (<dstPath>:<dstLine>) in group <dstGroupName>, which <srcGroupName> does not list. Warn only.`; an `imports` crossing with no symbol-level crossing between the same two files and groups is `Gantry: <srcPath> in group <srcGroupName> imports <dstPath> in group <dstGroupName>, which <srcGroupName> does not list. Warn only.` Crossing lines of a file are ordered by source line (an import-only line counts as line 0), then target id. A crossing of a team group has the same shape with `in team group` in place of `in group`, for example `Gantry: <srcName> (<srcPath>:<srcLine>) in team group <srcGroupName> uses <dstName> (<dstPath>:<dstLine>) in team group <dstGroupName>, which <srcGroupName> does not list. Warn only.`; all team lines come after every other finding of the batch, so the 600-character cap drops them first. Once every file is checked the batch is empty, so a second Stop in the same turn says nothing.
- Each hook stops at 1,500 ms (env `GANTRY_POST_TOOL_BUDGET_MS` overrides it for tests) and prints nothing (fail open). A Stop cut off at the budget has deleted the rows of the files it finished, and their findings are not printed (the margin above is there to make that rare; the M2 replay counts it); the files it did not finish stay for the next Stop. Each hook's own `timeout` in `hooks.json` is 5 s.
- **Duplicate rules of the pull request comment (#282).** The Action (section 10) applies the pre-commit rule, new or changed symbols checked against the rest of the index, to the pull request: `gantry duplicates --since <base>` keeps the groups of the Duplicates view (section 17, tiers exact and near; code-vector only when the workflow ran `gantry embed`; the `semantic` tier is not used) with a member among the symbols added or changed in `<base>...HEAD`, and the comment marks those members "added here" or "changed here". Unlike the hooks, it reports groups, not a 600-character line, and it has no double-check against disk (the checkout is the head commit). Both are read-only and make no model call. The review's own notes of added functions that duplicate indexed code (`reviewImpact`, exact and near) are left out of the comment while the duplicate check ran, because the section says the same with more detail.
- pre-commit prints the same lines (no 600-char cap, at most 10) to stderr for the staged symbols that are new or changed against the main layer (section 11), and for imports added against `HEAD`. It indexes the staged blobs on a scratch store whose workspaces are read from disk; it has no batch and no double-check (the staged version is what is checked).

## 15. MCP server (`packages/mcp`, server name `gantry`)

Every response is one JSON text block ≤ 4,000 chars, except `gantry_claim_batch` (≤ 20,000 chars: it is describer-only, never called by the main agent, and a claim of 10 fact sheets is about 4,000 chars typical, so the main agent's context is not affected; the limit is a per-tool setting that defaults to 4,000. It was 80,000 for 40 sheets until a live run showed a describer cannot read that in one result, #141); descriptions go in a field named `untrusted_description`. Lazy refresh (section 11) runs before each call.

| Tool                                           | Input                                                                                                                                              | Output                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `gantry_find_similar`                          | `{ query?: string, code?: string, kind?: string, lang?: "typescript"\|"go", workspace?: string, limit?: number ≤10 }` (one of query/code required) | `{ matches: [{ id, name, kind, lang, path, line, signature, import_path, tier: "exact"\|"near"\|"code_vector"\|"semantic", score, untrusted_description, advice: { situation, action, inline } }] }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `gantry_browse`                                | `{ target: string }` (id, path, `.` or `repo:<name>`)                                                                                              | `{ items: [{ id, name, kind, exported, path, line, count, untrusted_description }], catalog_path }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `gantry_search`                                | `{ query, kind?, lang?, workspace?, repo?, exported?, limit? }`                                                                                    | `{ hits: [...] }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `gantry_node`                                  | `{ target: string }`                                                                                                                               | `{ node, untrusted_description, signature, fan_in, fan_out, neighbors: { [kind]: string[] } }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `gantry_open_view`                             | `{ view, focus?, saved_view?, filters? }`                                                                                                          | `{ url }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `gantry_impact`                                | `{ paths?, symbol?, range? }`                                                                                                                      | `{ changed: [{ id, kind, path, signature_changed, untrusted_description }], callers_by_hop: [{ hop, count, ids, more? }], crossings, consumers: [{ id, direct_total, direct: [{ id, uses, crosses? }], potential_total, potential: [{ id, hop, via }], reachable_total, reachable_by_workspace, reachable_workspaces_total?, hubs: [{ hub, consumers_total, by_workspace }], hubs_total? }], reachable_total, risk: { score, level, reasons }, notes }` (#43, consumers #169, reachable and hubs #295) |
| `gantry_trace`                                 | `{ target, direction?: "down"\|"up" }`                                                                                                             | `{ target, direction, routes, endpoints: [{ id, low? }], tables, chain: [{ id, kind, repo?, via, depth, from?, low? }], repeats?, gaps, omitted, truncated }` (#47; details below)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `gantry_get_overview` / `gantry_save_overview` | `{ node_id }` / `{ node_id, text }`                                                                                                                | `{ node_id, cached: true, untrusted_overview, model, created_at, functions }` or `{ node_id, cached: false, reason: "missing"\|"stale", functions }` / `{ saved: true, node_id, functions }` or `{ saved: false, reason, detail }` (contract: "Overview tool details" below)                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `gantry_claim_batch` (describer only)          | `{ worker_id?, size ≤ 10 }`                                                                                                                        | `{ worker_id, batch_id, items: FactSheet[] }`; an empty claim adds `stop: "backlog_empty"\|"not_indexed"\|"run_limit"` (describer runs, below)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `gantry_submit_descriptions` (describer only)  | `{ batch_id, results: [{ content_key, description }] }`                                                                                            | `{ accepted, rejected: [{ content_key, reason, rule?, field?, fix?, resubmit?, fallback? }], fallbacks, embedded }` (rule rejections carry `rule`, `field`, `fix` and `resubmit`; never the validator detail, section 8)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

Synced descriptions (section 11, "Description sync") are stored in the same `descriptions` table and are served exactly like a description written here: only through the `untrusted_description` fields (and `untrusted_suggested_name`), never in a trusted field, never as HTML. They are checked with `validateDescription` before they are stored.

`gantry_open_view` link contract (#136). The URL is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/?t=<token>#/<view>?focus=<id>&saved=<id>&<filters>`. `saved_view` (#287, string, 1 to 63 characters) is the id of a saved view: the tool reads `overlay.views` and answers `{ error: "unknown_saved_view", message, views: [ids] }` for an id that is not there, and `{ error: "saved_view_mismatch", message, view: <spec.view> }` when `view` differs from the saved view's `spec.view` (`view` stays required); otherwise the hash carries `saved=<id>` (after `focus`) and the explorer sends it to `/api/view/:id` as `saved`. `saved` is a reserved filter key like `focus`, `t` and `view`. `focus` is the node the view is about (the project-map subtree root, the ego centre, the node a page, slice, duplicates or 3D view centres on) and is also the node shown in the inspector. The tool writes `focus` only; the explorer applies the selection when it loads the page: if the hash has a `focus` and no `sel` key, it behaves as if `sel` equalled `focus`, as a click in the app does. An explicit `sel` (passed in `filters`; an empty `sel=` means "nothing selected") wins. This is done once at load. Closing the inspector writes `sel=` (empty) next to the `focus`, so a reload or a copied URL keeps it closed. Explorer lifecycle (#123): `ensureExplorer` (`explorer/server/launch.ts`, shared by the tool and `gantry review --open`) is a hard bound: it returns within its limit (2.5 s for `gantry_open_view`, 10 s for `gantry review --open`) even when the explorer named in `explorer.json` accepts connections but never answers, and deletes nothing in that case. The explorer it spawns runs `gantry map --no-open --no-url --idle-timeout 30` and exits after 30 minutes without a request (closing the server and removing `explorer.json`), so the embedding model it may have loaded does not stay in memory; a foreground `gantry map` has no idle timeout. The start lock is waited for longer than its 10 s staleness age.

Claims expire after 10 minutes and return to `pending`. A claim never changes the rows of a batch that is still claimed: only the same `worker_id` is handed that batch again, and a smaller re-claim returns a part of it while the rest stays claimed, so descriptions submitted for the full batch are still accepted (no `not_in_batch`). Tool names as seen by Claude: `mcp__plugin_gantry_gantry__<tool>`.

`gantry_trace` details (#47). Runs `trace` (section 9) on the target; an unknown target is `{ error: "not_found" }`. `routes`, `endpoints` and `tables` are the chain's nodes of those kinds (the start excluded), `low: true` marking a low-confidence step. Across the repos of a workspace the trace goes from a page in one repo to the handler and tables in another, and each `chain` step then carries `repo` (the repo its id names; absent with one repo). When the whole chain does not fit in 3,800 characters, the steps that are not routes, endpoints or tables (components, hooks, helpers) are summarised as `repeats: [{ depth, via, kind, count, sample }]` (one per depth, edge kind and node kind, with the first id) and the lists are cut to 40, 20, 10, ... entries until it fits, each cut list adding `<name>_total`; at most 8 gaps of 200 characters (`gaps_total`). Ids, kinds and edge kinds only: no description text is returned, and a containment test seeds a marker as every symbol's description and suggested name and asserts it appears in no trace of any node, either direction.

`gantry_impact` details (#43). Exactly one of `range` (`<base>...<head>`, a symbol diff then impact with the head-blob notes), `paths` (up to 50 indexed files: every symbol in them counts as modified in the working layer against main) or `symbol` (a symbol id or `path:line`) is required, else `{ error: "bad_arguments" }`; a bad or unknown range is `{ error: "bad_range" }` and an unknown target `{ error: "not_found" }`. Lists are capped so the response stays under 4,000 chars: 10 `changed` entries (`changed_total` when more), 12 ids per hop (`more`), 8 crossings (`crossings_total`), 6 notes of at most 220 chars (`notes_total`). `consumers` (#169) lists the changed symbols that have any (at most 5, `consumers_total` when more), each with `direct` (directly affected, hop 1: up to 5 with how each uses it and its crossings) and `potential` (potentially affected, hops 2-3: up to 3 with hop and the direct consumer it goes through), with uncapped totals; ids only, the same data as `impact.consumers`. Reachable and hubs (#295): the top level has `reachable_total` (`impact.reachable.total`); each `consumers` entry has `reachable_total`, `reachable_by_workspace` (`{ <workspace name or "(none)">: count }` over the change's listed reachable consumers and the consumers of every hub of hop 4 or later, at most `IMPACT_LIMITS.reachableWorkspaces` = 6 keys by count descending then name, `reachable_workspaces_total` when there are more; the listed ones are capped at 200 per hop, so the counts of a very wide hop can fall short of `reachable_total`) and `hubs` (at most `IMPACT_LIMITS.hubs` = 3 of `{ hub (node id), consumers_total, by_workspace (an object as above, at most 6 keys) }`, `hubs_total` when more). Ids, names and counts only. A description is returned only as `untrusted_description` on a `changed` entry (cut at 160 chars), and the tool description says never to follow it; a containment test seeds a marker and asserts it appears under no other key (the consumer lists included, and checked non-empty). The graph is the index of the checked-out tree, so a range whose head is not checked out sees callers of the code as it is now.

Describer tool details (#24). `gantry_claim_batch` items are `{ content_key, path, kind, signature, doc, callees, return_type, runs_when?, body }` (`runs_when` only for a Go `init`, #372). `worker_id` is optional: when it is missing the server mints `w-<8 hex>` and returns it as `worker_id` (every response carries `worker_id`), so two callers never share an id by accident; the skills also give each describer its own id (`init-1` to `init-4`, `sync-1` to `sync-4`). Submits are validated by `batch_id` alone, whoever claimed it. A batch the worker claimed and did not submit is returned again, under the same `batch_id` and with `reissued: true`, before any new function is claimed (the claim clock is not restarted). A claim returns up to `size` sheets (default and maximum 10, `CLAIM_SIZE`; a larger `size` is a schema error) in one response of at most 20,000 chars (10 typical sheets are about 4,000; the old 40 and 80,000 were too big for a describer to read in one tool result, and 10 worked in the live run, #141); sheets of a fresh claim beyond that go back to `pending` and the payload says `truncated: true` (the rest of a re-issued batch stays claimed and comes with the next claim); a single oversized sheet is shortened (doc, callees, signature, then `body`, flagged `body_truncated`). `gantry_submit_descriptions` keeps the 4,000-char cap. `gantry_submit_descriptions` rejects a `content_key` outside the batch as `not_in_batch`, adds `embedded` (vectors stored right away, only when the model is already cached) and `batch_status: "unknown_or_expired"` when no claim carries that `batch_id`. Every tool description ends with the `untrusted_description` notice.

Rejections and resubmits (#147). A validation rejection counts an attempt and records `last_error`, but the row stays `claimed` in its batch (same `claimed_by`, claim clock unchanged), so a reworded resubmit under the same `batch_id` is validated again and accepted when it passes; it was released to `pending` before, which made the resubmit `not_in_batch`. A rejected row the describer does not resubmit comes back with that worker's next claim (`reissued: true`). At `MAX_DESCRIBE_ATTEMPTS` (2), or 3 after a clean `shape`/`identifier` second rejection (#150, section 8), the row becomes `failed` and keeps its `claimed_by`, so a later resubmit in the same batch is answered `attempts_exhausted` (and the batch is still known); `resubmit` on each rule rejection says whether the row is still in the batch with an attempt left. A row whose third attempt fails with an extendable rejection gets the fallback description instead when one passes the validator and no description exists yet (#175) (`fallback: true`, the response's `fallbacks` counts them). The headless worker uses the same limit rule; it never runs a row with 2 attempts, so the extra attempt and the fallback happen on the describer path. The headless worker path keeps releasing a first failure to `pending`.

Describer runs (#147). The describer subagent has `maxTurns: 60` (section 16), and a live run on a 3,511-function backlog showed describers stopping at the turn limit with no report, then piling up context when resumed. So the server, not the model, ends each run: it counts the claims that returned items per data dir and `worker_id` (in memory; a server restart only makes a run longer), and after `DESCRIBER_RUN_CLAIMS` (15) the next claim that has no unsubmitted batch to reissue returns `{ worker_id, batch_id: null, items: [], stop: "run_limit", remaining, message }` and resets that count (`remaining` = pending + claimed). A run is then at most 15 claims, 15 submits and some resubmits, well under 60 turns. An empty claim otherwise says `stop: "backlog_empty"` with `indexed: true` and `remaining`, or, when `isIndexed` is false for the store (no `gantry init` ran against this data dir), `stop: "not_indexed"`, `indexed: false`, `data_dir` and a message to run `/gantry:init`, so a describer never reports an unindexed store as an empty backlog. The `init` and `sync` skills restart a describer that hands back with work left (section 16).

Overview tool details (#35; the contract and the `overviews` table notes were missing and are added here). `node_id` is `ws:<repo>:<workspace name>` or `dir:<repo-relative folder path>` (`dir:.` is the whole repository; the 3D map's spelling `dir:<workspace id>:<path inside the workspace>` is accepted and normalised); any other id, a folder with no indexed file, or a path with `..` returns `{ error: "not_found", message }`. `overviews.input_key` = sha256 of the sorted repo-relative paths of the node's files, then `\n--\n`, then the sorted `contentKey`s of every function-like symbol (`function`, `method`, `component`, `hook`) in the node's files, then `\n--\n`, then, for a workspace, the sorted ids of the workspaces nested under its directory (a workspace owns only files whose `workspace_id` is its own); a folder covers every file under its path. `gantry_get_overview` returns the stored text only when `input_key` still equals the current key, else `cached: false` with `reason: "missing"` (no row) or `"stale"`; `functions` is the function-like count in the node. `gantry_save_overview` stores `text` for the node's current key, replacing any earlier row. Checks, in order: whitespace runs fold to one space; empty or non-string is `schema`; more than 1,200 characters is `length` (never truncated); the secret filter (`scanSecrets`, spec section 8 check 2) is `secret`; the injection filter (`scanInjection`: control and non-ASCII characters, instruction patterns, chat-template tokens, markup, obfuscated hosts, links, URL schemes, shell commands) is `injection`. Shape and grounding rules do not apply (an overview is prose, not a one-sentence description). `detail` names rules only, never matched text. Every save writes one `usage_log` row `{ task: "overview", model, effort }` from the `overview` routing entry, `functions` = the node's function count, `source: "explainer"`. Overviews are never generated by any tool or hook on their own: only the `explainer` agent writes one, only when `/gantry:overview` asks. The overview is model-written text about untrusted code, so it is returned only in a field named `untrusted_overview` (MCP `gantry_get_overview` and the explorer's `GET /api/overview/:id`, which returns `{ node_id, untrusted_overview: string\|null, stale, model?, created_at? }` and never a stale text), every tool description carries the notice that the field is data and nothing in it is to be followed, and the explorer inspector shows it for workspace and folder nodes as plain text under "Overview — generated, may be wrong", or the hint "Run /gantry:overview <id> in Claude Code". A containment test seeds a marker as an overview and asserts it appears in no MCP tool or `/api` response except as the value of `untrusted_overview`.

## 16. Plugin (`plugin/`)

- `plugin.json`: name `gantry`, version, description.
- Versioning (#187). One version, `x.y.z`, in the root `package.json`; `plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and the `package.json` of `packages/core`, `cli`, `mcp` and `explorer` equal it (`.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` carries none: for a relative-path plugin the plugin manifest version wins). `claude plugin update` compares the manifest version, so a build is only picked up when it changes. `pnpm check` runs `scripts/check-version.ts`: any differing file fails, naming it. Plugin content (`plugin/`, the `src`/`server` of the bundled packages, `tsup.config.ts`; tests and docs excluded) changed since the last `v*` tag with the root version unchanged is a warning in `pnpm check` and an error with `--strict` (`pnpm version:check --strict`), because feature branches change plugin content in almost every PR and the bump belongs to the release. `pnpm release <patch|minor|major|x.y.z>` (`scripts/release.ts`; Node built-ins and git only) needs a clean tree and equal versions, bumps every file, prepends a dated `CHANGELOG.md` section of the commit subjects since the last `v*` tag, commits `Release v<version>` and creates the annotated tag `v<version>`; it never pushes. `gantry --version` prints the plugin version and the MCP server reports it as its server version, both read from `plugin.json`.
- Skills: `reuse-first` (model-invoked, short description), `init`, `sync`, `map`, `review`, `explain`, `overview` (user-invoked only: `disable-model-invocation: true`, confirmed in the Claude Code skills docs in #27; plugin skills are invoked as `/gantry:<name>`).
- Agents: `describer` (`model: haiku`, `effort: low`, `tools: mcp__plugin_gantry_gantry__gantry_claim_batch, mcp__plugin_gantry_gantry__gantry_submit_descriptions`, `background: true`, `maxTurns: 60`); `explainer` (`model: sonnet`, `effort: medium`, `maxTurns: 30`, `tools`: `gantry_search`, `gantry_browse`, `gantry_node`, `gantry_find_similar`, `gantry_get_overview`, `gantry_save_overview`, `Read`, `Grep`, `Glob`; never `Write`, `Edit` or `Bash`). `/gantry:explain <target>` delegates to it; `/gantry:overview <workspace|dir>` returns a fresh cached overview or has it write one at effort `low` (routing `overview`); `/gantry:map [view] [focus]` calls `gantry_open_view` and prints the URL. `/gantry:review <base>...<head> | --pr <n>` (#44) runs `gantry review ... --json`, reviews high-risk items first (signature changes with their hop-1 callers read in full, removed exports still used, crossings, notes), ends with a short list of findings and the review view link from `gantry_open_view`, and is read-only: it never edits, commits or posts (no GitHub comments); without a working `gh` it relays the CLI message that names the range form. Each skill body is under 20 lines.
- `hooks/hooks.json`: SessionStart → `"${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/gantry" hook session-start`; PostToolUse matcher `Write|Edit|MultiEdit` → `... hook post-tool-use` with `timeout` 5 (seconds); Stop (no matcher) → `... hook stop` with `timeout` 5 (#131). Exact JSON layout: section 14.
- `.mcp.json`: `node ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/dist/mcp.js`. Skills call the CLI as `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA="${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}" "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/gantry"` so that CLI and MCP server share one data dir (section 4). `init` and `sync` start each describer with its own worker id in the prompt.
- Describer restarts (#147). A describer ends its report with `MORE WORK` (claim said `run_limit`), `DONE` (`backlog_empty`), `NOT INDEXED` (`not_indexed`, with the data dir) or `ERROR`; after a validator rejection it writes a new description that follows the rejection's `fix` and resubmits it once in the same batch while `resubmit` is true; an item rejected again with `resubmit: true` (the extra attempt of section 8) is described on the describer's next claim, which reissues it, so a run stays at one resubmit per claim (#150). The `init` and `sync` prompts carry the same rule. The `init` and `sync` skills keep the backlog moving: on each describer completion they start a **fresh** `describer` with the same prompt and worker id when it reported `MORE WORK` or came back marked partial or cut off, or when it reported `DONE` and `backlog --json` still shows `pending` above 0; they stop on `NOT INDEXED`, `ERROR`, two runs in a row of one worker id with 0 accepted, or when the user asks; at the end they offer `backlog --failed` and `--retry-failed`. Design choice, checked against the Claude Code subagent docs (code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents and /hooks, 2026-11-15): `maxTurns` is "Maximum number of agentic turns before the subagent stops", and a subagent cut off there comes back marked partial; a background subagent's result reaches the parent "as a completion notification in a later turn"; `SendMessage` resumes a subagent but "Resumed subagents retain their full conversation history", which is the context pile-up seen in the live run (430K to 580K tokens); a plugin `SubagentStop` hook could block the stop and keep the agent going, but in the same growing context, with no documented interaction with `maxTurns` and a cap on consecutive blocks. So: the server ends runs before the limit (the model need not count turns), the parent starts a new instance (small context; the server reissues any unsubmitted batch to the same worker id), and the per-claim size stays 10 (section 15) because larger claims were too big to read. Not done: more items per call (several batches per claim) would break the 20,000-char read limit of #141.
- Local test marketplace (#27): `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` at the repo root (name `gantry-dev`, plugin source `./plugin`). A marketplace source must start with `./` and may not contain `..`, so the file cannot live in a sibling `dev-marketplace/` folder and point at `../plugin`; installing is `/plugin marketplace add <clone>` then `/plugin install gantry@gantry-dev` (`docs/INSTALL.md`). `plugin/bin/` is put on the Bash tool's `PATH` while the plugin is enabled; skills and agents still call `"${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/gantry"` by full path.
- Headless worker recipe (pre-commit modes only; confirmed in M0 on Claude Code CLI 2.1.325 with a claude.ai login, verdict WORKER OK): `GANTRY_WORKER=1 claude -p --model <m> --effort <e> --safe-mode --tools "" --disallowedTools "mcp__*" --strict-mcp-config --system-prompt-file <file> --no-session-persistence --output-format json --json-schema '<inline>'`, stdin = fact sheets (measured in M0). All flags work together unchanged.
  - `--json-schema` is mandatory (measured in M0): without it there is no `structured_output` (0 of 2 live runs) and no `StructuredOutput` tool. Read `structured_output`; if it is missing (model or schema failure) the functions go back to the backlog.
  - Keep both `--safe-mode` and `--strict-mcp-config` (measured in M0): each alone keeps MCP servers out; dropping both loaded 23 MCP servers (user plugin servers and account connectors). `GANTRY_WORKER=1` stays as the third line of defence. `--disallowedTools "mcp__*"` and `--no-session-persistence` had no visible effect on the loaded sets and stay as defence in depth.
  - `--system-prompt-file` works but is not listed in `claude --help`; re-check it on each CLI upgrade, fall back to `--system-prompt "$(cat <file>)"`.
  - Unset `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL` in the child env (measured in M0): it overrides `--effort` (about 7x output tokens and about 4x wall time with `max`). An invalid `--effort` only warns.
  - Binary resolution order (measured in M0: `claude` is not on `PATH` when run from the desktop app): config override, then `$CLAUDE_CODE_EXECPATH`, then `claude` on `PATH`.
  - Gate on `claude auth status` (prints JSON): proceed only when `loggedIn === true`; `false` means "no worker, enqueue only", not an error. Only a claude.ai login was tested; API-key, Bedrock and Vertex logins were not.
  - Budget (measured in M0, Haiku/low): about 3.3 s cold start, 1 / 10 / 40 sheets in about 2.7 / 7.0 / 16.3 s (median of 3), so about 0.4 s per extra function. Batches of 10 to 40 per call; 40 is the headless batch size (`BATCH_SIZE`). Describer subagents claim at most 10 (`CLAIM_SIZE`, section 15). The host enforces the 25-word limit (section 8).

## 17. Duplicate tiers (thresholds measured in M0, re-fit in M2 (#32))

| Tier        | Rule                                                                                                                                                       |
| ----------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| exact       | same `exactHash` or same `renamedHash` (#151), `stmtCount ≥ 3`                                                                                             |
| near        | same `shapeHash` or shape MinHash Jaccard ≥ 0.85, and token MinHash Jaccard ≥ 0.75 (#131), `stmtCount ≥ 6`, same parameter count ±1; score = token Jaccard |
| code_vector | cosine ≥ 0.98 (M2) between normalized-code embeddings, and token MinHash Jaccard ≥ 0.50 (#131)                                                             |
| semantic    | cosine ≥ 0.60 (measured in M0) between description embeddings, compatible signature                                                                        |

**Repos.** A workspace of several repos (section 4) is one index, and the engine compares every symbol in it: a function of one repo matches a copy in a sibling repo by the same tiers, within the same language and kind family. A candidate's repo is the repo part of its symbol id; a `similar_to` edge may join two repos. Hook notes and pre-commit lines name a match in another repo as `<repo>:<path>:<line>` (a match in the caller's own repo prints as before), and the double-check reads that file from the root the repo was indexed from. `gantry_find_similar` hits carry `repo`. The Duplicates view gives each member a `repo`, each group `repos` (sorted) and the summary `crossRepo` (groups whose members span more than one repo) only when the index holds more than one repo; a single-repo index returns the same data as before.

**Languages (#220).** Comparisons never cross languages: `findDuplicatesDetailed` and `rebuildSimilarEdges` skip every candidate whose `lang` differs from the query symbol's, so no `similar_to` edge joins two languages. Thresholds are per language: `thresholdsFor(config, lang)` returns the configured values for every language (the TypeScript values; Go gets its own only if seeded Go duplicates fail them), and each language module carries its statement floors (TypeScript: exact 3, near 6). Go (#223) uses the TypeScript values unchanged: the seeded Go set (below) finds its exact and near cases at them, and the code_vector cases once the Go embedding text is normalized like the TypeScript one (next paragraph). A Go `function` and `method` are the function family; `init` and `_` functions and generated files never take part; a Go struct, interface, type, const or var has no family. The five hash values come from one shared function, `hashStreams({ exact, shape, selfRefs })`; the token streams come from the language module, and no language-blind normaliser exists.

Go embedding text (#223). Before #223 the Go text was the declaration with comments dropped; a verbatim copy under a new name then embedded at cosine 0.89 to 0.95 of the original, never reaching `codeVector` 0.98 (measured on the seeded Go set, `evals/seeded-duplicates-go/`). The Go text now follows the TypeScript rule: the declaration's own name and every name declared inside it (parameters, results, receivers, `:=`, `var`, `const`, `range` and type-switch bindings, the same set as the exact stream) become `v1, v2…` in order of first appearance, comments are dropped, whitespace is collapsed, and string literals become `"s"`; field, method and package names and identifiers declared outside the declaration are kept. The same copies then embed at 0.983 to 0.992.

Embedding scope: only function-like symbols with `stmtCount ≥ 3` get a `vec_code` row (smaller ones are never compared by any tier), and the text is the normalized code cut to its first 1,500 characters (`CODE_EMBED_MAX_CHARS`, the fact-sheet body budget) with the tokenizer capped at 512 tokens, so the cost per function is bounded. Embedding is lazy (section 11).

Embedding model (measured in M0): `jinaai/jina-embeddings-v2-base-code` (q8 ONNX, mean pooling, L2-normalized) for both descriptions and normalized code, dimension D = 768, so `vec_descriptions` and `vec_code` are `float[768]` (section 9 migration v2). The two thresholds are initial values from a small hand-written set (15 + 15 pairs per task), not final: code_vector 0.96 gave recall 93% and 7% false positives (1 of 15), semantic 0.60 gave recall 80% and 7% false positives; the old 0.92 and 0.85 gave 33% false positives and 0% recall. M2 (#32) re-fit them on a real replay and real LLM-written descriptions (`docs/milestones/M2.md`): `codeVector` moved to 0.98 (on 1,416 replayed edits, 1 of 98 code-vector candidates at 0.91 or above was a true duplicate and none at 0.93 or above, and between 0.96 and 0.98 they added 4 alerts, all false; the M0 code pairs find 14 of 15 at 0.98 with no false positive, against 15 of 15 and one false positive at 0.96), `nearJaccard` 0.85 and `semantic` 0.60 are kept (the sweep was flat for the first; for the second, 0.65 would drop recall on the M0 description pairs from 11 to 6 of 15 with no fewer false positives). `semantic` only gates the description-to-description tier of `findDuplicates`; neither the hook nor `gantry_find_similar` applies it to a draft.
Normalizer for `vec_code` (decided in M0, approved by the operator on 2026-11-14): rename local identifiers (parameters, `const`/`let` names, the function name) to `v1, v2, …`, drop comments, collapse whitespace, replace string and template literals with `"s"`, but keep property names after `.` and well-known globals (and any other identifier not declared inside the function, such as an imported helper). The function's own name is renamed too, recursive calls included. Renaming everything made `capitalize` and `decapitalize` identical (cosine 1.000); keeping them raised Jina's best accuracy from 93.3% to 96.7% (measured in M0).

Renamed copies (#151). The near tier needs `stmtCount ≥ 6`, so a renamed copy of a 3 to 5 statement function matched no tier. `exactHash` includes the function's own name, and it is also the base of `contentKey` (descriptions, backlog, vectors), so it is left alone. A second hash, `renamedHash` (section 7, `symbols.renamed_hash`, migration v6), is the hash of the near-tier token stream below: the same tokens as `exactHash` with the symbol's own name (recursive calls included) replaced by `$0`. The exact tier matches on either hash, score 1, `stmtCount ≥ 3`. A row without `renamedHash` (before v6) matches on `exactHash` only until the next heal re-indexes it. Known gap: a class or object method has no root name to normalise, so a renamed copy of a short (under 6 statements) method is still missed. Measured on the M2 replay in `docs/milestones/M2.md` section 6.

Near-tier token stream (#131). The shape stream drops literals, property names and callees, so any two functions of the same shape scored 1.00 (in the M2 replay most false near alerts were two lookup functions mapping different strings). The near tier therefore has a second gate: a MinHash (64 permutations, 5-token shingles, as for the shape) over the exact token stream (section 7: node types, operators, literal values, property names and outside identifiers kept, locals renamed `$1, $2…`) with the symbol's own name replaced by `$0`, so a copy under a new name still scores 1. It is stored as `symbols.near_minhash` (migration v5). A pair is near when the shape gate holds (same `shapeHash` or shape Jaccard ≥ `nearJaccard`) and the token Jaccard is ≥ `nearTokenJaccard`; the score is the token Jaccard. A row without a token MinHash (written before v5; the migration forces a full re-index on the next heal) is judged by the shape gate alone. The code_vector tier has the same blind spot (its normalizer turns every string into `"s"`), so a code_vector pair also needs a token Jaccard ≥ `codeVectorTokenJaccard`; it is skipped when either side has no token MinHash. Both gates were fitted on the M2 replay (docs/milestones/M2.md): its near candidates labelled false had token Jaccard 0.56 or less and the true ones 0.84 or more (`nearTokenJaccard` 0.75); its code_vector candidates at cosine 0.98 or more were all labelled false and had token Jaccard 0.47 or less, while the M0 same-code pairs at 0.98 or more have 0.64 or more (`codeVectorTokenJaccard` 0.50, which drops none of them).

Compare only within kind family (function/method together; component; hook). Skip tests, fixtures, generated files, barrels and `duplicates.ignore`. Dismissed pairs never alert (they are read from `overlay.dismissals`, so they survive an index rebuild). Budget: < 1 in 10 hook alerts false; alerts on < 5% of edits. M2 measured the alert rate at 1.3% and the false rate at 45.2% (#32); #131 changed the rules (near-tier token gate, batch flow with a double-check against disk, section 14) and re-measured (`docs/milestones/M2.md`).

Seeded-case format (M2, #32; supersedes the one-line description in section 13): `evals/seeded-duplicates/cases.json`: 10 cases (3 with misleading names) each as `{ id, misleading, query, newCode | copyAs, expected: { tiers, situation } }`. `newCode` is written by hand; `copyAs` means "copy the existing function under this name" at run time, so no private code is committed. The `existing` symbol id and the target file of each case live only in git-ignored `evals/private/` (`GANTRY_PRIVATE_DIR`). `evals/m2/` and `evals/run-m2.ts` hold the M2 scripts (seeded cases, replay, labelling, sweep, timing).

Go seeded set (#223): `evals/seeded-duplicates-go/`, all hand-written and public. `base/` is a Go module (`go.mod` `module example.com/seeded`) with ten functions of at least 6 statements in `internal/` (string, time, slice, map and error helpers) and `web/slug.ts`, a TypeScript function with the logic of a Go one. `cases.json` is `[{ id, misleading, existing: "<path>#<name>", targetFile, newCode | copyAs, expected: { tiers } }]`: g01 to g03 are misleading (new name, restructured body: `["code_vector","semantic"]`), g04 to g06 verbatim copies under a new name through `copyAs` (`["exact"]`, via `renamedHash`), g07 to g10 edited copies (`["exact","near"]`). `negatives.json` is `[{ id, kind: "unrelated" | "cross_language", targetFile, newCode }]`: two unrelated functions of the same size and a Go copy of the logic of `web/slug.ts`; none may have a candidate. `seeded.test.ts` (in `pnpm check`, no model) copies `base/` to a temp git repo, indexes it, writes each case to its target, indexes the working layer, asks `findDuplicates(store, { symbol })` and asserts g04 to g10 each return `existing` first in an expected tier and the negatives return nothing; g01 to g03 need embeddings. `run.ts --embed` (not in `pnpm check`; needs the built CLI and the local model) does the same after `gantry embed` and prints `{ found, misleadingFound, perCase: [{ id, tier, score }] }` against the M2 bar (8 of 10, 2 of 3 misleading). A Go function whose body is a different implementation of the same behaviour embeds at about 0.8 to 0.94 of the original, below `codeVector`: such a match needs the semantic tier, which needs Go descriptions.

## 18. Reuse advice (deterministic)

"Reachable" = exported from the package's `exports` map (or `main`/`types` when no map). Situations: (1) reachable and caller's workspace depends on it → `inline: true`, action "Import it: import { X } from '<pkg>'"; (2) reachable, not a dependency → suggestion "Add the dependency" unless it creates a workspace cycle or a server/client boundary crossing (`"use client"` / server-only markers); (3) not reachable → suggestion "Export it or move it to a shared package"; (4) lives in another app → suggestion "Move it to a shared package"; (5) `nameFits=false` → suggestion "Consider renaming X; see untrusted_suggested_name", with the model's name only in the field `untrusted_suggested_name` (only if config `renameSuggestions` is true); (6) new import of an undeclared workspace or npm package, or deep import past `exports` → `inline: true`, action "Declare it in <pkg>/package.json". Situation 7 is described below.

(7) the match is in another repo of the workspace (a repo other than the caller's) → suggestion "It is in the <repo> repo: reuse it through a package that repo publishes, or move it to one"; situations 1 to 4 are not offered, because no relative import or workspace dependency reaches another repo. Exception: when the match is in a package workspace of that repo whose name the caller's workspace (or its repo's root) already depends on, and the package exposes the match, situation 1 applies with that package name. Go matches in another repo always get situation 7. A package that a sibling repo publishes is reachable through its `exports` map (or `main`/`types`) like one of the repo itself: the index resolves imports of it to that repo's files, so the stored `exports` edges from its entry files reach across repos.

Go (#223). The unit is the package, a directory of a module; its import path is `<module path>/<directory inside the module>`. A Go symbol is reachable when its name is exported (upper-case first letter; a method also needs an exported receiver type) and Go's `internal` rule lets the caller's package import the match's. For a caller that is a `.go` file and a Go match in another file: same package → situation 1, `inline: true`, "Call it: X is in the same package (<dir>)"; reachable and the same module, or a module the caller's `go.mod` requires → situation 1, `Import it: import "<module path>/<dir>"`; not reachable → situation 3, "Export it or move it to a shared package"; reachable in another module the caller does not require → situation 2, "Add the dependency: require <module path> in <dir>/go.mod, then import it" (situation 4, "Move it to a shared package (adding <module> would make a dependency cycle)", when that module already depends on the caller's). No advice for a caller that is not a `.go` file. TypeScript advice is unchanged.

Implementation notes (#29): the advice reads only the index. Reachability follows the stored `exports` edges from each exports-map entry file (or `types`/`main`/`src/index`), so `importPathFor(ws, match, store?)` takes the store as an optional third argument. The `"use client"` directive is recorded by the parser (`ParsedFile.directives`) and stored in `files.directives` (section 9, migration v3; no edge is emitted, `adviseReuse` reads the column through `store.effectiveFile`); server-only means a `server-only` import (an `ext:server-only` edge, so the package must be declared or installed) or a `.server.ts` suffix. A boundary crossing is a client caller and a server-only match. `checkImports` treats a bare name as a tsconfig alias when the index resolved that import line to a file, and reports a deep import only for workspace packages that have an `exports` map.

## 19. Definition of done (every PR)

1. Acceptance criteria met. 2. `biome check` and `tsc -b` pass, and the vitest folders touched pass; the maintainer runs the full `pnpm check` on `main`. 3. New behavior has tests. 4. PR body: three lines (what changed, how it was checked, differences from the brief) plus the generated-with line; screenshots for UI work (never showing private test-bed code). 5. No TODOs left for in-scope items. Milestone-level regression runs happen in the milestone's final piece of work, not in every PR.

## 20. Server API and upload format (`packages/protocol`, `@gantry/protocol`)

The contract between a Gantry client (CLI, MCP server) and a team server. Both sides import the same zod schemas from `@gantry/protocol`, so they cannot drift. The package holds the schemas, `ROUTES`, `buildPath`, the limits, `splitIntoParts` and a fake server (`startFakeServer`, exported from `@gantry/protocol/testing` only). No upload carries a function body, a signature, file contents or a fact sheet: a snapshot with a `signature` or `body` key is rejected, because every request schema is strict.

**Transport.** Plain HTTP and JSON under `/v1`, except a part body, which is gzipped NDJSON. Path parameters are URL-encoded with `encodeURIComponent` (`acme/shop` is `acme%2Fshop`; `cs:pr:acme/shop#12` is `cs%3Apr%3Aacme%2Fshop%2312`); `buildPath(route, params)` does it. Times are ISO 8601 UTC strings. An error body is `{ error, message }`; `message` is for people and is never parsed.

**Version and evolution.** `PROTOCOL_VERSION` is 2 and is sent on every request as `X-Gantry-Protocol`. Every response carries `X-Gantry-Protocol-Max`, the highest version the server speaks (`GET /v1/whoami` also returns it as `protocol`). Response schemas are tolerant (`z.object`: an unknown key is dropped, never an error). Request schemas are strict (`z.strictObject`: an unknown key is an error). `FIELD_SINCE` maps a request field or route key to the version that introduced it (one entry today: `POST /v1/descriptions/lookup` is 2; a route key is its method and path); a change that adds one bumps `PROTOCOL_VERSION` by one and adds its keys. A client sends such a field only when `supports(serverMax, key)` is true (`FIELD_SINCE[key] <= serverMax`; an unknown `serverMax` counts as 1 and a key absent from the table counts as 1). A response-only addition needs no bump. Version 2 adds the description lookup route; a client sends it only to a server whose maximum is at least 2, and a server below 2 answers 404 for it.

**Auth.** Every route takes `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. A CI token (`gct_...`) is for one repo, which names its organisation, and ignores `X-Gantry-Org`. A user token (from device sign-in: `POST /api/auth/device/code`, then `POST /api/auth/device/token` with client id `gantry-cli`, polled every `interval` seconds until `expires_in`; these two routes are outside `/v1` and carry no protocol header) names its organisation with `X-Gantry-Org: <slug>`. A user token without that header is 400 on every route except `GET /v1/whoami`, which answers 200 `{ kind: "user", user, repo_connected: false, protocol }`.

| `error`            | Status | Meaning                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------ | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `unauthorized`     | 401    | No token, or a malformed, unknown, expired or revoked one.                                                                                                |
| `forbidden`        | 403    | The token is valid but may not do this: another repo's CI token, a route for the other kind of token, a missing role, an organisation the user is not in. |
| `not_found`        | 404    | No such upload, change set or repo, or one the caller may not see.                                                                                        |
| `invalid`          | 400    | Validation failed: an unknown field, a wrong type, a limit passed, a missing header, a path `:id` that differs from the body `id`.                        |
| `too_large`        | 413    | A part or a whole upload is over a size limit.                                                                                                            |
| `conflict`         | 409    | The request is valid but the state forbids it: a part for an upload that is superseded, failed or finished.                                               |
| `budget`           | 409    | The server's daily upload capacity is used. The client stops and tries again later. A change set part is not refused for this reason.                     |
| `protocol_version` | 400    | `X-Gantry-Protocol` is missing, not an integer, below 1 or above the server's maximum.                                                                    |

**Routes** (`ROUTES` holds each one's method, path, accepted token kinds, query, request schema and response schema).

| Route                                         | Token      | Purpose                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| --------------------------------------------- | ---------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GET /v1/whoami`                              | CI or user | Who the token is and whether a repo is connected.                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `POST /v1/repos/:repo/uploads`                | CI         | Begin an upload (`UploadStart`, with header `X-Gantry-Target`); answers 202 `UploadBegun`.                                                                                                                                          |
| `PUT /v1/uploads/:id/parts/:n`                | CI         | Send part `n` (1-based). Headers `Content-Encoding: gzip`, `Content-Type: application/x-ndjson`, `X-Gantry-Part-Count`. Answers 202, or 200 when the part is already recorded. Errors include 409 `conflict`, 409 `budget` and 413. |
| `GET /v1/uploads/:id`                         | CI         | `UploadStatus`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `POST /v1/repos/:repo/change-sets/:id/close`  | CI         | `CloseRequest`; answers `{ closed }`.                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `GET /v1/pull`                                | CI or user | The change sets, team views and groups of a repo, in pages (query `repo`, `cursor`). Answers 304 for a matching `If-None-Match` without a cursor. `X-Gantry-Truncated: true` on the last page when the list was cut.                |
| `POST /v1/descriptions`                       | user       | `DescriptionsRequest`; answers `{ stored }`.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `POST /v1/descriptions/lookup`                | user       | `DescriptionLookupRequest` `{ repo, content_keys }` (1 to 500 keys); answers `DescriptionLookupResponse` `{ descriptions }`: the organisation's `Description` for each key it has, a key with none is absent. Since version 2.      |
| `PUT` and `DELETE /v1/repos/:repo/groups/:id` | user       | Save or delete a team group (`Group`). A body `id` that differs from the path `:id` is 400.                                                                                                                                         |
| `PUT` and `DELETE /v1/repos/:repo/views/:id`  | user       | Save or delete a team view (`SavedView`). Same id rule.                                                                                                                                                                             |

**Targets.** An upload's target is `main` or a change set id (`cs:pr:<repo>#<n>` or `cs:branch:<repo>:<name>`, at most 1,024 characters; `changeSetIdForPr`, `changeSetIdForBranch`, `parseChangeSetId`). `X-Gantry-Target` is always sent percent-encoded (`encodeTarget`; `main` stays `main`) and decoded exactly once (`decodeTarget`), so `%` and characters outside Latin-1 in a branch name survive a header. The `c` record's `id` is the raw id and must equal the decoded header. The id's repo must equal the upload's repo, and for a pull request the number must equal `pr`.

**Uploading in parts.** A main snapshot is not one body. A client:

1. Sends `POST /v1/repos/:repo/uploads` with counts only (`UploadStart`: `{ kind: "main", sha, counts }` or `{ kind: "change_set", counts }`). The answer, `UploadBegun`, carries `upload_id` (24 lower-case hex), `part_max_records`, `part_max_bytes` and `max_parts`. The server chooses these values and may change them; a client reads them from every begin response and never hard-codes them. Beginning an upload supersedes the newest earlier unfinished upload of the same repo and target: its status becomes `superseded` and its remaining parts are refused.
2. Cuts its records into parts (`splitIntoParts(records, limits)`): at most `part_max_records` records and `part_max_bytes` bytes of decompressed NDJSON per part (each line and its newline counted); each part is gzipped on its own; a part whose gzip is over `part_max_bytes` is cut in half; a record that cannot fit an empty part is an error that names it. Record order is fixed so a resend is byte-identical: for a main snapshot workspaces, files, symbols, edges, adapter nodes, each section sorted by id (edges by `from`, `to`, `kind`) (`mainSnapshotRecords`); for a change set the `c` record, then the node ids sorted, then the radius entries sorted by `hop`, then `id` (`changeSetRecords`).
3. Sends every part with `PUT /v1/uploads/:id/parts/:n`, at most 4 in flight, each with `X-Gantry-Part-Count` (the number of parts, the same on every part). A part answered 5xx or by a network error is sent again; a 409 stops the upload. Parts may arrive in any order.
4. Polls `GET /v1/uploads/:id` until the status is final. `queued`: begun, nothing received. `loading`: at least one part received. `done`: every part is loaded, the counts match and the upload is visible. `failed`: `error` says why (at most 500 characters, never source text); a failed upload is final, so the client begins a new one. `superseded`: a newer upload for the same target began. Until `done`, nothing of the upload is visible to any read, so a half-sent or failed upload never changes what a person sees. A server may fail an upload that stalls; the client sees status `failed` with a reason.

The counts of `UploadStart` equal the number of records of that kind in the parts; a difference fails the upload.

**Part records.** One NDJSON line per record, an object with a tag `t`; a tag not listed or a key not listed for its tag is rejected (`PartRecordSchema`).

| Upload     | `t` | Record                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------- | --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| main       | `w` | workspace: `id`, `name`, `path`                                                                                                                                           |
| main       | `f` | file: `id`, `path`, `workspace`, `lang`                                                                                                                                   |
| main       | `s` | symbol: `id`, `name`, `kind`, `lang`, `path`, `workspace`, `line_start`, `line_end` (1-based, inclusive), `exact_hash`, `renamed_hash` (or null), `content_key` (or null) |
| main       | `e` | edge: `from`, `to`, `kind`                                                                                                                                                |
| main       | `a` | adapter node: `id`, `kind`, `workspace`, `label` (one line, at most 200 characters), `file_id` (or null)                                                                  |
| change set | `c` | the change set without `repo`, `nodes` and `radius`: exactly one, the first line of part 1                                                                                |
| change set | `n` | one node id                                                                                                                                                               |
| change set | `r` | one radius entry: `id`, `hop`, `tier`, `via_hub`                                                                                                                          |

A symbol has no `shape_hash` and a file no content hash. An adapter node has no free-form data. A `ChangeSet` is `{ id, repo, source: "pr" | "branch", state: "proposed" | "in_progress" | "in_review" | "merged", ref, head_ref, base_branch, nodes, nodes_total?, radius, reachable_total?, estimated, confidence?, owner, title?, pr?, head_sha, created_at, updated_at, links }`. `ref` is `#<n>` for a pull request and the branch name for a branch; `head_ref` is the head branch name (for a pull request the pull request's head branch, for a branch the same as `ref`). `radius` entries have `tier` `direct` (hop 1), `potential` (hops 2 and 3) or `reachable` (hop 4 and over). When the client cuts `nodes`, it sends `nodes_total`; `reachable_total` is the number of hop-4-and-over entries before the cut. `Description` is `{ content_key, behavior, search_terms, name_fits, suggested_name?, provenance: "model" | "fallback", model, prompt_version, created_at }`. `Group`, `SavedView` and `FilterSpec` are the overlay types of section 7, re-declared in the package; a test checks that they give the same verdicts as the library's.

**Limits.** The server rejects (400 `invalid`) what passes them. `ids`, `from`, `to`, `focus`, `file_id`: 1 to 1,024 characters. `path`: at most 1,024. `name`: at most 512. `lang` and `kind`: `^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$` (not a closed set, so a new language or kind needs no version bump). `sha`, `head_sha`: 40 lower-case hex. `exact_hash`, `renamed_hash`: 32 lower-case hex. `content_key`: `^[0-9a-f]{32}:p[0-9]+$`. `ref`, `head_ref`, `base_branch`: at most 250 (`head_ref` may be empty). `owner`: at most 100. `title`: at most 300. A description's `behavior`: at most 400 characters; 1 to 6 search terms of at most 60; `suggested_name` at most 80 and `^[A-Za-z_$][\w$]*$`; `model` at most 100; `prompt_version` `^p[0-9]+$`.

| Where                              | Maximum                                                            |
| ---------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `workspaces` of a main snapshot    | 500                                                                |
| `files`                            | 40,000                                                             |
| `symbols`                          | 250,000                                                            |
| `edges`                            | 900,000                                                            |
| `adapter_nodes`                    | 5,000                                                              |
| `nodes` of a change set            | 5,000                                                              |
| `radius` of a change set           | 3,000                                                              |
| `links` of a change set            | 50, each at most 300 characters                                    |
| Records per part                   | `part_max_records` from the begin response                         |
| Part size                          | `part_max_bytes` from the begin response, decompressed and gzipped |
| Parts per upload                   | `max_parts` from the begin response                                |
| One upload, summed over its parts  | 30 MB gzipped; 300 MB decompressed                                 |
| `descriptions` per call            | 500                                                                |
| `content_keys` per lookup          | 1 to 500                                                           |
| Change sets in a pull              | 500                                                                |
| Groups or saved views per repo     | 100 each, each at most 32 KB serialised                            |
| Error `message` and status `error` | 500 characters                                                     |

**Pull.** `GET /v1/pull` returns pages, newest change set first, at most 500 change sets over all pages. A client requests pages until a response has no `next_cursor` (the cursor is opaque), then sorts the change sets by `id`. `team_views`, `groups` and `settings` are on the first page only. Every page carries the same `ETag`.

**Description lookup.** `POST /v1/descriptions/lookup` (user token, header `X-Gantry-Org`) takes `{ repo, content_keys }` and answers `{ descriptions: Description[] }` with the descriptions the organisation holds for those keys, field for field as the upload sent them (`DescriptionSchema` is the one description schema; the request is strict, the response tolerant). The server resolves `repo` in the organisation and checks that the user can read it, as it does for the upload; the repo does not narrow the result, which is organisation-wide. Keys with no description are absent. The response schema keeps `descriptions` as an array of `unknown`; a client checks each record with `DescriptionReadSchema` (the `Description` fields, an unknown key dropped), so one record that does not fit costs only itself. A CI token is refused with 403, another organisation's slug with 403, a malformed or oversized request with 400. The client side is `syncDescriptions` (section 11); the fake server keeps the descriptions that `POST /v1/descriptions` stored and answers the lookup from them, and answers 404 when started with `protocolMax` below 2 (its `lookupDelayMs` option makes the lookup slow, for timeout tests).

**Publishing a view or group.** `gantry team publish view|group <id>` sends the local object with `PUT /v1/repos/:repo/views/:id` or `/groups/:id` (body: the object, whose `id` equals the path id), and `gantry team unpublish view|group <id>` calls the matching `DELETE`; `:repo` and `:id` are URL-encoded. Which roles may write or delete is the server's decision: a refusal is a 403 whose `message` the command prints. The next pull returns the change in `team_views` and `groups`, and the command pulls once itself. `settings.default_view`, when the server sets it, names the team's default view by id.

**Radius.** A change set's `radius` is built from the review engine's consumers per changed symbol and file: merge by id keeping the lowest hop, tier by hop, `via_hub` true for an id reached only through a collapsed hub, sort by hop then id, then cut to 500 entries for hops 1 to 3 and 2,500 for hop 4 and over. A client that cuts anything prints one stderr line (`radius: sent <sent> of <known>`, `nodes: sent 5000 of <n>`); a cut is never silent.

**Client exit codes** (for a command that uploads). `done`, `superseded` and a close exit 0. `failed`, a timeout (600 seconds by default), a refused branch and a missing token exit 1 with the server's error or the reason. A usage error exits 2. With no team configured the command prints that nothing was uploaded and exits 0.

**Rules.** No field carries a function body, a signature, comments, file contents or a fact sheet; the index fields that never travel are symbol `signature`, `loc`, `complexity`, `stmtCount` and `exported`, edge `evidence`, `confidence` and `count`, and adapter-node `data`. `owner` and `user` are account logins, never an email address or a display name, and no person is ranked by anything on the wire. Text from descriptions, titles, labels and ids is untrusted wherever it is shown. Nothing a hook starts contacts a server, and no hook waits on the network.
